# Round 1 advocate for variant 1 (#409 observer vs sweep)

Role: ADVOCATE for V1. Read-only. Date: 2026-11-13. Sources re-checked by me today are marked "re-verified".
Cites: "V1 C-07" = variant-1 id; "X-nnn / A-nnn / SC-nn" = diff-analysis ids; file:line is the PR #539 checkout at head 873599d59 unless `origin/master` is named.

## 1. Position summary

V1's central premise ("no observer exists; it will read the sweep JSON") is wrong and I concede it: `superclaude observe` is merged (PRs #503 and #514) and registered, and it reads Orca directly, never the sweep. V1's findings about the sweep itself still stand and I re-verified them today: JSON v1 has nine row keys including `policy`, the JSON goes to stdout only, five of ten dimensions are never populated, PR #539 has six review threads with two unresolved (one a git-config security item), `git status` runs with the inherited environment on every host-global worktree path, and the #409 "boundary" sentence is not stated anywhere before the #409 body itself. The right course is therefore: keep V1's sweep-side fixes and its vocabulary mapping, adopt V5's "keep two programs, write one contract" as the working default, and treat "persist the sweep JSON" as a later, reversible option for v2 (#423) and Coder#477 rather than for the observer.

## 2. Steelman of each opposing variant

### 2.1 V2 (#450 watcher)

**Strongest version.** V2 is the only report that follows one merged mechanism end to end (watcher -> `orca agent launch` -> what the sweep sees) and finds a real defect in code, not in prose. The watcher's fixed prompt `/sc:issue --claim <N>` carries no `--pipeline`, and the claim's default is `worktree` (`src/superclaude/commands/issue.md` option table, re-verified: default `worktree`; "`worktree` and `full` create the worktree and launch an agent"; `launch.py:33` pins the prompt pattern with no pipeline). So each accepted claim creates worktree `issue-<N>` and starts a second governed agent, against the watcher design's own decision D2 "The claim stops at `next-steps.md`" (`docs/designs/issue-watcher.md:44`, re-verified). V2 adds that the claim's ship gate (OB-H1/H2/H3) forbids exactly that on a real issue, so the FU-1 live test as written would trip it. It also shows that a watcher session is Run-less and unowned, that the watcher keeps only `operation_id`, and that the sweep cannot tell such a session from a person's: `_person_origin` returns `True` with the note "origin not proved" when no tab-map entry exists, and rule `person_active` needs `person_started is True` (`classify.py:302-315, 748-767`, re-verified), so a later phase would print "person session (active)" for a machine-started session. V2 is also the only report that touches #409 decision 9 ("an ordinary session to the sweep"), which V1 never claim-checked.

**What it gets right that V1 missed.** W-01/W-02 (claim level), W-05 (mislabel risk), W-06 (the #409 diagram and Coder#477 describe a flow the built watcher does not have), W-12 (a "done" item still listed in #450). These are all independent of the observer question and survive V5.

**Critique with counter-evidence.** (a) Its subject is #450's rollout, not the observer/sweep alignment, so about half the findings (W-07 worktree ownership, W-09 ccsession naming, W-13..W-15) do not bear on the question the owner asked. (b) Its live-host facts (29 worktrees, 43 agent rows) cannot be re-checked (SC-12, UNVERIFIABLE). (c) W-10 "do not build an observer reader before FU-1" now addresses an observer that already exists and has no watcher input; the sound residue is "do not add a watcher panel to `superclaude observe` before FU-1". (d) W-06 and V3 F17 are the same finding counted twice, so their agreement is not independent corroboration.

### 2.2 V3 (Coder#477 listener)

**Strongest version.** V3 brings the only external evidence: five of eight candidate paths in Coder#477 omit `image/` (SC-4 confirmed), and #477 presumes a workspace listener that the built watcher deliberately does not have ("Option A makes IronbellyOrg/Coder#477 unnecessary", `docs/designs/issue-watcher.md` section 2.1, quoted by V3 F1). It then shows from Claude Code's own docs that a prompt sent as a peer message to a running session arrives as plain text, that a bypass-mode session holds the message, and that `crossSessionInbound` is all-or-nothing (F8). It adds the read-versus-send separation (F10), the no-expiry Orca pairing offer as a bearer credential (F11), the anonymous heartbeat endpoint (F14), and the lifecycle holes (stopped, Minimal-profile workspaces; F6). Its owner questions come with recommendations, and the 12 edits are paste-ready. F1 is right and important: the 2026-11-13 owner comment ("Ensure this aligns with Orchestrator and TUI Panel", re-verified, the only comment on Coder#477) does not say the option-A decision is reversed.

**What it gets right that V1 missed.** The whole Coder-side picture: identity envelope (F4), "what is the monitor" (F3), trust-criteria gaps (F12/F13). V1 never read Coder#477.

**Critique with counter-evidence.** (a) Edit 5 requires that a registered workspace return "the JSON object printed by `superclaude sweep run` ... produced without any write". That is false today: `commands.py:270-278` publishes a dated report with `os.link` and only then `click.echo`s the JSON, so every call writes a report (V1 F-03, C-18; X-004). The criterion is unachievable until the JSON is persisted or a no-write mode exists. (b) Edit 4/5 assume the sweep JSON can carry an identity envelope, but JSON v1 has nine row keys and no run id or worktree path per row (V1 F-01, SC-1). (c) Edits 5 and 6 treat "the TUI observer" as a consumer of a network status route; the built observer is single-host, runs the local Orca CLI with three argv shapes and has no network or credential path at all (V5 S3, SC-15). (d) Edit 8 copies `/sc:issue --claim <N>` into #477 without the second-agent problem V2 found (X-001). (e) V3 itself says only Edit 1 is needed if the owner keeps option A, so most of its 12 edits are contingent on one unanswered question.

### 2.3 V4 (#423 v2 and the #339 boundary)

**Strongest version.** V4 is the only report that stress-tests v2 against v1 and finds that "v2" has two meanings (#423 knowledge layer versus #339's comments, where "version 2" means the trust layer and the orchestrator of orchestrators; SC-8 confirmed), so #423's gate "after v1 (#339) is complete" is ambiguous (F1). It shows the sweep cannot be a catalogue as it stands (F2: `command`, `last_activity`, `changed_paths` exist in `models.py` but not in `to_json`; F3: one Run, one host; F4: no history, reports "never posted"), that v2's relay is the "cross-session permission laundering" path (F8), that ordinary and watcher-started sessions are never messaged so v2 checkpoints have no channel (F7, WC-16 `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:288`), and that LLM-derived catalogue entries must be kept apart from deterministic sweep facts (F14, U-008). F21 (no v2 leakage into PR #539) is a useful negative result. It also gives paste-ready edits for three issues.

**What it gets right that V1 missed.** F1 and F7 and F14 are genuinely new and independent of the observer; F9's "one grant vocabulary, not one design" is the better-supported reading of #339 comment 2.

**Critique with counter-evidence.** (a) C8 marks the sentence "this observer shows the human what happened, v2 shares knowledge between agents" TRUE "as in the #409 body", but in the live #409 body that sentence occurs once, inside decision 8 itself (re-verified at body line 119; the Out-of-Scope and Related text say only "distinct from this view"). The evidence is circular (X-002), and V4's #409 edit 2 appends to the unsupported sentence instead of relabelling it. (b) F17 says the sweep "confines every path", which is true of file reads (`probes.py:529`) but not of `git status` targets: `_worktree_paths` adds every path from `worktree ps` (`probes.py:1203-1214`) and `_git` runs with `{**os.environ}` (`probes.py:309`), re-verified (X-007). (c) Proposals (a) and (b) ("v2 and the observer share the sweep as one source", "no second scanner of Orca") are overtaken: the observer is already a second Orca reader with its own schema (V5 O-01, O-08), and its 15 s poller is the "second scheduler" F16 warns about (X-015). (d) F13 speaks of one JSON contract; there are two.

### 2.4 V5 (the built observer versus the sweep)

**Strongest version.** V5 found the one fact that dominates the question, and I confirmed it myself: `origin/master` registers `observe` at `src/superclaude/cli/main.py:572-574`, holds 14 entries under `src/superclaude/cli/observe/`, PRs #503 (merged 2026-11-11T19:54:57Z) and #514 (merged 2026-11-12T12:36:19Z) are MERGED, `QUESTION_GATE_PASSED = False` on master (`commands.py:67`), and a grep for "sweep" in the observer tree returns nothing while a grep for "superclaude observe / cli/observe / #503 / #514" over the sweep code, design, command, skill and WC returns nothing either. The observer allow-list (`adapter.py:29-52`) has three argv shapes and a forbidden-prefix set that still names `check` yet permits one `check --all --types question --run` template; its cadence is `INTERVAL = 15.0` (`collector.py:28`). V5 then does what no other report does: it re-tests V1 finding by finding (F-01..F-18) with explicit status words, concedes where V1 stands (F-02, F-05, F-07, F-08..F-11, F-13, F-17 remain), and turns the result into 23 exact body corrections, nine decisions each with a reversible first step, and a dependency table. Its recommendation (keep two programs, one written contract, join key `dispatchId` = `row_id` minus `orca:`) is cheap and reversible. It also identified a third vocabulary (observer `UNKNOWN` / `unavailable (no source)`) which strengthens V1's mapping-table point.

**What it gets right that V1 got wrong.** Everything in V1's "no observer" chain: C-02, C-03, C-21, C-26, F-01's premise, F-03's "observer would have to run the sweep", F-04, F-12, F-14, F-15, A-01, A-05..A-07 (see section 5).

**Critique with counter-evidence.** (a) Overreach: "All four earlier reviews ... were wrong about one fact" (verdict) is false for V2 and V3, which make no claim that the observer is unbuilt (X-019). (b) "Drop W-3 from the #409 body" (RT-F04): W-3 is V1's proposed wording, not body text; the live body has no such passage (X-020, re-checked by grep). (c) RT-F04 marks F-04 "CLOSED", but V5's own facts table says the observer has no git, contract or transcript data; closure holds for the Orca ledger only, and the "waiting on a person" scale question stays with the per-Run sweep (50 `worker-show` calls and 90 s, `probes.py:115-116`). (d) O-05 calls the sweep's "cannot be read without consuming" a contradiction, but V5's own counter-evidence is owner-only on probe Runs and "non-owner non-consumption is UNVERIFIED"; the sweep wording is unproven, not refuted (X-016). (e) Its decisive owner-decision facts (D5, D6, AC-S) rest on files only in a sibling worktree, not reproducible from master or GitHub (SC-18 partial; authorship of the pass record UNVERIFIED by V5). (f) D1 recommends "separate" without weighing the other two consumers (v2, #477 status route) that V4 and V3 want fed from the sweep's JSON; that persistence need does not vanish because the observer does not use it.

## 3. Strengths claimed for variant 1

Only claims that survive V5 and my own re-checks today are listed.

1. **PR #539 review state is wrong in #409 and V1 had it right (V1 C-07, F-07; SC-2).** Re-verified with GraphQL `reviewThreads`: exactly 6 threads, 4 resolved, 2 unresolved: Augment at `probes.py:1052` and Codex Security Review at `probes.py:1211` (both with a single comment, no reply). #409 says "five findings: four fixed, one not confirmed". No other variant counts the threads; V5 lists the count as "UNVERIFIED today".
2. **The sweep is not "read-only in its own code" without a caveat (V1 F-07, C-18; X-007; SC-9).** `_worktree_paths` adds every path from `worktree ps` (`probes.py:1203-1214`) and `_git` runs `git --no-optional-locks -C <path> ...` with `env = {**os.environ, ...}` (`probes.py:305-327`). V4 F17 asserts confinement (true for file reads only) and V5 marks F-07 "NOT AFFECTED" because the observer runs no git, so V1 is the only report that carries this as a safety item. It remains an open Codex security thread.
3. **`policy` IS a JSON row key; "ten dimensions are table columns only, not JSON keys" is wrong (V1 C-25; SC-1).** `report.py:650` emits `"policy": r.policy`; the golden test pins nine dimension keys (`DIMENSION_KEYS`, `tests/sweep/test_report.py:1243`) and nine row keys (`ROW_KEYS`, `:1255`). V5 records F-01's key list as "still a correct description of sweep JSON v1".
4. **JSON is stdout-only and each run writes a report (V1 F-03, C-18; X-004).** `commands.py:270-278`: `_publish(...)` then `click.echo(json.dumps(...))`. This refutes V3 Edit 5 ("without any write") and is the reason any future consumer (v2, #477) needs a persisted file or a no-write mode. V5 RT-F03 marks it "CHANGED" only because the observer has its own writer, not because the sweep fixed it.
5. **Vocabulary gap (V1 F-02, U-001; A-04).** Only lifecycle, health, result, freshness and policy ever get a value; phase, approval, admission, evidence packet, tests/review print `unknown`/`unavailable`; `unknown` must never render as `not admitted` or `not frozen`. V5 O-07 and RT-F02 confirm and widen it to a third (observer) vocabulary, so the single mapping table V1 asked for is now more needed, not less.
6. **The "boundary" sentence is unsupported (V1 C-34, F-16; X-002; SC-7).** In the live #409 body "Keep the boundary this issue already states: this observer shows the human what happened, v2 shares knowledge between agents" is at body line 119 inside decision 8 and nowhere else (re-verified); V4 C8 therefore proves it circularly.
7. **JSON strings are Markdown-escaped, not raw (V1 F-13).** `report.py:589-599` replaces `\`, `|`, `<`, `>`, `[`, `]` (re-verified); any future JSON consumer must unescape and re-sanitize. V5 RT-F13 says "REMAINS for the sweep JSON".
8. **`Messages coverage: partial` is fixed text (V1 F-18).** `report.py:199` prints it unconditionally; V5 O-05 sharpens it into a wording problem at `report.py:534`.
9. **Sweep-side scale and scope limits (V1 F-04, C-20; SC-10).** 50 `worker-show` calls and 90 s (`probes.py:115-116`), host-global `worktrees[]` (X-010 resolved in V1's favour over V4). This stays true for the per-Run depth the sweep owns even after V5 closes it for the Orca ledger.
10. **Doc drift (V1 F-17).** Design section 17 and 7.5 are stale; V5 O-02 and RT-F06 add two more instances. This is a cheap, certain fix.
11. **The "Missing for integration" list is the template for the contract V5 recommends (V1 section "Missing for integration", items 1-5, 7, 10).** Transport, atomic publish, "latest valid report" rule, schema-version policy, error and stale handling, sanitization contract, security stance: V5 D1's "contract content" is a subset of this list.
12. **Method honesty.** V1 declined to run `superclaude sweep run` (it writes a report) and labelled what it could not verify (UNVERIFIED list in its Verdict). Its claim-check of the #409 body also found the L18 and L24 overstatements, which V2 to V4 never examined.

## 4. Weaknesses identified in the other variants

1. **V3 Edit 5 is unachievable as written.** "Produced without any write" contradicts `commands.py:270-278` (publish, then print). Evidence: X-004, SC-9. Posting it into Coder#477 would create an acceptance criterion nobody can pass.
2. **V3 Edit 8 and V4 C7/F11 omit the second governed agent the watcher prompt starts.** V2 is right (X-001; `issue.md` default `worktree`, `launch.py:33`, `issue-watcher.md:44`, all re-verified). Both would write "one ordinary top-level session" into issue text.
3. **V4 C8 and F19 prove the boundary sentence with the sentence itself,** and V4's #409 edit 2 appends to it without relabelling (X-002). The sentence occurs once in the live body, inside decision 8 (re-verified).
4. **V4 F17 says the sweep confines every path.** True for file reads (`probes.py:529`); false for `git status` targets (`probes.py:1203-1214`, `:309`). Its proposed v2 read-permission AC therefore starts from a wrong baseline (X-007).
5. **V4 (a)/(b) and its proposed #423 text ("does not add a second scanner of Orca, git or transcripts"; observer and v2 "read the same sweep facts") are overtaken by the built observer,** which is a second Orca reader with its own schema and allow-list (V5 O-01, O-04, O-08; re-verified `adapter.py:29-52`). V4 also did not read the #409 design branch and did not notice the observer in its own checkout.
6. **V5 overreaches in places.** (i) "All four earlier reviews ... wrong about one fact": V2 and V3 never state that the observer is unbuilt (X-019). (ii) "Drop W-3 from the #409 body": W-3 was never in the body (X-020; grep of the live body returns nothing). (iii) F-04 "CLOSED" is only true for the Orca ledger; V5's own "facts each program owns" table gives git, contracts, transcripts and `safe_to_close` to the sweep. (iv) O-05 calls the sweep's non-consumption claim a contradiction while its own counter-evidence is owner-only and non-owner non-consumption is UNVERIFIED (X-016).
7. **V5's strongest owner-facing recommendations rest on evidence nobody else can open.** AC-S missing, pass-record scope (D5), staged direction of 2026-11-08 come from `owner-decisions-20261108.md`, `owner-decisions-20261112.md`, `owner-pass-record-20261112.md` in a sibling worktree (untracked or unpushed; SC-18). I confirmed on master only that `QUESTION_GATE_PASSED = False`; the owner records are not reproducible here.
8. **V5 D1 option (a) prices only the observer.** It does not weigh that V3 (Coder#477 status route) and V4 (#423 catalogue) both need the sweep's output in a form that can be read without re-running the sweep (X-004). "Keep separate" is right for the observer; it says nothing about those two consumers.
9. **V2 W-10 and W-06 partly restate each other across reports and are partly moot.** W-10 targets a non-existent "observer reader" and the diagram contradiction in W-06 is V3 F17 again (not independent corroboration).
10. **V3's 12 edits are mostly contingent.** V3's own text says only Edit 1 holds if the owner keeps option A; Edits 4-9 are written for a scenario Q1 may close. Posting them before Q1 is answered would be premature.
11. **V2, V3 and V4 never claim-checked the #409 body line by line** (only V1 and V5 did), yet V4 proposes four edits to its text and V3/V2 rely on a scratch copy of it (S-009). All of V1-V4 missed an observer that was in their own checkout (A-016); this is a shared method failure, not V1's alone.
12. **Five reports now edit the same #409 sentences (L18, L109, L116) in different directions** (S-006, X-002, A-011); none of V2-V5 proposes a single serialized edit pass.

## 5. Concessions (what is wrong or weak in variant 1)

1. **C-03 "No observer code exists ... Verified" is false.** I re-verified: `origin/master` registers `observe` (`main.py:572,574`), has the 14-entry `src/superclaude/cli/observe/` tree, and PRs #503 and #514 are MERGED. V1's check (`gh pr list --head docs/409-orca-dashboard-designs --state all` = 0) tested the wrong branch: the merged work came from other branches. The code was also in V1's own checkout history. This was a method failure (A-016), and it invalidates V1's headline "Both are read-only ... The observer does not exist" (C-02) and the L16 "Verified".
2. **F-01's framing "(1) Observer reads the sweep JSON", F-03's third option, Q1 "persist the JSON so one collector serves both" and the #409 decision-1 proposal are built on that false premise.** The observer has no file input except fixtures and reads Orca (V5 RT-F01, SC-15). The key-list part of F-01 survives; the "observer reads it" part does not. Q1 must be re-asked as V5 D1 (keep separate / optional panel / merge).
3. **F-04, F-12, F-14, F-15 are closed or moot on the observer side.** The observer reads all Runs, pages up to 5 x 100 rows, has a 15 s cycle with STALE after 45 s, 160/116/80 widths with `--ascii`, and an in-process "changes since last look" baseline. V1's "observer must merge several reports" and "observer must render from JSON" are wrong premises. (They stay true for any future panel that reads sweep reports, and F-04 stays true for the sweep's per-Run depth.)
4. **A-01 "Aligned (process)" is wrong.** The command is registered in the shipped CLI while no dated direction-selection record exists (PR #503 "Known gaps", per V5; I could not open the owner files). A-05..A-07 are met by the observer for Orca data, not gaps. C-21 ("reading the JSON keeps the observer free of an LLM polling loop") has a wrong premise: the observer's poll is already a plain thread.
5. **Verdict sentence "Both are read-only and share the same dimension names" contradicts V1's own C-02 and F-02 (X-008) and is false for the observer,** which shows none of the ten dimension names (V5 RT-F02). The tally line also miscounts (X-009).
6. **V1 never checked #409 decision 9 or the L71 diagram** (the watcher "hands a prompt to the project's orchestrator"), so it missed the V2/V3 contradiction (C-010/C-011), and it was silent on the watcher, Coder#477 trust and v2 altogether. Scope was narrower than the lead's question.
7. **Staleness.** V1 recorded the live body "identical" at 2026-11-13T16:30:18Z. The live body now has a correction banner at line 13 (my fetch: updatedAt 2026-11-13T16:57:51Z, banner dated 17:05 UTC), so V1's line cites (L16, L18, L24, L85, L109, L116) are against a superseded scratch copy (S-009, A-010).
8. **Unverified items that V1 stated plainly or left open:** "not confirmed" label on the Augment thread (the thread is unanswered, but whether it is a true finding is untested), whether PR #539 needs a rebase (PR state today: UNSTABLE; #541 is CLEAN and open), QA claims of the design package (C-37).
9. **Several V1 wording proposals now collide with, or are superseded by, V5's corrections:** W-1 (JSON gap list) needs the "which program reads it" caveat; W-3 must not be added (the observer covers fleet scale for the Orca ledger); W-5 and W-7 rewrite L18 and L116 and clash with V5 BC-03 and BC-17 (X-002); W-6 is the only one that is still correct as written (and is now confirmed by GraphQL).

## 6. Shared assumptions A-001 to A-016

- **A-001 (sweep JSON v1 is the right integration point for observer, v2 and #477): QUALIFY.** For the observer it is refuted (the observer reads Orca and never the sweep; SC-15, SC-16). For v2 and a #477 status route it is a candidate only after the JSON is persisted (`commands.py:270-278` prints and publishes in one run) and gains keys (`models.py` fields not emitted).
- **A-002 (one host, one Run is the unit; a fleet is a merge of per-Run reports): QUALIFY.** True for the sweep (`worker-list --run`, 50 shows, 90 s, `probes.py:115-116`); false as a fleet model, because the observer reads all Runs on the host (V5 RT-F04, `WORKER_LIST_TEMPLATE` `--limit 100`, re-verified).
- **A-003 (the observer is unbuilt and its direction will be chosen): REJECT.** Merged PRs #503 and #514 and `main.py:572-574` (re-verified). What stays open is the dated direction-selection record (PR #503 "Known gaps", per V5; owner files not reproducible here).
- **A-004 (PR #539 merges essentially unchanged; adding keys is cheap): QUALIFY.** Two review threads are unresolved today (`probes.py:1052`, `:1211`), so changes are likely, and adding row keys changes the golden pins (`ROW_KEYS`, `test_report.py:1255`), a deliberate test change that should not ride inside a PR under review.
- **A-005 (Phase 2 and #534 will absorb every deferred item): REJECT.** The only Phase 2 artifact is a reading list (`PHASE2-preflight-input.md`); the observer's R3/R4 have no package either (V5 S10). Treat each deferral as unowned until it has a line in #534 with a named owner.
- **A-006 (the sweep is read-only in effect and safe to run on demand for other consumers): REJECT as stated.** Each run writes a dated report (`commands.py:270-278`) and creates `.dev/sweeps/`; `git status` inherits the environment and runs in every host-global worktree path (`probes.py:305-327, 1203-1214`); the Codex security thread is unresolved. It is read-only toward Orca only.
- **A-007 (one owner, one trust domain, one OS user): ACCEPT.** True today and built in: the sweep reads one OS user's transcripts and the observer's questions panel works only in the terminal that holds the Run (V5 S8). It should be written down as a constraint, because v2 and #477 would break it.
- **A-008 (a coordinator is active and keeps running WC-16): QUALIFY.** Holds for the sweep: no coordinator wake, no report ("Nothing starts it automatically", `sweep.md`; WC-16 is a cap, `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:288`). Not for the observer's worker ledger, which polls on its own and needs no Run-bound coordinator.
- **A-009 (Orca CLI shapes and version are the same on every host): QUALIFY.** The sweep locks 1.4.245 in its design; the observer records no Orca version (V5 dependency table); both captures saw the same `worker-list` envelope. Treat cross-host shape equality as UNVERIFIED.
- **A-010 (the reviewed text is stable): REJECT.** The live #409 body gained a correction banner at line 13 after V1 verified it (my fetch: updatedAt 2026-11-13T16:57:51Z); PR threads and CI also move. Every line cite must carry a pinned ref.
- **A-011 (edit sets can be applied independently and in any order): REJECT.** Five reports rewrite L18, L109 and L116 differently (S-006, X-002); apply one serialized, owner-approved edit pass.
- **A-012 (the owner wants both programs kept and aligned): ACCEPT, as a working default pending owner confirmation.** The facts differ (observer: all-Run Orca ledger; sweep: contracts, git state, transcripts, `safe_to_close`; V5 "facts each program owns"), and merging breaks one design's rules; one owner question (V5 Q2) confirms it.
- **A-013 (polling Orca CLI read verbs is the only practical read source): QUALIFY.** It is the only source anyone has verified; event replay is explicitly UNVERIFIED (#409 L66) and `projection.attention` is read by neither program (V5 O-13). Not proven to be the best.
- **A-014 (there is one "JSON v1"): REJECT.** Two exist: `src/superclaude/cli/observe/snapshot.schema.json` (camelCase, `"UNKNOWN"`, `dispatchId`) and the sweep's report JSON (snake_case, `null`, `row_id`; `report.py:619-695`). Name them "observe snapshot v1" and "sweep report JSON v1".
- **A-015 (owner decisions are recorded where a reviewer can read them): REJECT.** Master has only `owner-decisions-20261111.md` under `.dev/issues/409/`; the 2026-11-08 and 2026-11-12 records are in a sibling worktree (SC-18, as reported by the diff analysis; I did not open the sibling).
- **A-016 (reading the issue text and the branch under review finds every relevant implementation): REJECT.** V1 is a worked example: the observer was in its checkout's history and tree, and V1 concluded it did not exist. The observer also has no command, skill or routing row (V5 O-15), which makes this failure likely to repeat.

## 8. Course of action for the owner

Nothing here is posted or done by me. "Reversible" means the first step can be undone without loss (issue-body edits keep history; a design-doc edit is a git revert; a code change in an open PR is a commit). Order of work: facts first (no dependency), then PR #539 hygiene, then owner decisions, then contracts and docs, code last.

### 8.1 #409 (issue body)

1. **One serialized correction pass on the body.** Merge V5's 23 corrections (headed by L16 "No observer code exists" and L54/L124 "Bubble Tea") with V1's still-valid items: C-07 (six threads, two open, one a git `status` security item), C-25 (`policy` is a JSON key; five of ten dimensions are never populated), C-34 (boundary sentence relabelled "proposed"), C-02/C-18 ("read-only toward Orca"; the sweep also writes a report and `.dev/sweeps/`). Dependency: none; owner posts (V5 says the wording is for the owner to confirm). Reversible: yes. Do not add V1 W-3 (X-020).
2. **Replace decision 1 ("read the sweep's JSON, not a second collector")** with V5's three options (keep separate with one contract / optional observer panel / merge) and proposal (a). Dependency: item 1 and the owner's answer to V5 Q2. Reversible: yes.
3. **Direction-selection record (AC-S).** Owner writes the dated record or amends acceptance criterion 1. Dependency: owner only; agents may cite it, not write it (V5 D6). Reversible: yes (a new file or a comment).

### 8.2 `superclaude observe` (merged, PRs #503, #514)

1. **No observer code change now.** Dependency: none. Reversible: n/a (nothing done).
2. **Questions-read scope (V5 D5).** Owner states in one sentence whether the 2026-11-12 pass covers only the named Run or any Run the terminal holds; then the 409 session opens a fresh branch from master with the one-constant flip. Dependency: owner sentence; the sibling's history predates the squash merge, so a fresh branch is required (V5; SC-14 confirms master is not an ancestor and the guard is False on master). Not reversible in effect once used on a live Run, so decide scope first.
3. **Discoverability row (V5 D8).** One routing row in `HOW-WE-WORK.md` in its own small PR. Dependency: none. Reversible: yes.
4. **Optional, later: a sweep panel.** Only if the owner picks V5 D1(b); the observer would need to unescape and re-sanitize Markdown-escaped JSON strings (V1 F-13). Dependency: sweep persists JSON; D1 decision. Reversible: yes.

### 8.3 sweep / #339 (PR #539, #534)

1. **Resolve the two open threads before merging #539:** the Codex security one (restrict `git status` to the selected repository and neutralise executable repo config, or record the risk as accepted in design section 13) and the Augment one (reply and fix or dispute). Dependency: none for the fix; #541 (CLEAN, open) fixes the one red test that is already red on master (V1 C-08; not re-run by me). Reversible: yes (a commit in the PR).
2. **Do not add JSON keys inside #539.** Record the additive key list (V1 W-1, corrected for the observer premise) in #534 and decide after merge. Dependency: #539 merged. Reversible: yes (the golden test change is deliberate and separate).
3. **Doc and wording fixes as #534 follow-ups, not edits to #539:** design 7.5 "copied from #409 ... unmerged" and section 17 items 1, 2, 5 and 22 are stale (V1 F-17, V5 O-02, RT-F06); `Messages coverage: partial` and `report.py:534` wording (X-016); say "no Orca write verb" instead of "read-only" in the design and the skill (V1 C-18, V5 O-11). Dependency: none. Reversible: yes.
4. **Persist `<stem>.json` beside the report: defer.** It is additive and low risk, but only v2 and Coder#477 need it, and neither is scheduled. Dependency: owner schedules a consumer. Reversible: yes.
5. **One relationship note (V5 D1(a)) naming "observe snapshot v1" and "sweep report JSON v1",** the join key, the vocabulary mapping table (V1 F-02 + V5 O-07), which program owns which fact, sanitization (consumer re-sanitizes), and who may call which Orca verb (V5 D4). Dependency: owner confirms V5 Q2; write it after #539 merges to avoid churn. Reversible: yes (a short doc).

3. **Strike the already-done item 4 and fix the test count (V2 W-12).** Dependency: none. Reversible: yes.

### Variant 2 Advocate

(Source file: round1-advocate-V2.md; full text below)

# Round 1 advocate for variant 2 (#450 issue watcher)

Role: advocate for V2, read-only. Date: 2026-11-14 (session date; the reviewed artifacts are dated 2026-11-13). Checkout: `/home/coder/workspaces/worktrees/IronClaude/issue-339-orchestrator-mode`, HEAD `873599d59`. Nothing was posted, edited or switched; this file is the only write.

Re-verified by me in this round (live source, not copied from a report): `issue.md:43,88`, `launch.py:33,84,95`, `claim.md:131`, `classify.py:302-315,748-767`, `cycle.py:1270-1335`, observer adapter templates on `origin/master` (`adapter.py:29-32`), `git diff origin/master HEAD` over the watcher code and design (empty), `gh` state of #450, Coder#477, PR #485, PR #539, the live #409 body (updatedAt 2026-11-13T16:57:51Z). Everything else is marked "relied on diff-analysis SC-n" or "not re-checked".

## 1. Position summary

V2 is the only report that tested what the built watcher actually starts, and it found a verified contradiction that V3 and V4 repeat wrongly: the watcher's fixed prompt `/sc:issue --claim <N>` runs at the claim's default `worktree` level, so it creates a worktree and launches a governed second agent, against the watcher design's "claim stops at next-steps.md" (X-001). V2 also shows that watcher-launched sessions have no Run, no recorded identity and a likely "person session" label, so neither the sweep nor (my extension) the built observer can see or label them. V2's real weakness is that it was written without knowing `superclaude observe` exists, so its W-10 wording is stale, and a few line cites have drifted; its findings W-01 to W-09 and W-11 to W-16 are untouched by V5.

## 2. Steelman of each opposing variant

### 2.1 V1 (#409 observer versus the sweep, written without knowing `observe` exists)
**Strongest version.** The sweep's JSON v1 is a stdout-only, per-Run, nine-row-key contract (`report.py:642-661`; SC-1). Any consumer (an observer, v2, a #477 status route) needs a stored, identity-bearing, versioned carrier, and today there is none: the JSON is printed after the commit point (`commands.py:277-278`) and only Markdown is stored. On top of that, PR #539 carries two unresolved review threads, one of them a real security item (`_worktree_paths` accepts every path from `worktree ps` and `git status` runs with `{**os.environ}`, `probes.py:1203-1214,309`), and #409 contains two wrong claims (review-thread count; "the ten dimensions are not JSON keys" while `policy` is one). V1 also gives the most careful claim check of any report (39 rows) and a concrete, minimal fix list (W-1..W-7).

**What it gets right (confirmed).** X-004 (a status route cannot be "no write" if it runs `sweep run`), X-007 (confinement is for file reads only), C-07 and C-25 wrong claims, X-002 (the boundary sentence first appears inside decision 8). SC-1, SC-2, SC-9 confirmed these.

**Counter-evidence.** (1) Its central premise, "no observer code exists" (C-03, repeated in C-02 and C-21), is false: PRs #503 and #514 are merged and `observe` is registered (X-012, SC-13); the code was in V1's own checkout (A-016). F-01, F-03, F-04, F-12, F-14, F-15 and the recommendation to "persist the JSON so one collector serves both" are built on that premise (S-011). (2) V1 never checked #409 decision 9, so it has no finding on what the sweep does with a watcher-launched session (C-010 is "silent"). (3) Its verdict sentence "Both are read-only and share the same dimension names" contradicts its own C-02 and F-02 (X-008).

### 2.2 V3 (Coder#477 workspace listener)
**Strongest version.** Coder#477 presumes a listener that the built watcher deliberately does not have (design D1 option A: "No listener, inbound port or address registry", "Option A makes Coder#477 unnecessary", `issue-watcher.md:28,43,47-49`). V3 adds three things no other report has: external evidence from the Claude Code docs (a peer message arrives as plain text, a slash command in it does not run, bypass sessions hold inbound messages; F8), the Coder-side lifecycle facts (the `minimal` profile has no Orca; a stopped workspace polls nothing; F6) and the credential facts (the Orca pairing offer is a no-expiry bearer credential; F11). Five of its eight "wrong path" claims are verified (SC-4), and Edits 1 to 12 are directly pasteable.

**What it gets right.** F1 and F7 agree with my W-06: nothing addresses "the project's orchestrator" and the flow in #477 differs from the built one. F2 (wrong paths) is a clean, checkable fix.

**Counter-evidence.** (1) Edit 8 tells #477 that an accepted request "starts a new top-level session ... using `orca agent launch ... --prompt \"/sc:issue --claim <N>\"`" and offers the watcher's live test as the proof. It never mentions that the default claim level creates worktree `issue-<N>` and launches a second governed agent (`issue.md:43,88`; X-001). The edit would write an unexamined default into #477 as an acceptance criterion. (2) Edit 5 requires "the JSON object printed by `superclaude sweep run` ... produced without any write"; each run publishes a dated report (`commands.py:186`; X-004), so the criterion cannot be met as worded. (3) Edit 4's identity envelope assumes a row can be joined to a workspace, but for a watcher-launched session the watcher stores only `operation_id` (`launch.py:42-45`; V2 W-03), so there is no session key to attach. (4) Edit 5 and 6 now point at a built observer that has no network or credential path at all (C-011 V5 effect).

### 2.3 V4 (#423 v2 and the #339 boundary)
**Strongest version.** The word "v2" means two things (#423 knowledge layer versus #339 comments' trust layer plus orchestrator of orchestrators), so #423's gate "after v1 is complete" is ambiguous (F1). V4 correctly sees that v2's relay is the "cross-session permission laundering" path (F8), that one grant vocabulary and one audit record can be shared while one trust design cannot (F9, F9 versus X-005), that v2's LLM output must not leak into sweep classes (F14, the best unique contribution, U-008), and that ordinary sessions are "never messaged" (F7, WC-16 `:288`). Its paste-ready edits for three issues are the most complete of any report, and its F21 ("no v1 leakage into v2") is a useful negative check.

**What it gets right.** C7 and F11 agree with my W-06 that #423's and Coder#477's descriptions of the watcher flow differ from what was built; F9 is supported by V2's own W-08 (the watcher's trust is an approvers file plus labels plus an integer-only prompt, a local model different from any grant).

**Counter-evidence.** (1) C7/F11 repeat "starts one ordinary top-level agent session" and V4's proposed #423 edit 7 would publish exactly that sentence; the default claim also launches a second agent (X-001, SC-3), so the sentence is incomplete. (2) F7's proposed acceptance criterion requires v2 to state, per class (Orca worker, watcher-launched, person-started), how a checkpoint is obtained. The sweep cannot separate the second class from the third: `_person_origin` returns `(True, ..., NOTE_ORIGIN_NOT_PROVED)` when there is no tab-map entry and no join failure (`classify.py:762-767`) and `_rule_person_active` then labels it a person session (`:302-315`) (X-003, V2 W-05). The criterion cannot be met from sweep facts. (3) F17's "every path is confined" holds for file reads only (X-007, SC-9). (4) Proposals (a) and (b), "one data source", are weakened by V5: the built observer reads Orca, not the sweep (X-013). (5) F16 names WC-16, a hypothetical v2 timer and the PM 30-minute checkpoint; it omits two cadences that already exist or are planned: the watcher's own 5-minute cron (#450 item 3, `0 */5 * * * *`) and the observer's 15 s poll.

### 2.4 V5 (the already built observer versus the sweep)
**Strongest version.** V5 is the only report that read the built program rather than the issue text. It shows that `superclaude observe` (PRs #503 and #514) is merged and registered (`main.py:572-574`), starts only three Orca argv shapes (`adapter.py:29-32`, re-checked by me: `worker-list`, `skills list`, one `check` template), never mentions the sweep and is never mentioned by it (SC-15, SC-16), and writes its own snapshot JSON, which makes "two JSON v1" and "two readers of `worker-list`" real. It re-tests V1 row by row, gives 23 exact body corrections, puts each decision (D1 to D9) with a reversible, non-code first step, and labels what it could not verify. X-012, X-013, X-015 and SC-13 to SC-17 confirm its load-bearing claims. It is the best report for deciding what to build next.

**What it gets right.** The premise that invalidates V1 (X-012), the correct reading of "read-only" (no Orca write verb, local files written by both; O-11) and the decision D5 (the questions-read switch-on has no PR; the owner record names one Run while orchestrator mode creates a new Run per coordinator).

**Counter-evidence.** (1) V5 is silent about the watcher. The built observer cannot see a watcher-launched session at all: its only reads are `orchestration worker-list`, `skills list` and one `check --all --types question --run <id>` (`adapter.py:29-32`), and `agent launch` is not an orchestration verb (V2 W-04; `orca agent launch --help` re-run: "Start an agent in an existing workspace", no Run key). V5's claim that the observer "needs no bound Run" (A-008 V5 effect) is true only for orchestration Workers, so Run-less sessions stay invisible to both programs. (2) V5 D9 guesses that "TUI Panel" in Coder#477 means `observe`; the owner's one-line comment ("Ensure this aligns with Orchestrator and TUI Panel") does not say so (SC-5), and the only #477 flow the observer could join is one that does not exist. (3) "All four earlier reviews ... were wrong about one fact" (X-019) overgeneralises: V3 states no such premise and V2 W-01..W-09, W-11..W-16 do not depend on it. (4) X-020: "Drop W-3 from the #409 body" is a mislabel (W-3 is V1's proposed wording). (5) Several decisive facts live only in a sibling worktree or an unpushed commit (X-018, SC-18, A-015) and V5's own test run is not reproducible (SC-20), so part of its owner-decision timeline cannot be re-checked from master or GitHub.

## 3. Strengths claimed for V2

1. **W-01 is a verified contradiction, found by V2 alone.** `issue.md:43` sets the `--pipeline` default to `worktree`; `issue.md:88` says that level "create[s] or reuse[s] the Orca worktree `issue-<N>` and launch[es] one agent in it"; `launch.py:33` allows only `/sc:issue --claim <int>`, with no `--pipeline`. The design says the claim "stops at `next-steps.md`" (`issue-watcher.md:44`, SC-3). I re-read the first three just now. V3 Edit 8 and V4 C7/F11 omit the second agent (X-001).
2. **W-02 (ship gate) is verified and unique.** `claim.md:131`: "no governed claim (`--pipeline worktree` or `full`) is used on a real issue until OB-H1 item 1 ..., OB-H2 and OB-H3 have passed." The FU-1 live test is a claim on a test issue at the default level, and neither #450 nor the runbook mentions the gate. A claim also performs GitHub writes (assign, two comments, ClickUp step), so the live test is not a reversible step.
3. **W-03/W-04/W-09: a watcher session cannot be joined to anything.** The watcher keeps only `operation_id` (`launch.py:42-45`); `status_report` returns typed scalars and no session id (`cycle.py:1326-1335`, re-read). `orca agent launch` is not an orchestration verb (help text re-run on this host), so no Run or Dispatch row is expected. V2 labelled the unproven part (does Orca write any Run record) UNVERIFIED and routed it to FU-1.
4. **W-05 resolves X-003, which the diff analysis could not.** `_person_origin` (`classify.py:748-767`) returns `(True, notes + NOTE_ORIGIN_NOT_PROVED)` when the row has no tab-map entry, no left-over join and no failed join; `_rule_person_active` (`:302-315`) then classifies a readable, in-window row with `person_started True` as `person_active`. Nobody typed the command, so the label is wrong in kind. I verified the code path; the "today versus later" state split rests on `orchestrator-mode.md:65,992` (not re-read) and no sweep was run.
5. **W-06 (diagram versus design) still holds on the live body.** The live #409 body (updatedAt 2026-11-13T16:57:51Z, later than V1's check) still draws "issue watcher ... --starts a claim session--> project's orchestrator (coordinator)" at L75 and says "A session the watcher launches is an ordinary session to the sweep" at L121; the added banner did not touch either. Design option A (`issue-watcher.md:28,43,49`) has no orchestrator in the flow. V3 F17 and V4 F11 independently agree.
6. **W-07 (worktree ownership) is unique (U-004).** All watcher sessions start in one configured worktree and the runbook never says how it is created or who owns its agent, while the repository rule is "every worktree gets an agent; its creator owns it" (`core/CLAUDE.md:55-61`).
7. **W-10/W-11 (status contract) are unique and re-verified (U-005, U-006).** `status_report` keys are `healthy, last_completed_cycle_age_seconds, disabled_since, next_permitted_at, counts, day_cap_used, stale, retry`; no `schema_version`, host, login or `enabled`; `retry[].argv` is a runnable launch command; `healthy` is `age is not None and age <= HEALTH_AGE_S`, so a never-run watcher, or one whose last cycle is older than `HEALTH_AGE_S` (as after a kill switch or `enabled: false`), reads unhealthy although the stop is planned.
8. **W-12 is current.** #450's body has not changed since 2026-11-08T23:56:26Z and still lists item 4 and "2655 tests"; PR #485 merged 2026-11-10T06:10:27Z (re-queried).
9. **V2's code is stable on master.** `git diff origin/master HEAD` over `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch` and `docs/designs/issue-watcher.md` is empty, so V2's watcher cites do not depend on PR #539 landing.
10. **Evidence method.** V2 alone ran live read-only host commands (`agent launch --help`, `worktree ps --help`, `orchestration --help`, `worktree ps --json`), and it marks the unproven parts (Run record, Claude command, name prompt) UNVERIFIED instead of assuming.
11. **Reversibility.** Every V2 proposal is issue or runbook text, plus one small change to `launch.PROMPT_PATTERN`/`build_claim_prompt` that the owner decides first (Q1). None changes the sweep or PR #539.
12. **Extension no other report reaches (mine, from V2 plus V5).** Because watcher sessions have no Run, the sweep and the built observer are both blind to them; #409 decision 9 ("Decide whether the observer also shows watcher state") has a concrete answer today: with the current adapter it cannot, and the watcher's own `status --json` or `dispatch.json` is the only observer-readable source, after FU-1.

## 4. Weaknesses identified in the other variants

1. **V1: a refuted premise carries six findings.** "No observer code exists" (C-03) is false (X-012, SC-13); F-01, F-03, F-04, F-12, F-14, F-15 and C-02, C-21, C-26 depend on it (S-011). Its headline recommendation (persist the sweep JSON so "one collector serves both") answers a question the repository had already answered differently.
2. **V1: internal inconsistency.** The verdict "Both are read-only and share the same dimension names" reverses its own C-02 and F-02 (X-008); the claim tally does not add up (X-009).
3. **V1, V3, V4 and V5 are silent on what the tools show for watcher-launched sessions.** C-010 is "silent" for V1, V3 and V4 as findings; V5 never mentions the watcher. Nobody else asks what the sweep or the observer shows for a session no Run owns.
4. **V3: Edit 8 omits the second governed agent** (X-001; `issue.md:43,88`; `launch.py:33`). Adopting it would put an unexamined default into #477 as an acceptance criterion.
5. **V3: Edit 5 is unachievable as worded** ("no write" while running `sweep run`; X-004; `commands.py:186,277-278`, SC-9).
6. **V3: scratch-file drift.** V3 calls `/tmp/pr339/i409-new-body.md` "the draft" although it was the live body (S-009); the live body has since moved (banner; now 30,758 bytes), so its `:70-81,113,114,118,195` cites are off.
7. **V4: C7/F11 and proposed edit 7 repeat "one ordinary session"** (X-001), and F7's per-class acceptance criterion cannot be met for the watcher-launched class from sweep facts (X-003; `classify.py:748-767`, `:302-315`).
8. **V4: F17 is incomplete** (X-007; `probes.py:1203-1214,309`), and proposals (a) and (b) now rest on a single-source premise V5 refutes (X-013, X-017).
9. **V4: F16 counts too few cadences** (omits the watcher cron and the observer's 15 s poll; X-011, X-015).
10. **V5: scope and overreach.** Silent on the watcher; D9 is a guess about what "TUI Panel" means (SC-5); X-019 overgeneralises (V3 and V2 W-01..W-09 are not wrong about the observer); X-020 is a mislabel; evidence for the owner timeline is in a sibling worktree or unpushed commits (X-018, SC-18, A-015) and its test run is unreproducible (SC-20).
11. **All reports cite a `/tmp` scratch file or a moving live body** (S-009, A-010); only V1 and V5 verified the live text, and V1's check is now stale.

## 5. Concessions (what is wrong or weak in V2)

1. **V2 did not know the observer exists.** Its W-10 ("Do not build an observer reader before FU-1") assumes an unbuilt observer. Corrected wording: "do not add a watcher panel or reader to `superclaude observe` before FU-1" (U-005 V5 effect). W-05 and W-16 say "the observer" generically and should name the built one.
2. **Cites have drifted.** V2 cites `/tmp/pr339/i409-new-body.md:70-75,113,117,195`. On the live body (30,758 bytes, banner added) the diagram is at L75 and item 9 at L121, so the +4 shift applies; the text is unchanged (S-009, A-010).
3. **Two key facts are unproven.** That `agent launch` creates no Run or Dispatch record (W-04) rests on help text and a live listing, not on an Orca source read; V2's "absent by construction" is stated too strongly. Whether the name prompt stalls and whether a tab-map entry appears (W-09) is UNVERIFIED by design and left to FU-1.
4. **W-05's "today versus later" labels are partly inferred.** I verified the code path (`classify.py:748-767`, `:302-315`) but not the `session_join` state (`orchestrator-mode.md:65,992`), and nobody ran a sweep against a real watcher session (X-003 was UNVERIFIED in the diff analysis; I could only close the code-structure half).
5. **Live host numbers are not reproducible** (29 worktrees, 43 `claude` agent rows; SC-12) and the host Orca version was not checked.
6. **Thin on other systems.** V2 has nothing on trust, scale, the JSON schema, v2 or Coder lifecycle, so it cannot adjudicate X-005, X-006, X-014, X-017 or most A-rows on its own evidence; my verdicts there are lower confidence.
7. **The W-01 fix is a code change proposed before the owner decides.** `--pipeline none` touches `PROMPT_PATTERN`, `build_claim_prompt`, the 12-element argv pin and the NFR-001 property test; the owner may legitimately want the chain (claim, worktree, governed agent), in which case W-02's OB-H gate becomes a precondition instead. W-01 is a contradiction with the design's D2, not proof of the owner's intent.
8. **Not read:** PR diffs of #447/#448/#485, hook scripts, `tests/sweep/`, #534, #423, the #409 design branch; W-08's "only claim-script-launched sessions are governed" comes from greps of `sc-issue-launch-level-hook.md:23`.

## 6. Shared assumptions A-001 to A-016

- **A-001: QUALIFY.** The sweep JSON is a fit for per-Run classification facts only; the built observer does not read it (SC-15), a #477 status route cannot run it without a write (X-004), and it cannot carry a Run-less watcher session at all (W-04).
- **A-002: QUALIFY.** One Run per coordinator is the sweep's unit, but a fleet is not "a merge of per-Run reports" because watcher-launched and person-started sessions belong to no Run, so no merge of Run reports can contain them (W-04).
- **A-003: REJECT.** The owner staged a direction on 2026-11-08 and said "Proceed with building the first release" on 2026-11-11; PRs #503 and #514 are merged and `observe` is registered (SC-13, SC-18), so "no code, direction unchosen" is false.
- **A-004: QUALIFY.** PR #539 is still OPEN at head `873599d59` (re-queried) with two unresolved threads (SC-2). All V2 items are independent of #539: W-05 needs no Phase 1 code change, W-01 changes `issue_watch`, not `sweep`.
- **A-005: QUALIFY.** #534 exists and Phase 2 is a reading list only; V2's own "waits for Phase 2 (or #534)" (W-05) is acceptable only if turned into a concrete #534 row that needs the FU-1 capture first.
- **A-006: REJECT.** `git status` runs with the inherited environment in every `worktree ps` path (`probes.py:1203-1214,309`) and each run writes a report (`commands.py:186`; SC-9). V2 assumed the sweep safe and did not test it; that is a gap in V2, not a point for the assumption.
- **A-007: ACCEPT.** For FU-1 and the shadow run the unit is one person's workspace, one `gh` login and one OS user (`issue-watcher.md:15`); multi-owner trust is a later problem V3 raises.
- **A-008: QUALIFY.** True for the sweep, which needs a bound Run and an active coordinator (WC-16); false for the sessions the watcher starts (no Run, no coordinator; W-04, W-06). The observer's worker ledger needs no bound Run, but still sees only orchestration Workers.
- **A-009: QUALIFY.** The watcher's Orca reply shapes are literally assumed: #450 item 1 requires "every `_assumed_shape` fixture is replaced with real output" and `tests/issue_watch/fixtures/_assumed_shape/orca_launch_*.json` exist; the host Orca version is unchecked (V2 scope).
- **A-010: REJECT.** The live #409 body changed after V1's check (updatedAt 2026-11-13T16:57:51Z; diagram now L75, item 9 L121); the watcher code and design did not (empty diff `origin/master..HEAD`).
- **A-011: REJECT.** The edit sets overlap (S-006, X-002). V2's five edits touch #450, the runbook and `launch.py` and are independent of the #409 rewrites, except W-05(a), which rewrites #409 item 9 (L121) and should be applied in the same pass as V5's BC list.
- **A-012: QUALIFY.** The evidence supports keeping both programs (different facts owned, different allow-list rules; V5 FO table), but only V5 states it and it is the owner's question (V5 Q2); treat it as a working default whose first steps are reversible under either answer.
- **A-013: QUALIFY.** For orchestration Workers the CLI read verbs are the source; for a watcher session they show nothing (observer adapter has three templates only, none of them `worktree ps`), so only `worktree ps` agent rows (V2 W-04, W-05 b) and transcripts carry it. An Orca event feed is unexamined by everyone.
- **A-014: REJECT.** There are at least three machine-readable contracts: the sweep JSON v1, the observer snapshot v1 (O-08) and the watcher's `status --json` (no `schema_version`; `cycle.py:1326-1335`) plus `dispatch.json` v1 (W-10).
- **A-015: REJECT.** V5 shows the observer decisions sit in a sibling worktree (SC-18). For the watcher, option A is in a tracked design (`issue-watcher.md:43`), but the 2026-11-13 Coder#477 comment ("Ensure this aligns with Orchestrator and TUI Panel") does not say whether it reverses option A, so the current owner position is not recorded anywhere (SC-5).
- **A-016: REJECT.** V5 (X-012) shows the observer was in V1's own checkout; I would add that V2 was equally exposed (it also did not find the observer), so every "silent" in V1 to V4 may mean "not found".

## 8. Course of action for the owner

Order that matters: step 0 (owner answers) gates everything marked "needs 0"; the watcher live test (FU-1) is the one step that is NOT reversible (real GitHub and ClickUp writes), so it comes last. R = reversible first step (issue text, runbook text, a code change that a revert undoes); NR = not reversible.

**Step 0. Owner decisions (one message, answers only; R).**
- Q1 (V2): claim level the watcher passes: `--pipeline none` (claim ends after the comments; recommended) or the default `worktree` (claim plus worktree `issue-<N>` plus a governed second agent, which needs OB-H1 item 1, OB-H2, OB-H3 first). Needs: nothing.
- Q2 (V2/V3/V4 Q9): which flow Coder#477 targets (the built watcher, option A, or a handoff to a project's orchestrator) and whether the 2026-11-13 comment reverses option A. Needs: nothing.
- V5 Q2: keep `observe` and the sweep separate with one written contract, or merge. Needs: nothing.

**#450 (watcher rollout) - V2's own items.**
1. Add to #450 "Done when 0": the Q1 decision. If `--pipeline none`: change `launch.PROMPT_PATTERN`, `build_claim_prompt`, the 12-element argv pin and the NFR-001 property test, in a small PR through `/sc:pr-submit --monitor 3`. Needs 0. R (code revert).
2. If Q1 keeps the default: add the OB-H1/H2/H3 precondition to #450 and to runbook 14.1 (`issue-watcher-setup.md:584-589`). Needs 0. R (text).
3. Strike item 4 (done by PR #485) and fix or drop "2655 tests". Needs nothing. R (text).
4. Extend FU-1: record the session identity fields (pane key, terminal handle, session id) from the real reply and from `worktree ps --json`; before/after `orchestration run-list`; the Orca Claude command (`ccsession-orca`?), whether the session stalls at the name prompt, tab-map entry, permission mode; keep unsanitised values in a private note and commit only the sanitised fixture. Share the capture with #534 step 1.0a. Needs nothing (text); the capture itself needs the live test. R (text).
5. Add runbook step 1.3a: create the watcher worktree with `orca worktree create --name issue-watch --agent claude --prompt "<task>"`, name its owner, note that each session writes `.dev/issues/<N>/` there (W-07). Needs nothing. R (text).
6. Run FU-1 live (real `gh`, real Orca) only after items 1/2, 4 and 5 and the OB-H gate if the default is kept. Needs 1-5. NR.
7. Do not add a watcher panel or reader to `observe` before FU-1 is recorded; when wanted, add `schema_version`, `enabled`, host and login to `status --json` (additive) or declare `dispatch.json` v1 the read contract; show `retry[].argv` as text only; show planned stops from `disabled_since` and `enabled`, not from `healthy` (W-10, W-11). Needs 6. R.

**#409 (TUI observer issue body).**
8. Apply one merged edit pass: V5's 23 corrections (BC-01..BC-23), plus the diagram fix (watcher -> an ordinary top-level session, no coordinator) and a rewrite of decision 9 ("a session the watcher launches has no Run and no coordinator; neither the sweep nor `observe` can tell it from a person's session or sees it; label it in a later phase"). Re-fetch the live body first (A-010). Needs 0 (Q2, V5 Q2). R (issue-body history).
9. Resolve the decision-8 rewrite collision (V1 W-7, V4 edit 2, V5 BC-17) in the same pass. Needs 8. R.

**`superclaude observe`.**
10. No code. Keep the three-template allow-list. Optionally add one routing row (V5 D8) in its own small PR. Needs nothing. R.
11. Merge or drop the unpushed questions-read switch-on per V5 D5 (owner's decision, not part of the watcher work). Needs the owner's sentence on Run scope. R (revert).

**Sweep and #339 (PR #539 and #534).**
12. Do not hold PR #539 for the watcher items. Resolve or record the two open threads (V1 F-07, SC-2) on their own. Needs nothing. R.
13. Log in #534: label machine-started top-level sessions as not person-started (W-05), the `worktree ps` agent-row fields (`prompt`, `paneKey`) as a candidate source, and V5's wording fixes for `report.py:199,534` and design 7.5/17 item 5. Needs 6 for the capture. R (text).
14. In #339, add the terminology line and the contract-owner decision (V4 #339 edits 2 and 4) after V5 Q2. R (text).

**Coder#477.**
15. Post the status comment (V3 Edit 1) and fix the five wrong paths (V3 Edit 2) after Q2. Do NOT post V3 Edit 8 as written; replace the launch sentence with "the launch uses the claim level chosen in IronClaude#450 (Q1); at the default level the claim also creates worktree `issue-<N>` and launches a second governed agent". Needs 0. R (comment edits).
