### Variant 3 Advocate

(Source file: round1-advocate-V3.md; full text below)

# Round 1 Advocate for Variant 3 (Coder#477 listener review)

Advocate: adv-r1-v3. Date: 2026-11-14. Read-only on GitHub and all checkouts.

## 1. Position summary

Variant 3 is the only review that tests the cross-workspace edge (Coder#477: listener, registration, trust, read versus send, lifecycle) against what is built and against Claude Code's own messaging rules. Its core findings (F1 premise contradiction, F2 wrong paths, F6 lifecycle, F8 messaging, F10 read/send split, F11 to F14 trust and audit, F17 diagram conflict) hold against sources I re-read today. Its real defects are three: Edit 5 assumes the sweep JSON can be the status payload with no write (X-004, A-006, A-014), Edit 8 restates the watcher's unexamined default launch (X-001), and it never noticed the built observer (A-016). Recommended: keep V3's findings, amend Edits 5, 6 and 8, post nothing on Coder#477 until the owner answers Q1 (and says what "TUI Panel" means).

Verification done for this report (all read-only): re-read `issue.md:43,88`, `launch.py:33`, `issue-watcher.md:28,43,47-49`, `seed-brief.md:46`, `classify.py:300-312,744-768`, `probes.py:309,1203-1214`, `sweep/commands.py:153-186`, `main.py:576-578` (observe registered), `git grep sweep` over `cli/observe` (no hit); the live #409 body (updatedAt 2026-11-13T16:57:51Z) and Coder#477 (still one comment, updatedAt 2026-11-13T16:20:07Z); the Coder checkout (`profiles.yaml`, `collector/app.py`, `runtime.mjs`, `Dockerfile.coder:110`, `README.md:49-51`, `llm-env.tf:37`); the Claude Code cross-session-messaging page fetched today; and the sibling `issue-409-mvp` TUIBBS notes (read-only).

## 2. Steelman of each opposing variant

### 2.1 Variant 1 (#409 observer vs sweep)

**Strongest version.** Anyone who wants a second consumer to read the sweep must first know what the sweep actually emits and does. V1 is the most rigorous claim-check of the five (39 rows): JSON v1 has nine row keys (`row_id, class, waiting_kind, rule, evidence, notes, policy, person_started, safe_to_close`), is printed to stdout only after a report is published (`commands.py:153-186`, echo after), keeps no history, is bounded to one Run (50 `worker-show` calls, 90 s), and PR #539 has two unresolved review threads, one a real git-config execution risk (`probes.py:309` builds `{**os.environ}`; `probes.py:1203-1214` accepts every path from `worktree ps`). V1 finds two wrong claims in the #409 body (C-07, C-25) and correctly refutes V3's own Edit 5 wording (X-004) and V4's confinement claim (X-007). Its "Missing for integration" item 9 (no host or coordinator key, so two hosts' reports cannot be told apart; "where Coder#477 registration fits") is the same gap as V3 F4, reached independently.

**What it gets right.** Contract-first thinking; "read-only in its own code only"; the observation of one JSON schema owner; and the warning that unknown must never render as "not admitted".

**Counter-evidence.** V1 was written without knowing `superclaude observe` exists: C-03 "No observer code exists ... Verified" is false (observe is registered at `main.py:576-578` in V1's own checkout), so F-01, F-03, F-04, F-12, F-14, F-15, C-02, C-21, A-01, A-05 to A-07 rest on a consumer that "reads the sweep JSON", which the built observer does not (`git grep -i sweep -- src/superclaude/cli/observe` returns nothing). Its verdict line ("Both are read-only and share the same dimension names") contradicts its own C-02 and F-02 (X-008). Its headline recommendation (persist `<stem>.json` "so one collector serves both") is opposite to what the code shows (X-017). And it stops at the #409 body: nothing on the Coder side, nothing on Claude Code message rules.

### 2.2 Variant 2 (#450 watcher)

**Strongest version.** The watcher is the one built component that starts sessions, so its behaviour is the ground truth for what #477 would have to route. V2 shows the fixed prompt `/sc:issue --claim <N>` carries no `--pipeline`, so the claim runs at its default `worktree` level, creates worktree `issue-<N>` and launches a second governed agent; that contradicts the design's D2 "claim stops at next-steps.md" and runs into the claim ship gate (OB-H1/H2/H3). I confirmed it: `issue.md:43` (default `worktree`), `issue.md:88`, `launch.py:33` (pattern allows only `--claim <int>`), `issue-watcher.md:44`. V2 also shows a watcher session is Run-less and unowned, so the sweep shows it as `could not determine`, or later as "person session (active)": `classify.py:744-768` returns `person_started=True` with note "origin not proved" when no join exists, and rule `person_active` fires at `classify.py:300-312`. V2 W-06 (diagram versus design) independently matches V3 F1. It is the only report with live host data and a concrete live-test gap list (W-03, W-04, W-09).

**What it gets right.** Mechanism-first narrative; W-01 is the single most consequential new defect among the five reports; W-10 and W-11 (planned stop shown as fault) are real false-alarm paths.

**Counter-evidence.** No claim-check of #450; it covers one issue only and never reaches the Coder side beyond W-06; its W-10 ("do not build an observer reader before FU-1") now reads as "do not add a watcher panel to the existing observer" (U-005 V5 effect); its live Orca counts (29 worktrees, 43 agent rows) are not reproducible. None of this reduces W-01; it adds a defect V3 inherited (see Concession 3).

### 2.3 Variant 4 (#423 v2 and #339 boundary)

**Strongest version.** v2 is where trust and relay become hardest, and V4 audits it against the real v1 contract. F1 (the word "v2" means the knowledge layer in #423 but "trust layer plus orchestrator of orchestrators" in #339's comments; the gate "after v1 is complete" is ambiguous) is verified in the #339 comments. F7 is a genuine contradiction: v2's checkpoint and relay model assumes a message channel that exists only for Orca workers, while WC-16 says person-started sessions "are never messaged", WC-12 is pull, WC-13 forbids workers messaging peers. F8 (relay of one agent's summary into another is the "permission laundering" path; relayed knowledge must be data, never instruction) and F9 (promise one grant vocabulary and one audit record, not one design, because the threat models differ and "signing does not stop poisoned content") are the best trust-design analysis in the set. F14 (LLM-derived content must not alter deterministic sweep classes) and F17 (read-permission model for other workspaces) are real. V4 gives paste-ready edits for three issues, and its C10 (#423's description of #339 is contradicted by #339 itself) is solid.

**What it gets right.** The boundary logic; F11 notices the same flow conflict as V3 F1 (#423 and #477 describe different flows) and routes it to the owner (Q9).

**Counter-evidence.** C8 is circular (X-002): the sentence it calls "TRUE" exists only in #409's own decision 8. F13 and proposals (a) and (b) assume the observer and v2 share the sweep as one source, which V5 refutes (X-013, X-017). F16 warns of a second scheduler that already exists (observer 15 s versus sweep at most one run per 5 minutes; X-015). F17's "confines every path" is incomplete for git-status targets (X-007; `probes.py:1203-1214`). Several edits (#423 edit 3: "depends on Coder#477") proceed as though #477 will exist, without conditioning on V3's F1 finding that the design says #477 is unnecessary under option A. Verifiability is lowest of the five: v2 is not designed.

### 2.4 Variant 5 (built observer vs sweep)

**Strongest version.** V5 found the one fact that changes the question: `superclaude observe` is built and merged (PR #503 on 2026-11-11, PR #514 on 2026-11-12), registered in the shipped CLI, 28 test files, reads Orca only through three argv shapes and never writes to Orca, never mentions the sweep and is never mentioned by it. I confirmed the registration (`main.py:576-578` in the review checkout), the absence of any "sweep" reference in `cli/observe`, and the live #409 correction banner (body updated 2026-11-13T16:57:51Z, line 13). From that V5 derives the second "v1" JSON schema (O-08), two readers of `worker-list` (O-01), two allow-lists that disagree on `check` (O-04), a sweep sentence ("pending questions cannot be read without consuming them") that the observer's gate research weakens (O-05), and D1 to D9 each with a reversible first step. Its re-test of V1 (F-01..F-18, with REMAINS / CHANGED / CLOSED / WRONG PREMISE) is the only audit of another report in the set, and the "keep both, write one contract" recommendation is the lowest-regret answer to the observer/sweep question.

**What it gets right.** X-012, X-013, X-015, X-018 and the 23 body corrections; D9 correctly notes `observe` is single-host, never sends, and that remote fleet is R4 behind "cross-host ownership joins ... Not established".

**Counter-evidence.** V5 does not touch the Coder side: nothing on #477 beyond D9's guess that "TUI Panel" means the observer. D9 omits a third candidate that the owner's own files name: the owner is "exploring TUIBBS as an internal communication tool and platform for all human staff who manage agent teams; the observer may become a TUIBBS module" (sibling `owner-decisions-20261108.md` item 4), and the sibling's TUIBBS review says TUIBBS runs on a separate SSH host while Orca and the observer run in Coder, so "Orca data still has to reach that host by some other channel" (`tuibbs-review/notes-3.md:78-82`). That channel is exactly the read-only status route V3's Edits 5 and 6 define. Several V5 facts exist only in an untracked sibling worktree (S-013, A-015), so they are not reproducible from master or GitHub. X-019 (V5 overgeneralises that "all four" missed the observer; V3 made no claim either way) and X-020 (an instruction to "drop W-3 from the #409 body" that is not in the body) are small errors. D5 (guard switch-on) is not an alignment item.

## 3. Strengths claimed for variant 3

1. **F1 (premise contradiction) is correct, decisive, and sits in tracked files.** #477 assumes a watcher that sends to a listener; the built watcher has none. Evidence: `docs/designs/issue-watcher.md:28` (out of scope "Any listener, port, webhook ..."), `:43` (D1 option A "No listener, inbound port or address registry"), `:47-49` (#477 "unnecessary unless B or C is revisited"), `seed-brief.md:46` (owner decision 2026-11-07); both files are tracked (`git ls-files --error-unmatch` succeeds). #477 is unchanged (one comment, updatedAt 2026-11-13T16:20:07Z, re-read today). Triple-corroborated: V2 W-06, V4 F11, diff analysis C-011. Q1 and Edit 1 are therefore the right gate for everything else on #477.
2. **F2 (five of eight paths omit `image/`) is confirmed** (SC-4). I additionally confirmed `image/catalogue/profiles.yaml` exists with the slugs V3 cites. Low severity, but it is a defect a reader following #477 literally would hit; no other variant checks the Coder repo.
3. **F8 (a listener-routed prompt versus Claude Code's rules) is correct against the current docs, and two of V3's UNVERIFIED items are now resolved in its favour.** Docs fetched today: "Commands don't run: ... arrives as plain text"; a bypassing receiver holds each message unless the sender also bypasses; `dialogExpiry` default five minutes; "A session inside a container and a session on the host can't reach each other"; other-machine delivery needs a claude.ai sign-in and "Claude can't find those sessions with an API key". Coder side: the image pins `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code@2.1.289` (`image/Dockerfile.coder:110`, above the 2.1.224 floor) and every `claude` launch uses `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` from `LITELLM_API_KEY` against a LiteLLM `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` (`README.md:49-51`, `llm-env.tf:37`), i.e. gateway auth, not a claude.ai sign-in. So Claude Code cannot be the cross-workspace channel, which is V3 Q4's recommendation (UNVERIFIED in V3; answered here). ccsession's `--dangerously-skip-permissions` default (`ccsession:135-140`) puts orchestrators in the bypass class. No other variant raises any of this (C-015).
4. **F10 and Edit 6 (reading status and sending a prompt are separate permissions) give #477's half of a rule #409 already states.** Live #409 item 5 (re-fetched): "the observer must stay read-only and must never send through it". V5 confirms the built observer starts only three Orca argv shapes and forbids `send`, `ask`, `reply`, `inbox` by prefix (`adapter.py`, SC-15). V3 is the only report that turns the rule into a testable #477 criterion.
5. **F11 and F14 (credential hygiene) are verified and unique.** `collector/app.py:5` and `README.md:10` say the heartbeat endpoint is anonymous; `app.py:274` and `Dockerfile:11` bind `0.0.0.0:8080`; reusing it for registration would fail #477 AC4. The Orca server is loopback-only (`runtime.mjs:285`) behind an owner-shared Coder app, and the #195 PRD calls the Orca offer a no-expiry bearer credential that must never sit in agent metadata. #477 does not forbid registering such a credential; Edit 4 does.
6. **F6 (lifecycle and profile coverage) is real and unique.** `profiles.yaml:18-21`: `orca` profile has the Orca and IronClaude slugs, `minimal` has only `claude-code`; Orca starts from its own `coder_script`, not `startup.sh`. Any multi-workspace read path (the observer's R4, TUIBBS) must represent "no orchestrator", "stopped" and "stale", which #477 does not.
7. **F12 and F13 (trust criteria and audit) are testable and align with V4 once wording is fixed.** Expiry, nonce, scope and an audit line joinable to the watcher's `audit.jsonl` by issue number and workspace id. The join key also gives a source for labelling watcher-launched sessions, which V2 W-05 and V4 F7 say the sweep cannot do today (X-003): an audit record keyed by issue number and workspace id is the missing "who started this session" fact.
8. **F17 survives the live #409 correction.** The "How the pieces fit" diagram (watcher "--starts a claim session--> project's orchestrator", "workspace listener ... routes an approved prompt") is still in the live body after the 16:57Z banner, and the banner covers only the observer. V1 missed this; V2 and V4 saw it; V3 states it as an IronClaude-internal contradiction with a fix (Edit 1 plus a diagram correction).
9. **Method.** The only variant that reads the Coder repo line by line and the Claude Code docs; marks F3, F5 and F9 UNVERIFIED instead of guessing; makes Edits 1, 2, 10, 11 and 12 independent of the owner's answer to Q1; posts nothing; gives a recommendation with every owner question (Q1: keep option A, rescope #477 to read-only registration and status, keep prompt routing disabled; this is the lowest-regret path and matches V2 Q2 and V4 Q9).
10. **The need V3 designs for is real and now has a named consumer.** The observer's snapshot is a designed interface for a later reader ("a read-only Go screen in TUIBBS", V5 S9, U-012); TUIBBS runs on a separate SSH host while Orca runs in Coder, so Orca data "still has to reach that host by some other channel" (sibling `tuibbs-review/notes-3.md:78-82`); observer R4 (remote fleet) is gated on "cross-host ownership joins ... Not established" (V5 S10). A read-only, identity-carrying, separately credentialed status route is the missing piece; V3 is the only report that specifies it.

## 4. Weaknesses identified in the other variants

1. **V1 rests on a false premise and contradicts itself.** C-03 "No observer code exists ... Verified" is false (`main.py:576-578`; `git grep sweep -- cli/observe` empty). F-01, F-03, F-04, F-12, F-14, F-15, C-02, C-21, A-01, A-05 to A-07 depend on an observer that "reads the sweep JSON" (X-012, X-013, S-011). Its verdict sentence "Both are read-only and share the same dimension names" reverses its own C-02 and F-02 (X-008); its claim tallies do not add up (X-009). Its recommendation to persist JSON "so one collector serves both" is the opposite of the code on master (X-017).
2. **V1 and V4 never reach the Coder boundary.** V1 mentions #477 in one line and one missing-item; V4 treats #477 as "the only planned cross-workspace channel" and builds #423 edit 3 on it without noting the design says #477 is unnecessary under option A (`issue-watcher.md:47-49`). Neither raises the Claude Code message rules, the anonymous heartbeat endpoint or the `minimal` profile.
3. **V4's confinement claim is incomplete and its source-sharing premise is refuted.** F17 says the sweep "confines every path"; `probes.py:1203-1214` adds `Path(text).resolve()` for every `worktree ps` item with no root check and `probes.py:309` runs git with `{**os.environ}` (X-007). Proposals (a) and (b) (v2 and observer share the sweep as one source) are refuted by V5 (X-013). F16's "second scheduler" risk already exists (X-015). C8 is circular (X-002).
4. **V4 (c) and #409 edit 1 do not condition on Q1.** "One grant vocabulary" has a consumer only if a listener exists. Under option A the watcher's trust model is the approvers file plus labels plus an integer-only prompt (`launch.py:33`, design D3), which is local and different (V3 F12). V4 does ask Q9 but still writes edits that assume the answer.
5. **V2 is narrow and partly superseded.** No claim-check; Edit 5 and W-10 target an observer reader that V5 shows does not exist in that form (U-005 V5 effect). Strong on the watcher, silent on #477's lifecycle, trust and read/send questions beyond W-06. (Its mechanism claims are sound: I confirmed W-01 and W-05 in code.)
6. **V5 does not cover the cross-workspace edge and leans on non-reproducible sources.** D9's "TUI Panel" analysis omits TUIBBS (sibling `owner-decisions-20261108.md` item 4; `tuibbs-review/notes-3.md:78-82`). Owner records live only in an untracked sibling worktree (S-013, A-015, SC-18), the 693/1 test run and the 428-worker sample cannot be reproduced (SC-20), X-019 overgeneralises, X-020 mislabels where W-3 lives. D5 (switch-on scope) is outside the alignment question. Its D1 relationship note, as scoped, names two programs and two schemas but not the third party that will have to carry either across a workspace boundary.
7. **The five reports collide on edits.** S-006 and X-002: V1 W-7, V4 #409 edit 2 and V5 BC-17 rewrite one #409 sentence three ways. V3's edits touch only #477 (plus references), so they do not collide with any other variant's edit set; that is a structural advantage when the owner applies them.

## 5. Concessions (weaknesses of variant 3)

1. **Edit 5 is wrong as written (X-004, A-006, A-014).** It says a registered workspace returns "the JSON object printed by `superclaude sweep run` ... produced without any write and runs only the sweep's read-only commands". `sweep run` publishes a dated report with `os.link` (`commands.py:186`) and prints JSON afterwards; it also runs `git status` with the inherited environment in every worktree path from `worktree ps` (`probes.py:309,1203-1214`), the open Codex security thread on PR #539. A route that ran it per request would write files and execute repo-config-controlled code. "schema version 1" also names one of two different v1 schemas (O-08).
2. **I (V3) missed the built observer (A-016, X-012, X-013).** The code was in V3's own checkout (`main.py:576-578`; commits `9e2608b03`, `cd8736199` in history). V3 wrote "the TUI observer" and "if the observer is to see several workspaces" generically and only checked that the #409 body contained the string "Coder#477". Edits 5 and 6 therefore target a consumer that has no network or credential path and reads no file input; they stand only as requirements for a future reader (R4, TUIBBS). V5 X-019's overgeneralisation ("all four") does not apply to V3's text, but V3 made the same omission.
3. **Edit 8 restates an unexamined default (X-001).** It prescribes `orca agent launch ... --prompt "/sc:issue --claim <N>"` and says it "starts a new top-level session". With no `--pipeline` the claim defaults to `worktree` (`issue.md:43,88`), creating worktree `issue-<N>` and launching a second governed agent; the claim ship gate (`claim.md:131`) forbids that on real issues. Its live-test criterion records only the first session's permission mode (V2 W-08).
4. **Cites to the #409 draft have drifted (A-010, S-009).** V3 calls `/tmp/pr339/i409-new-body.md` a "draft" and cites `:70-81,113,114,118,195`; the live body (30,758 bytes against 30,030, banner at line 13) has moved those lines. The substance still holds in the live body (diagram, item 5, item 6, item 8), which I re-verified, but the cites should be re-pinned to the live issue.
5. **Over-reach and weighting.** Twelve paste-ready edits to another repository's issue answer a one-line owner comment ("Ensure this aligns with Orchestrator and TUI Panel"). Five path errors (F2) are Low severity yet headline the claim check; the verdict "Not aligned yet" blends cosmetic and design findings; Q-count overlaps V2 Q2 and V4 Q9 (S-007).
6. **"TUI Panel" and "monitor" are left as unknowns.** F3 and Q2 record that the sources do not define "monitor" and never ask what "TUI Panel" means; V3 did not look for the owner's other written sources (TUIBBS notes), which I found in the sibling worktree.
7. **F9 and Q4 were answerable.** The Coder repo shows the gateway auth and the pinned Claude Code version (see Strength 3); V3 left both UNVERIFIED.
8. **F11 rests on the #195 PRD text, not Orca source** (V3 says so). What a paired device can do remains UNVERIFIED.

## 6. Shared assumptions (A-001 to A-016)

- **A-001: QUALIFY.** REJECT for the observer (it reads Orca only: `git grep -i sweep -- cli/observe` is empty) and for #477 as a fixed payload (V3's own Edit 5 inherits this error); the sweep JSON stays a valid candidate for v2 and for an optional observer panel only after it gains persistence and identity keys. A #477 route should carry a named, versioned record, not a hard-wired schema.
- **A-002: QUALIFY.** The per-Run unit holds for the sweep's disk evidence, but the observer already reads all Runs on the host (V5 RT-F04), and a fleet across workspaces is not "a merge of per-Run reports": no JSON carries host, workspace or owner (`report.py:619-695`) and every workspace has the same `HOME=/home/coder` (`startup.tf:57`), so paths are not unique (V3 F4).
- **A-003: REJECT.** The owner chose a staged direction (2026-11-08), said "Proceed with building the first release" (2026-11-11), and releases 1 and 2 are merged (PRs #503, #514). What remains unstated is the dated AC-S record and whether the reviewer's four asks are wanted.
- **A-004: QUALIFY.** PR #539 can merge without identity or persistence keys if those are logged as #534 follow-ups; but two unresolved threads (one security: `probes.py:1203-1214`) make "merges unchanged" unsafe, and any golden-key change must be deliberate. Do not widen #539 for #477.
- **A-005: REJECT.** Phase 2, #534 and "a later phase" are named by every report as the sink for identity keys, machine-started-session labels, GitHub reads, remote hosts, yet only a reading list exists (`PHASE2-preflight-input.md`); V3's own "Phase 1 follow-up or a Phase 2 item" shares this fault. Each deferred item needs a named tracking line.
- **A-006: REJECT.** The sweep writes a dated report (`commands.py:153-186`) and runs `git status` with inherited env on every `worktree ps` path (`probes.py:309,1203-1214`); it is not safe to run on demand for a remote caller. V3's Edit 5 relied on this assumption.
- **A-007: QUALIFY.** True today (one owner, one terminal, one host built in), but #477 is by definition multi-workspace and ties registration to "the appropriate GitHub account"; Coder's heartbeat already carries `workspace_id` and `owner` (`emit-heartbeat:7-9`). #477 must state whether it serves one owner's workspaces or several people's.
- **A-008: QUALIFY.** The sweep needs a bound Run; the observer's worker ledger does not; a watcher-launched session has no Run and no coordinator (V2 W-04). For #477 this is why "its project's orchestrator" has no address (V3 F7).
- **A-009: QUALIFY.** Holds inside one workspace image (Orca and Claude Code are pinned in `Dockerfile.coder`; the upstream monitor opens pin PRs, `orca-upstream-monitor.yml:1-9`); fails across a fleet during an image rollout (image hash at `main.tf:104-107`), so any cross-workspace reader must carry an Orca/version field.
- **A-010: REJECT.** The live #409 body already changed (updatedAt 2026-11-13T16:57:51Z, banner at line 13, 30,758 bytes versus the scratch 30,030). Three reports, V3 included, cite `/tmp/pr339/i409-new-body.md` by line. The substance V3 cites survives (I re-fetched the diagram and items 5, 6, 8).
- **A-011: REJECT.** V3 Edit 8 depends on V2's launch-level decision; V3 Edit 5 depends on the V1/V5 findings; three reports rewrite one #409 sentence (X-002). V3's Edits 1, 2, 10, 11, 12 are independent of Q1, but the set as a whole is not.
- **A-012: QUALIFY.** Unasked by V1 to V4. Keep both is the lowest-regret working assumption (both merged or in review; V5 D1 option a is reversible), but the owner should confirm (V5 Q2). V3's Edit 6 (read and send are separate) holds whichever way it goes.
- **A-013: QUALIFY.** Polling Orca's CLI is the only source either program uses, and the watcher itself chose pull over push (option A, D1). But push exists elsewhere: Claude Code lists "Channels: push external events ... into a session" among its related resources (not evaluated here, UNVERIFIED for fit), and Orca sends `projection.attention` that neither program reads (V5 O-13). Cross-workspace delivery through Claude Code messaging is excluded in this image (gateway auth, container isolation).
- **A-014: REJECT.** Two "v1" schemas exist (observe snapshot: camelCase, `"UNKNOWN"`, `dispatchId`; sweep: snake_case, `null`, `row_id`; V5 O-08). V3's Edit 5 ("schema version 1") must name which.
- **A-015: REJECT.** Owner decisions are scattered: option A is in tracked files on master (`seed-brief.md:46`, `issue-watcher.md:43,47-49`), but the observer's staged direction and questions-read pass are only in an untracked sibling worktree (SC-18). Reviewers need decisions recorded in a tracked place.
- **A-016: REJECT.** Reading the issue text and one branch did not find the observer, which was in V1 to V4's own working tree. V3 is affected (Concession 2). Each report's "silent" may mean "not found".

## 8. Course of action

Order: items marked "first" have no dependency. Nothing here is posted by this report. "Reversible" means an issue comment or edit, a doc line, or a constant that can be undone without data loss.

**Coder#477 (workspace listener)**
1. *First.* Owner answers Q1 (is #477 still wanted for the watcher; if not, is it rescoped to read-only registration and status?) and says what "TUI Panel" means: `superclaude observe`, the #409 design, or a TUIBBS module. Reversible: yes (a comment).
2. Apply Edits 2 (paths), 10 (Out of Scope), 11 (Related) and 12 (Depends on). They hold whatever Q1 answers. Dependency: none beyond the owner approving an edit to the issue. Reversible: yes (issue edit history).
3. Apply Edit 1 (status paragraph, option A) once Q1 is answered. Dependency: Q1. Reversible: yes.
4. If #477 stays open for status: apply Edits 4, 7 and 9, and an amended Edit 6 and Edit 5. Amended Edit 5: the route returns a named, versioned read-only record (for example `observe snapshot v1` or `sweep report JSON v1`) wrapped in an identity envelope (workspace id, owner, `gh` login, host, version, last-seen); it never runs `sweep run` per request; delete "without any write". Add to Edit 6: reading never consumes a message (X-016). Dependency: Q1, and the owner's D1 answer on the relationship note. Reversible: yes.
5. Amend Edit 8: replace the restated launch with "the same fixed prompt and launch the issue watcher uses (IronClaude #450 decides the claim level)", and extend the live-test criterion to record the permission mode of every session the claim starts. Dependency: #450 item 1 below. Reversible: yes.
6. Record the Q4 answer in #477: workspace sessions use LiteLLM gateway auth (`README.md:49-51`) with `@anthropic-ai/claude-code@2.1.289` (`Dockerfile.coder:110`), so Claude Code cross-machine messaging is unavailable; keep Edit 8's "nothing depends on Claude Code cross-session messaging" line. Dependency: none. Reversible: yes.
7. Q5 (minimal profile registers as "no orchestrator") and Q6 (never send to a running session; start new sessions only): owner answers; recommendations as in V3. Dependency: Q1.

**#450 (watcher rollout)**
1. *First.* Owner decides the claim level: `--pipeline none` (matches design D2) or the default `worktree` plus the OB-H1/H2/H3 gate (V2 Q1). Recommended `--pipeline none`: change `build_claim_prompt`, `PROMPT_PATTERN` (`launch.py:33`) and the argv pins. Dependency: none. Reversible: yes (one constant plus pin tests).
2. Extend FU-1 with V2 W-03, W-04, W-08, W-09 (capture session identity fields, before/after `run-list`, Orca Claude command, tab-map entry, permission mode). Dependency: item 1. Reversible: yes (runbook text).

**#409 (TUI observer issue) and `superclaude observe`**
1. Finish the body corrections V5 lists (the banner at line 13 is done) and apply two V3 corrections: the "How the pieces fit" diagram must show watcher to an ordinary session with the listener labelled option B/C, not built; item 5 gains "the observer today reads the local Orca CLI only; a remote read goes through a read-only credential on #477, never the send route". Item 6 takes V4's wording ("one grant vocabulary, one audit record"). Dependency: owner confirms Q1 on #477. Reversible: yes (issue edit).
2. No observer code change. Record in the R4 gate row that remote fleet depends on a #477-style read route, identity envelope and `cross-host ownership joins`. Dependency: Q1. Reversible: yes (one doc line).
3. Owner writes the AC-S record (V5 D6) and commits the owner decision records to a tracked place (A-015). Dependency: none. Reversible: yes.

**Sweep, #339 and PR #539**
1. Do not widen PR #539. Log in #534: (a) an identity block for JSON (host, workspace id, owner, `gh` login); (b) persisted JSON or a no-report mode (V1 F-03; X-004); (c) the two sweep sentences about message consumption and the stale design lines (V5 O-02, O-05, RT-F06); (d) the Codex security thread (`probes.py:1203-1214`), which blocks any remote or on-demand use. Dependency: none. Reversible: yes.

Dependency: owner answers V5 Q2. Reversible: yes (a doc).

### Variant 4 Advocate

(Source file: round1-advocate-V4.md; full text below)

# Round 1 advocate for variant 4 (#423 v2 and the #339 boundary)

Advocate: read-only (GitHub and all checkouts); this file is the only thing written. Date: 2026-11-14 (reports dated 2026-11-13).
Notation: D = this folder; V1..V5 = variant-1..5-original.md; ids S/C/X/U/A/SC are from D/diff-analysis.md; "F-nn / Fn" are the variant's own finding ids with the V-prefix.

## 1. Position summary

Variant 4 is the only report that tests #423 (v2) against what is really built, and its core findings survive the discovery that the observer exists: "v2" means two things (F1), v2's relay assumes a channel that only Orca workers have (F7), v2 has no trust or data-only rule (F8, F9), and v2 needs one named fact schema and store (F2, F4, F13, F15). V4 is wrong on one premise: it treated the observer as a future reader of the sweep JSON and did not notice the built `superclaude observe` (V5 is right, verified). That error changes the wording of V4's "(a)/(b)" proposals and of its #409 edit 2 (one data source becomes two collectors behind one contract), but it strengthens, not weakens, V4's F13 and F15. Recommended course: keep V4's #423 edits after correcting five lines (section 5), fold V5's D1 relationship note in as the schema-ownership answer, and do nothing irreversible before the owner answers the nine questions (recommendations in section 8).

## 2. Steelman of each opposing variant

### 2.1 V1 (#409 observer vs sweep)

**Strongest version.** V1 is the most exhaustive check of what a human-facing consumer can actually read from the sweep. It shows that the sweep JSON is printed to stdout only, after the commit point, and that the only file on disk is the Markdown report (V1 F-03; `commands.py:275-278` re-read: `_publish(...)` then `click.echo(json.dumps(...))`). It shows the key set is pinned (V1 F-01; design `orchestrator-mode.md:599`), that the sweep is not "read-only" in the sense #409 claims (two unresolved threads, SC-2; `_worktree_paths` takes every path from `worktree ps` with no root check, `probes.py:1203-1214` re-read; `_git` runs with `{**os.environ}`, `probes.py:309` re-read), that #409's "five findings: four fixed" was wrong (C-07), that the `policy` key does exist in JSON (C-25), and that the exact boundary sentence is not stated outside decision 8 (C-34; I confirm it occurs in live #409 only at line 119, inside decision 8, with "distinct from this view" at line 212). It also caught the clash between `Next inspection`/`safe_to_close` and the navigation-only rule (F-05) and the Markdown-escaped JSON strings (F-13). F-03, F-04, F-07 and F-13 are about the sweep itself and do not depend on whether the observer exists, so they hold for any consumer including v2.

**What V1 gets right that V4 needs.** V1 F-03 (no persisted JSON) is the same gap as V4 F4 (no durable store). V1 F-07 (git status runs on every path `worktree ps` names) is a real risk if v2 ever triggers sweeps across workspaces, which V4's F17 treats too lightly.

**Where I still critique it.** Its central premise ("No observer code exists", C-03, C-02, C-21) is false (X-012, verified). Its main recommendation (persist the sweep JSON so the observer reads it, F-03/Q1) targets a reader that does not exist and conflicts with the built design (V5 O-08, D1). It is silent on v2, trust, messaging and the "v2" label, and its verdict sentence "both are read-only and share the same dimension names" contradicts its own C-02 and F-02 (X-008).

### 2.2 V2 (#450 watcher)

**Strongest version.** V2 is the only report that follows the built watcher through to what the sweep would show. Facts: the watcher keeps only `operation_id`, so it cannot join to a transcript or tab map (W-03); `agent launch` is not an orchestration verb, so the session has no Run (W-04); the sweep would print `could not determine` today and "person session (active)" later, which is wrong in kind (W-05); the fixed prompt `/sc:issue --claim <N>` has no `--pipeline`, so the claim runs at the default `worktree` level and launches a second governed agent, against the watcher design's own "claim stops at next-steps.md" (W-01). I re-read `commands/issue.md:43` (default `worktree`) and `:88` ("unless `--pipeline none`, create or reuse the Orca worktree `issue-<N>` and launch one agent in it"), and `launch.py:33` (`PROMPT_PATTERN` has no `--pipeline`): W-01 is confirmed. I also re-read `classify.py:746-768`: when no tab-map entry exists and the origin guard is clear, `_person_origin` returns `True` with `NOTE_ORIGIN_NOT_PROVED`, which `_rule_person_active` (`:302-315`) turns into "person active". W-05's later-state mechanism is therefore real code, not speculation. V2 is unaffected by V5 and is the only report with live Orca host data.

**Where I still critique it.** It is narrow: 16 findings, most internal to #450's live test. Its consequence for v2 is limited to W-03..W-05 (a watcher session is Run-less and unlabelled), which V4's F7 already needs and should now cite. W-10 ("do not build an observer reader before FU-1") now reads as "do not add a watcher panel to the built observer" (U-005). Its live-host numbers (29 worktrees, 43 agent rows) cannot be re-checked (SC-12).

### 2.3 V3 (Coder#477 listener)

**Strongest version.** V3 is the only report that reads both the Coder repo and Claude Code's own cross-session docs. Its key finding is a premise contradiction: Coder#477 asks for a listener and registry, while the built watcher design chose "D1 option A (workspace-side poller, pull)" and states "Option A makes IronbellyOrg/Coder#477 unnecessary unless B or C is revisited" (`origin/master:docs/designs/issue-watcher.md` section 2.2, re-read). The owner's one comment on #477 ("Ensure this aligns with Orchestrator and TUI Panel", 2026-11-13T16:20:07Z, re-read) is six days after that decision and does not say whether it is reversed. V3 also shows five of eight candidate paths lack `image/` (SC-4), that #477 never separates read credentials from the send route (F10), that a peer message reaches a session as plain text and a slash command does not run (F8, Claude Code docs), that the Orca pairing offer is a no-expiry bearer credential (F11), and that the heartbeat collector endpoint is anonymous (F14). V3 F8 independently supports V4 F7: delivering a course correction to a running ordinary session is not a solved channel.

**Where I still critique it.** Edit 5 asks that a read-only status route return "the JSON object printed by `superclaude sweep run` ... without any write": `sweep run` publishes a dated report on every run (X-004, `commands.py:275-278`). Edit 8 prescribes the watcher launch without `--pipeline` and without the second agent (X-001). It treats "the observer" generically and so does not see that D9's likely referent, `superclaude observe`, has no network path at all (V5). Its F12 cites "decide one design, not two" without objection, which V4 F9 shows overpromises (X-005).

### 2.4 V5 (the built observer vs the sweep)

**Strongest version.** V5 reads the built code instead of the issue text, and finds the one fact that changes the question: `superclaude observe` is merged (PR #503 2026-11-11 `9e2608b03`, PR #514 2026-11-12 `cd873619`; both re-checked with `gh pr view`), registered in the shipped CLI, 33 files under `origin/master:src/superclaude/cli/observe/` (re-counted), and present in the review checkout's own `src/superclaude/cli/` listing. I re-ran the cross-reference test in both directions: no file in `src/superclaude/cli/observe/` mentions "sweep", and no file in `cli/sweep/`, `commands/sweep.md`, `skills/sc-sweep-protocol/`, `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` or `docs/designs/orchestrator-mode.md` mentions `superclaude observe`, `cli/observe`, #503 or #514 (both greps exit 1). The observer starts only Orca argv shapes (`adapter.py:29-32`: `worker-list --json --limit 100`, `skills list --json`, one `check` template; `MAX_CALLS_PER_CYCLE = 5` at `:57`, re-read) and polls every 15 s (`collector.py:28`, `INTERVAL = 15.0`, re-read), so the "observer reads the sweep JSON" premise of V1 and V4(b) is refuted. V5 also shows two different "v1" JSON schemas (O-08), two allow-lists that disagree on `check` (O-04), a sweep sentence ("pending questions cannot be read without consuming them", `report.py:534`, re-read) that the observer's research weakens (O-05), and that the observer shows none of the ten dimension names (RT-F02). Its D1 recommendation (keep both, write one contract, do not edit PR #539 for it) is reversible and cheap. It also catches that the live #409 body now carries a correction banner (my fetch: 30,758 bytes, banner at line 13).

**Where I still critique it.** (1) Scope: it compares observer to sweep and is nearly silent on v2 (only BC-17), trust, messaging, scale beyond Orca, or the "v2" label, so it cannot overturn V4's F1, F7, F8, F9, F14, F16. (2) Reproducibility: the owner-decision facts are only in a sibling worktree (SC-18; I confirmed `owner-decisions-20261108.md` is untracked there), and its 693-pass test run and 428-worker sample cannot be re-checked here (SC-20). (3) "All four earlier reviews ... wrong about one fact" overgeneralises: V2 and V3 never claim the observer is unbuilt (X-019). (4) "Drop W-3 from the #409 body" is a mislabel (X-020). (5) "Keep two readers" is an architecture choice the owner has not made (X-017; V5 itself asks it as Q2), and "Nothing needs code today" sits beside an unpushed switch-on with no PR (D5).

## 3. Strengths claimed for variant 4

1. **The "v2" label and gate (F1) is the highest-value finding and is unique to V4.** #339 comment 1 (2026-11-05T13:43:07Z): "Version 2 should include an orchestrator of orchestrators"; comment 2 (13:45:07Z) Step 3 line: "Build Steps 2 and 3 as part of version 2, before any orchestrator-of-orchestrators work" (both re-read with `gh issue view 339`). #423 says v2 = catalogue and cross-agent reviews and gates it on "after the v1 orchestrator (#339) is complete" (re-read #423 body). #339 is not complete after Phase 1 (design `orchestrator-mode.md:217` still lists Phase 2 `/sc:close`; PR #539 is "Phase 1"). Diff analysis C-001, SC-8 (CONFIRMED). No other variant addresses it; V1 only touches the O-of-O placement (F-16).
2. **The #423 claim check is accurate and has not drifted.** #423 `updatedAt` is still 2026-11-08T10:16:24Z, #339 still 2026-11-07T11:53:40Z, PR #539 head is still `873599d59` (all re-read on 2026-11-14), so V4's cites to them hold. V4 cites #409 by decision number, not by scratch-file line, so it drifts less than V1-V3 (S-009). C7 (#331 stale: the one #423 comment says "the intake poller is merged ... #331 is closed"), C6 (stale on PR #539) and C10 (contradicted by #339's own text) are all confirmed (C-017).
3. **F7 (v2 assumes a delivery channel that exists only for Orca workers) is decisive and untouched by V5.** WC-16 (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:288`, re-read): "Person-started sessions ... are never messaged. The `/sc:sweep` report is a dated local file; it is never posted. `/sc:sweep` recommends nothing." WC-13 (`:62`): a worker never calls `worker-start`, `dispatch`, `agent launch`. WC-12 (`:61`): follow-ups are pulled by the worker at checkpoints, not pushed. V3 F8 (Claude Code docs: a command in a peer message "arrives as plain text") and V2 fact 6 (a watcher session has no Run, so WC-16 does not apply) independently support it (diff C-015).
4. **F8 and F9 (no trust rule in #423; do not promise one trust design) are the better-supported side of X-005.** #339 comment 2 (re-read, line 22): signing or encrypting "cannot tell an honest instruction from one the sender picked up from a poisoned input"; Step 2 (line 34): fixed message types, free text only in `detail`. V3 F12 itself records that under option A the watcher's trust is "a different, local model". v2's relay (summarise one agent's work, feed another) is exactly the data-not-instruction case.
5. **F2, F4, F13, F15 (name the fields, the store, the schema owner, and a harness-neutral fact schema) become more valuable after V5, not less.** Sweep JSON row keys are `row_id, class, waiting_kind, rule, evidence, notes, policy, person_started, safe_to_close` (`report.py:642-661`, re-read); `ContractRecord.command` (`models.py:121`) and `WorktreeState.changed_paths` (`models.py:200`) exist and are emitted nowhere in `report.py` (grep: only `last_activity` at `:490`, in the Markdown report); the key set is pinned by a golden test (design `:599`). V5 O-08 shows a second "v1" (snapshot, camelCase, `"UNKNOWN"`, rows keyed `dispatchId`). With two collectors, V4 F15's "name the fact schema; the sweep is one collector of it" is the only frame that fits both, and V5's D1 "one contract, join key `dispatchId` = `row_id` minus `orca:`" is the same idea.
6. **F14 (determinism boundary) protects the observer's own rules from v2 and is stated by no other variant.** #409 live line 67: "stale is not failed, shared paths do not establish a conflict, and inspectable evidence is not admission"; design `:515`: finished rows never print `admitted`. V5 confirms the built observer is LLM-free and classifies nothing (U-008), so the rule v2 must respect is now running code.
7. **F16 (one cadence, not three) is proved by V5.** WC-16 `:288` "Phase 1 adds this duty, not a scheduler"; PM checkpoints "every 30min" (`skills/sc-pm-protocol/SKILL.md:46`); the observer polls every 15 s (`collector.py:28`). V4 asked v2 to reuse the WC-16 pass; the third cadence it feared already exists (X-015).
8. **F11 plus C7 (three documents describe a flow the design does not have) are confirmed.** The watcher design says it "starts one ordinary top-level agent session through `orca agent launch`" (`issue-watcher.md:15`, re-read); `grep -ri orchestrator|coordinator src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/` is empty (SC-6); Coder#477 body: "hand an approved issue command to its project's orchestrator" (re-read); live #409 line 74 draws "starts a claim session --> project's orchestrator (coordinator)". V2 W-06 and V3 F1/F17 reach the same contradiction from their own issues.
9. **F12 (cross-reference gaps) is right and checkable.** #423 Related lists #339, #409, #331, #326 and omits #444, #450, Coder#477, #534, PR #539 (re-read); #444 names #423 four times (re-counted); #339's body has no `#nnn` reference (re-counted). On #409, V4's prediction "already named" holds for #444, #450, #534, #539, Coder#477 and #423 (live body counts 3, 6, 3, 12, 7, 5) while V5 BC-22 adds the two it lacks (#503, #514).
10. **F21 (no v1 leakage into v2 territory) is a useful negative result** (`ls src/superclaude/cli/sweep` shows only classify, commands, models, probes, report, per V4 and the design scope section). F3, F5, F6, F10, F17, F18, F22 are further checkable gaps; F22 matches the technical reference ("No orchestrator role is implemented", `origin/master:docs/architecture/SUPERVISED-ORCHESTRATION-TECHNICAL-REFERENCE.md:760`; C9 at `:2038`, re-read).
11. **Paste-ready edits for three issues, with the unverified items labelled (C9, C11, #431, Collision Lens)** make V4 the most directly usable report for v2 (S-005), and its nine questions anticipate decisions V5 later surfaces independently (V4 Q9 "which flow does #477 target" is V5 D9; V4 Q5 "who owns the JSON contract" is V5 D1).

## 4. Weaknesses identified in the other variants

1. **V1's central premise is false** ("No observer code exists", C-03; C-02; C-21; X-012). `src/superclaude/cli/observe/` is in the review checkout itself (`ls src/superclaude/cli/` shows `observe`) and on master (33 files), PRs #503 and #514 are MERGED (re-checked). Consequence: V1's F-01 (1) "observer reads the sweep JSON", F-03 persist-for-the-observer, F-04 merge-several-reports, F-14, F-15 and A-05..A-07 rest on a reader that is not built that way (V5 RT-F01..RT-F15).
2. **V1's verdict contradicts its own claim check** ("Both are read-only and share the same dimension names" versus C-02 and F-02, X-008), and its tally does not add up (X-009). Its F-16/W-7 asks "where does the orchestrator of orchestrators belong" although #339 comments 1 and 2 already place it in "version 2" (a later #339 phase), which is the V4 F1 point.
3. **V1 is silent on v2, trust, messaging and the sweep-to-#477 status route** (C-014, C-015: "silent"). Four of the five reports therefore never test what v2 assumes.
4. **V2 is limited to the watcher** and its W-10 ("do not build an observer reader before FU-1") assumes no observer (U-005, X-019). Its W-05 is right but is a Phase 2 labelling item, not a #423 item, unless v2 wants per-class facts (it does: V4 F7).
5. **V3's Edit 5 is unachievable as worded** (a read-only status route returning `sweep run` JSON "without any write"; `commands.py:275-278` publishes a dated report on every run, X-004). Edit 8 repeats the watcher launch without the second agent (X-001). Its F12 adopts "decide one design, not two" without noting that its own text says the watcher's trust is a different local model (X-005). It treats "the observer" as unbuilt/generic, so Edit 5/6 target a consumer (`observe`) that has no network path.
6. **V3 and V2 and V1 cite a scratch file by line** (`/tmp/pr339/i409-new-body.md`), which no longer equals the live body (30,758 bytes live versus 30,030; the decision-8 sentence is at live line 119, scratch L116; S-009, A-010). V4 cites by decision number and #423/#339/PR head, all unchanged.
7. **V5 is silent on v2 except BC-17**, so it cannot decide the "v2" label (F1), the channel (F7), the trust rule (F8/F9), the determinism boundary (F14), the Related gaps of #423 (F12). Its sources for the owner decisions are in a sibling worktree and unpushed commits, not reproducible from master or GitHub (S-013, SC-18, SC-20).
8. **V5 overstates in three places.** (a) "All four earlier reviews ... wrong about one fact" (X-019: V2 and V3 never claim the observer is unbuilt). (b) "Drop W-3 from the #409 body" (X-020: W-3 is V1's proposal, not body text). (c) O-05 says the sweep's "cannot be read without consuming" is "stronger than the evidence"; but V5's own non-consumption evidence is owner-only and non-owner is UNVERIFIED, so the sweep's cautious wording is not shown wrong, only unproven (X-016). Also its D5 recommendation widens a permission ("any Run the terminal holds") that the owner has not given.
9. **V5's BC-17 replacement text ("the sweep's output or the observer's snapshot") would make the snapshot a v2 input** although the snapshot schema calls itself "PROPOSED, not an Orca contract", is additive-only with `UNKNOWN` strings, and was designed for a later Go screen (U-012). That is a larger commitment than V4's "name a fact schema, collectors plug in" (F15).
10. **All four of V1, V2, V3, V4 missed the observer, but only V4 is asked to be an anchor for v2.** The common cause (A-016, O-15: no command, skill, routing row or mention of `observe` anywhere in the sweep docs) is a discoverability defect in IronClaude, not only a reviewer error.

## 5. Concessions (what is wrong or weak in variant 4)

1. **Missed the built observer.** V4's scope table lists `cli/sweep`, `cli/issue_watch`, `reflexion.py` and `docs/memory`, and its "Not done" line only mentions the #409 design branch. `ls src/superclaude/cli/` in its own checkout shows `observe`. Result: proposals (a) and (b) ("observer and v2 share one data source: HOLDS") and #423 edit 3 ("reads the facts that `superclaude sweep run` already produces ... does not add a second scanner") describe a single source. The corrected statement: two collectors exist (sweep: per-Run, disk, contracts, git; observe: all-Run Orca ledger), no file references the other, and v2 should read both only through one named fact contract. Also add `src/superclaude/cli/observe/` and PRs #503/#514 to #423's Components Affected and Related list.
2. **The "boundary is aligned" evidence is circular (X-002, F19, C8).** The sentence "this observer shows the human what happened, v2 shares knowledge between agents" occurs only inside #409 decision 8 (live line 119). The weaker claim ("distinct", #423 Related; #409 live line 212 "distinct from this view") holds. V4's #409 edit 2 keeps the unsupported sentence; V1's W-7 wording ("proposed boundary") is more accurate.
3. **F13 and #409 edit 2 assumed the observer would ask the sweep for keys.** It does not (X-013). The corrected statement: the sweep's JSON owner decision (#339, PR #539, golden test) is separate from the observer snapshot's (observe spec, #409); v2 is the only consumer that may need both, so "decide once" applies to the relationship note (V5 D1), not to adding keys for the observer.
4. **F17 states the sweep "confines every path" (X-007).** True for file reads (`_inside`, `probes.py:525-531`, design `:512`), false for git targets: `_worktree_paths` (`probes.py:1203-1214`) resolves every path from `worktree ps` with no root check, and `_git` runs with `{**os.environ}` (`probes.py:309`). The conclusion of F17 (v2 needs a read-permission model) is strengthened.
5. **F3 understates scope (X-010).** Rows are Run-bound, but `worktrees[]` and transcript scans come from host-global `worktree ps`.
6. **F7's per-class acceptance criterion cannot be met from sweep facts (X-003).** The sweep cannot tell a watcher-launched session from a person's (`classify.py:746-768`, `:302-315`). The AC should add "or name the new source that labels machine-started sessions (#534 or Phase 2)".
7. **C7/F11 say "starts one ordinary session" and stop (X-001).** The claim at the default level then launches a second governed agent (`issue.md:43,88`; V2 W-01). The conclusion (it notifies no orchestrator) stands; the proposed #423 sentence should add the caveat.
8. **F18 over-reads "Collision Lens".** It is a synthetic design image (`origin/master:.dev/design/orca-observer-20261106/PREVIEWS.md:89`, `README.md:15`), not built. The only collision detector in code is `worktrees[].shared_package_ids`. "A third detector" should read "a second code detector plus an unbuilt design".
9. **Mis-cited file in C3.** "pm.md:23" and "pm.md:46" are in `src/superclaude/skills/sc-pm-protocol/SKILL.md:23,46` (re-read: "one store per worktree"; "every 30min"); `commands/pm.md` contains neither. The facts are true.
10. **F21/F17 use "read-only" loosely (A-006).** `sweep run` writes a dated report each run (design `:13` "read-only command" with one dated report; `commands.py:275-278`) and executes git with inherited env; V1 F-07 is a real open risk before v2 reuses sweep runs on other workspaces.
11. **F2 precision.** `last_activity` is emitted in the Markdown report (`report.py:490`) though not in JSON; only `command` and `changed_paths` are emitted nowhere.
12. **F10 candidate list.** The built observer is a human-facing reader, a weak candidate for "management server" (V5; C-012).
13. **Format.** Nine questions without recommendations (S-005; supplied in section 8), no severity, no live Orca checks, sub-orchestrator research and #431 only partly read (V4 says so itself).

## 6. Shared assumptions A-001..A-016

- **A-001 QUALIFY.** For the built observer REJECT: it contains no reference to the sweep and starts only Orca argv shapes (`adapter.py:29-32`; grep exit 1; X-013). For v2 the sweep JSON is the only carrier of per-session class, proof, git and contract facts but lacks `command`, branch and `changed_paths`, is not persisted, and is Run-bound, so v2 should read a named fact schema with the sweep and the observer as collectors (V4 F15). As the payload of a Coder#477 status route REJECT (`commands.py:275-278` writes a report per run).
- **A-002 QUALIFY.** Per-Run is the sweep's row unit but `worktrees[]` are host-global (`probes.py:1203-1214`, X-010); the observer reads all Runs for Orca rows (`--limit 100`, at most 5 calls per cycle). One host is the unit for both; v2's "all workspaces" has no stated unit (V4 F3).
- **A-003 REJECT.** PRs #503 and #514 are MERGED and the package is on master (33 files). V4 shared this error (section 5, item 1). What stays UNSTATED is whether the owner wants releases R3/R4 and the reviewer's four asks (V5 D7).
- **A-004 QUALIFY.** PR #539 is open at head `873599d59` with two unresolved threads (SC-2); additive keys are cheap only as deliberate golden-test edits (design `:599`), renames or retyping need a schema v2. Do not hang observer or v2 wishes on #539; log them in #534 or #339 (V5 D4 agrees).
- **A-005 REJECT as an assumption, ACCEPT as a risk.** The technical reference says the sub-orchestrator layer is "deferred" with no command, skill, flag or contract role (`origin/master:docs/architecture/SUPERVISED-ORCHESTRATION-TECHNICAL-REFERENCE.md:1829`, re-read), #444 is an open evaluation, and V4 found no v2 item in `PHASE2-preflight-input.md` (not re-read by me). Every deferred item needs a named issue; V4's v2 gate (PR #539 merged plus the #534 capture) is concrete where the others are not.
- **A-006 REJECT.** `_worktree_paths` accepts every path `worktree ps` returns with no root check (`probes.py:1203-1214`), `_git` runs with `{**os.environ, ...}` (`probes.py:309`), and each run writes a dated report (`commands.py:275-278`). "Read-only" means no Orca write verb plus one report. V1 F-07 is correct; V4 F21/F17 used the word loosely.
- **A-007 ACCEPT, and promote it to a v2 constraint.** The sweep reads one OS user's `~/.claude/projects/<key>` folders (`probes.py:1344-1350`); all Coder workspaces share `HOME=/home/coder` (V3 F4); the observer's question panel works only in the terminal that holds the Run (V5). "All workspaces" breaks the assumption, which is V4 F17.
- **A-008 QUALIFY.** The sweep needs a bound Run or `--run` (V2 fact 4; design `:241-245`, not re-read) and WC-16 applies to a coordinator that has a Run; the observer's ledger does not need one. Person-started and watcher-launched sessions have no Run and are transcript-only rows (V2). v2 must say whether Run-less sessions are in the catalogue.
- **A-009 ACCEPT as an unverified risk.** Two documents already record two different builds of the same Orca release: the sweep design `orchestrator-mode.md:30` says `1.4.245-aidev02.780594c521aa`, the technical reference (`origin/master:docs/architecture/SUPERVISED-ORCHESTRATION-TECHNICAL-REFERENCE.md:446`, read 2026-11-12) says `1.4.245-aidev02.9cfecdd30b2a`; the observer records no version (V5). Every v2 fact should carry its collector and Orca build.
- **A-010 REJECT.** The live #409 body moved (30,758 bytes, correction banner at line 13, decision 8 at line 119); #423 (2026-11-08T10:16:24Z), #339 (2026-11-07T11:53:40Z) and PR #539 (`873599d59`) did not. Pin every edit set to `updatedAt` and head sha.
- **A-011 REJECT.** The decision-8 sentence is rewritten by V1 W-7, V4 #409 edit 2 and V5 BC-17 (S-006, X-002). Apply one reviewed edit set, in the order given in section 8.
- **A-012 QUALIFY.** Keeping both programs is the cheapest-to-reverse working assumption (V5 D1(a)), but only the owner can say it (V5 Q2). #339's body asks for a static chat report "taking inspiration" from #409 (V1 C-33), which neither choice breaks. v2 does not depend on the answer if it reads a fact contract.
- **A-013 QUALIFY.** Polling Orca CLI read verbs is how both programs work today, and Orca's `projection.attention` and durable event replay are unexamined (V5 O-13; live #409 line 69 lists replay as unverified). v2 is not bound by it: #423's own proposal includes child-session reports at checkpoints and agents "searching sessions or diffing logs" (re-read), which are non-polling and transcript sources.
- **A-014 REJECT.** `snapshot.schema.json` has `schemaVersion` const 1 (camelCase, `"UNKNOWN"` strings); the sweep has `schema_version` (snake_case, `null`). Always say "sweep report JSON v1" or "observe snapshot v1"; read V4 F2/F13 as about the sweep JSON only.
- **A-015 REJECT (for the observer).** Master has only `owner-decisions-20261111.md` under `.dev/issues/409/` (SC-18); `owner-decisions-20261108.md` is untracked in the sibling worktree (`git status` re-read). The same risk applies to v2: the gate, trust and channel decisions (V4 Q1-Q9) are recorded nowhere yet and should go on #423 or #339 once made.
- **A-016 REJECT.** `ls src/superclaude/cli/` in the review checkout lists `observe`, yet V1-V4 missed it; no command, skill or routing row names it (V5 O-15). V4's own #423 component list needs `cli/observe/` added.

## 8. Course of action for the owner

Nothing below is posted or run by this report. "Reversible first step" means the step can be undone by editing or reverting a document, comment, issue body or one constant; none of these steps touches live Orca, GitHub state beyond text, or a merge. Every outward-facing edit (issue bodies, comments, PRs) needs the owner's go-ahead.

**0. Cross-cutting first step (do this before any issue edit).**
1. Write the short relationship note (V5 D1): names "sweep report JSON v1" and "observe snapshot v1"; join key `dispatchId` = `row_id` minus `orca:`; which program owns which fact (V5 "Facts each program owns"); the cadences (observer 15 s, sweep at most once per 5 minutes under WC-16, any v2 refresh is the WC-16 pass); one list of read-only Orca verbs and who may call which; what files each writes. Dependency: none. Reversible: yes (one document, linked from design section 1). It settles X-006, X-011, X-013, X-015, X-017 in one place and is the input every later wording needs.

**1. #409 (observer issue; body is the collision surface of five reports)**
2. Apply the V5 corrections that say "no observer code exists" (BC-01..07, 11, 14, 15, 19-21, 23) as one reviewed edit set, then rewrite decisions 1, 6, 8 and 10 once: decision 1 and 8 as "two collectors behind one contract (relationship note)"; decision 6 as V4's "one grant vocabulary and one audit record" (V4 #409 edit 1); decision 8's boundary sentence as "distinct from this view" (X-002); decision 10 gains the v2 store as a third candidate (V4 #409 edit 3). Dependency: step 1 (final wording) and the owner's go-ahead to edit the body. Reversible: yes (issue edit history). Do not keep V1 W-7, V4 #409 edit 2 and V5 BC-17 side by side (A-011).
3. Remove the correction banner only after step 2 (the status table, "Why this is being done" and "Plan" are still stale below it). Dependency: step 2. Reversible: yes.

**2. observe (built `superclaude observe`)**
4. Add one routing row for `superclaude observe` in the routing guide (V5 D8), in its own small PR, following the project's rule for copying the guide. This addresses the root cause of the miss (A-016). Dependency: none. Reversible: yes.
5. Leave the questions-read switch-on to the 409 session: the owner first states whether the pass covers one Run or any Run the terminal holds (V5 D5); then a fresh branch off master. Not a v2 dependency. Reversible: yes (one constant, `QUESTION_GATE_PASSED`).

**3. sweep and #339**
6. Do not edit PR #539 for anything in this report (A-004). Log in #534: the wording of `report.py:199,534` and design 7.5 and section 17 item 5 (X-016; V5 O-02, RT-F06); the `worktree ps` git-target and inherited-environment risk (V1 F-07), which needs an owner decision to fix or record as accepted before any other consumer (v2, a status route) runs the sweep on other checkouts. Dependency: none for logging; the F-07 decision belongs to the PR #539 owner. Reversible: yes.
7. #339 text: add the Related line, the terminology line ("version 2" in the comments is the trust layer and orchestrator of orchestrators; "v2" in #423 is the knowledge layer), the management-server candidates, and one comment recording the JSON-contract decision, rewritten for two contracts (V4 #339 edits 1-4). Dependency: step 1 and the owner's answer to Q1 below. Reversible: yes.
8. #534: add the shared read-only capture request (a Run with one live and one blocked worker; `projection.attention`; `worker-show` value names, V5 D3). Dependency: owner permission for the capture under the sweep's allow-list (it is read-only and uses an allowed verb). Reversible: yes (capture is read-only; it restores Rule 5 and Rule 4(a), V4 F6).

**4. #450 (watcher)**
9. The owner decides whether the watcher's claim runs at `--pipeline none` or the default level (V2 Q1; X-001) before the live test and the cron entry; add V2's FU-1 capture items (session identity fields, run-list before and after, permission mode, Orca Claude command). Dependency: none; it blocks the live test and cron. Reversible: yes until the first live run (the fixed prompt pattern and its pins are one place in `launch.py`).

**5. Coder#477 (listener)**
10. The owner answers three things on #477: is it still wanted given option A (V3 Q1); does "TUI Panel" mean the built observer, which is single-host and has no network path (V5 D9, V5 Q1); which flow it targets (V4 Q9; recommended answer: the built watcher, which starts an ordinary session). Then apply V3's status paragraph, `image/` path fixes, read-versus-send separation and cross-reference edits (V3 Edits 1, 2, 6, 11) and add #423. Do not adopt V3 Edit 5 as worded (X-004) or Edit 8 without the claim-level decision of step 9. Dependency: the owner's three answers. Reversible: yes.
11. Start no listener or registry work. Reversible: n/a (nothing started).

**6. #423 (v2)**
12. Apply V4's #423 edits 1-7 with these five corrections: (i) edit 3 names both collectors and the contract ("reads the facts the sweep and the observer already produce through one named fact contract; no third scanner of Orca, git or transcripts"); (ii) edit 4's per-class criterion adds "or names the source that labels machine-started sessions (#534 or Phase 2)"; (iii) edit 6 adds `src/superclaude/cli/observe/` and PRs #503 and #514 and cites the PM facts at `skills/sc-pm-protocol/SKILL.md:23,46`; (iv) edit 7's #331 line adds that the claim at its default level launches a second governed agent (#450 W-01); (v) the #409 Related line says the observer is built. 13. Recommended answers to V4's nine questions (for the owner to accept or change; V4 gave none):

Rename by terminology line, not by renaming the #339 comments.
