# Round 1 advocate: variant 5 (the already built observer versus the sweep)

Advocate: adv-r1-v5 (read-only; wrote only this file). Date: 2026-11-14. Sources: diff-analysis.md, variants 1 to 5 (same folder), plus my own re-checks listed in the "Evidence I re-checked" block under section 3.

## 1. Position summary

Variant 5 is right on the one fact that decides the rest: `superclaude observe` is merged and registered (PRs #503 and #514), reads Orca directly, and the sweep and the observer never mention each other. That removes the premise "the observer will read the sweep JSON" that V1 and parts of V4 rest on, and turns the question into "how do two programs built in isolation relate", where V5's answer (keep both, write one contract, change no code until a few owner decisions are made) survives the V1, V3 and V4 arguments for one data source, one trust design and a fleet view, with two qualifications: "keep both" must not mean "never share a parser", and V5's treatment of v2 inputs and of "TUI Panel" is thinner than it looks.

## 2. Steelman of each opposing variant

### 2.1 Variant 1 (#409 observer versus the sweep, written without knowing the observer exists)

**Strongest version.** V1 is the most careful claim check of the #409 body and of the sweep's own contract. Its core argument: two programs that both show a human "what needs me" will drift unless there is one definition of each term and one source of each fact. It backs that with code: the JSON has nine row keys and no run, worktree, question text, requester or age (`report.py:642-661`); only four of ten dimensions ever get a value; `Next inspection` and `safe_to_close` collide with the "no action, even greyed" rule; the JSON goes to stdout only, after the commit point (`commands.py:270-277`, re-read by me); the git-status path accepts every path from `worktree ps` (`probes.py:1203-1214`, re-read) and two PR threads are still unresolved (my GraphQL read: `probes.py:1052` and `probes.py:1211` open). It also finds two wrong claims in the #409 body (six threads not five; `policy` IS a JSON row key) and one unsupported sentence (the "boundary this issue already states"). Even after V5, every sweep-side finding (F-02, F-05, F-07, F-13, F-18, C-07, C-25, C-34) stands, and V1's instinct that a single written contract is needed is the same conclusion V5 reaches (D1).

**What V1 gets right that V5 must accept.** (1) The reviewer's four asks (summary, links, named steps, open a session) have no source in either program, and the only deterministic sources V1 found (Task spec text read at `probes.py:1199`, `Command:` lines, handoff files) live on the sweep side. So an observer that ever grows those asks will have to read sweep-side facts: V1's "persist the JSON next to the report" is the prerequisite for V5's own option (b). (2) The two programs already disagree in vocabulary (V5 O-07 confirms and adds a third vocabulary).

**Critique with counter-evidence.** V1's premise is false: "No observer code exists" (C-03, "Verified") is refuted by `git ls-tree origin/master src/superclaude/cli/observe/` (14 entries), `main.py:572,574`, and both PRs MERGED (re-checked: 2026-11-11T19:54:57Z and 2026-11-12T12:36:19Z). The observer is not "a future reader of the sweep JSON": a grep for "sweep" in the observer tree on master returns nothing, and a grep for the observer in the sweep tree, design, command, skill and WORKER-CONTRACT returns nothing (both re-run by me). So F-01, F-03 (as an observer requirement), F-04, F-12, F-14, F-15, C-02, C-03, C-21, C-26 and A-01/A-05/A-06/A-07 describe a consumer that does not exist in that form. V1's Q1 recommendation, "persist the JSON so one collector serves both", was made without knowing a second collector with its own 15 s poller already runs. V1 also contradicts itself: its verdict says "both are read-only and share the same dimension names" while C-02 says "overstated" and F-02 says the vocabulary diverges (X-008). Its claim tally does not add up (X-009).

### 2.2 Variant 2 (#450 watcher rollout)

**Strongest version.** V2 is the only report that traces what a watcher-launched session actually looks like to the sweep, and its top finding is a real defect with a one-line fix. The watcher fixes the prompt to `/sc:issue --claim <N>` with no `--pipeline`; the command's default level is `worktree`, which creates or reuses the Orca worktree `issue-<N>` and launches one agent in it (`src/superclaude/commands/issue.md:43,88`, re-read by me), while the watcher design says the claim "stops at next-steps.md" (`issue-watcher.md:44`). Then the session is Run-less, so a Run-bound sweep cannot read it; the sweep's own `_person_origin` returns `person_started=True` with the note `origin not proved` when no join or tab-map entry exists (`classify.py:748-767`, re-read), so the report could call a machine-started session a person's. V2 adds that its own live test and 24 h shadow run do not capture the facts Orchestrator mode needs (W-03, W-04, W-09, W-16), and that `healthy` is false during a planned stop (W-11). All of this is untouched by V5 and I accept it.

**Critique.** V2 is mostly orthogonal to V5, so the critique is narrow. (1) W-10 ("do not build an observer reader before FU-1") assumes the observer is unbuilt; after V5 it must read "do not add a watcher panel to the existing observer". (2) V2 never says that the built observer is equally blind to watcher-launched sessions: the observer reads `worker-list`, which is Dispatch-based, and a watcher session has no Dispatch (V2's own W-04 mechanism, which V2 marks UNVERIFIED). So neither program can show "started by the watcher for issue N", and the labelling work V2 defers to Phase 2 applies to both. (3) V2's live host counts (29 worktrees, 43 agent rows) cannot be reproduced from a checkout.

### 2.3 Variant 3 (Coder#477 workspace listener)

**Strongest version.** V3 reads the Coder side and external Claude Code docs, which nobody else does, and finds that Coder#477 is out of step with what was built: #477 presumes a listener, the built watcher has none and its design says "Option A makes IronbellyOrg/Coder#477 unnecessary unless B or C is revisited" (F1); five of eight file paths are wrong (verified by the diff analysis, SC-4); "monitor" is never defined (F3); read and send are not separated, so a read credential could reach the send route (F10); the Orca pairing offer is a no-expiry bearer credential (F11); trust criteria lack expiry, replay and scope (F12); and a peer message to a running session arrives as plain text, so a slash command does not run (F8). It also lists what IronClaude must supply (identity envelope, remote collection, a grant format, a written listener contract). None of this depends on the observer, and I accept all of it.

**Critique with counter-evidence.** (1) Edit 5 would make a registered workspace return "the JSON object printed by `superclaude sweep run` ... produced without any write". That cannot hold: `sweep run` publishes a dated report and then prints the JSON (`commands.py:270-277`, re-read), so each call writes a file (X-004). The only no-write static status that exists today is the observer's `observe --once` (it writes only `if options.snapshot`, `commands.py:232`, re-read). (2) Edit 8 (and V4's C7) describe one ordinary session and omit the second governed agent the default claim level launches (X-001). (3) V3 treats "the observer" as a generic future consumer and never checks whether it is built, although the owner comment it analyses says "Ensure this aligns with Orchestrator and TUI Panel". Edits 5 and 6 ask a registered workspace to serve a read-only status route to "the TUI observer", but the built observer has no network, credential or file-input path at all (three Orca argv shapes only; `adapter.py:29-32`, re-read). (4) V3's identity gap (F4: no host, workspace, owner, Run key) is partly met on the observer side: the observer snapshot rows carry `runId`, `taskId`, `hostId`, `hostKind` (15 required row keys, re-read from `snapshot.schema.json`), which the sweep JSON rows lack; workspace id, owner and login are still missing on both, so F4 stands for #477, but the envelope should be designed against two row shapes, not one.

### 2.4 Variant 4 (#423 v2 and the #339 boundary)

**Strongest version.** V4 finds the vocabulary trap that will bite the owner: "v2" means the knowledge layer in #423 but the trust layer plus orchestrator of orchestrators in #339's comments, so the gate "after v1 (#339) is complete" is ambiguous (F1, verified by the diff analysis SC-8). It then lists what v2 assumes that v1 does not provide: JSON v1 omits command, branch, changed paths, issue/PR, last activity although the data model has some of them (`models.py:121,157,200`, SC-1); scope is one bound Run on one host; there is no durable store ("never posted", WC-16); no GitHub reads; the delivery channel for course corrections exists only for Orca workers (F7); the relay is the "permission laundering" path (F8); an LLM-derived catalogue could break the deterministic rules the observer and sweep keep (F14, which V5 supports: the built observer is itself deterministic and LLM-free). It also argues for "one grant vocabulary and one audit record" rather than "one trust design" (F9), which I think is better supported than V3's version. V4's paste-ready edits to #423 are the most directly usable edit set in the folder, and F1, F7 to F12 and F14 to F22 are not touched by V5.

**Critique with counter-evidence.** (1) Proposals (a) and (b) say the observer and v2 "share one data source" and "both read the same sweep facts". The observer does not read the sweep (verified, see 2.1), so the closing sentence of V4's #423 edit 3, "both read the same sweep facts", is false as written and must not be pasted. (2) F13 ("observer and v2 will both ask for keys") assumes one contract; there are two (A-014). (3) F16 warns a v2 timer would create a second scheduler beside WC-16; a second poller already exists (`collector.py` `INTERVAL = 15.0`, re-read) with no shared limit. (4) C8 marks "#409 is a read-only observation dashboard distinct from v2" TRUE; true of intent, but the #409 body still says the observer "does not exist". (5) F19 ("boundary aligned") leans on the sentence that V1 showed appears only inside #409 decision 8 itself (X-002), so the evidence is circular. (6) F17 says the sweep "confines every path"; true for file reads, incomplete for git-status targets (X-007). (7) V4 did not read the #409 design branch or notice `src/superclaude/cli/observe/` in its own checkout (A-016).

## 3. Strengths claimed

**Evidence I re-checked on 2026-11-14 (all read-only).** `git show origin/master:src/superclaude/cli/main.py` lines 572 and 574 register `observe`; `git ls-tree origin/master src/superclaude/cli/observe/` lists 14 entries; `gh pr view` shows #503 MERGED 2026-11-11T19:54:57Z (`9e2608b03`) and #514 MERGED 2026-11-12T12:36:19Z (`cd8736199`), #539 OPEN head `873599d59` MERGEABLE; `QUESTION_GATE_PASSED = False` at `commands.py:67` on master; `git grep -i sweep origin/master -- src/superclaude/cli/observe` is empty; a grep for "superclaude observe|cli/observe|#503|#514" over the sweep tree, design, `sweep.md`, the sweep skill and WORKER-CONTRACT is empty; `docs/designs/orchestrator-mode.md:603` still says "copied from #409 at `495eb96d` because #409 is unmerged" and `:559` says the cursor template "is added only after step 1.0a captures the cursor field"; the snapshot schema's `row` requires 15 keys (no worktree, no command, no branch); sweep rows are keyed `orca:<dispatch>` (`classify.py:891`); sibling worktree `issue-409-mvp` HEAD `f104ca51a` with the two unpushed commits and untracked `owner-decisions-20261108.md`, `live-test-guide.md`; PR #539 GraphQL shows two unresolved threads (`probes.py:1052`, `probes.py:1211`); the live #409 body is 30,756 bytes (updatedAt 2026-11-13T16:57:51Z) and opens with a correction banner; #477 still has one comment and #339, #423 and #450 are unchanged since 2026-11-07/08, so no owner has answered any question yet; `orca-ide orchestration --help` contains no watch, stream, event, subscribe, follow or tail word; no routing row, command or skill mentions `superclaude observe` in `src/superclaude/{core,commands,skills}` or `docs/user-guide` on master.

1. **It found the decisive fact and it holds.** The observer is merged, registered and tested. Evidence: `main.py:572,574`; PRs #503 and #514 (above); diff analysis X-012, SC-13. Every other variant's premise about "the observer" is checked against this, not the other way round.
2. **It is the only report that read the built code instead of an issue's text.** Evidence: S-008 and S-013 (code on `origin/master` extracted with `git archive`, a sibling worktree, a fixture run, 693 tests passed in V5's copy). The diff analysis lists the miss as a shared defect of V1 to V4 (A-016: the review checkout itself held `src/superclaude/cli/observe/`).
3. **It shows there are two "JSON v1" contracts and gives a join key.** Observer snapshot: `schemaVersion`, camelCase, `"UNKNOWN"` strings, rows keyed `dispatchId`, 15 required row keys (re-read from `snapshot.schema.json`). Sweep: `schema_version`, snake_case, `null`, rows keyed `row_id` = `orca:<dispatch>` (`classify.py:891`). Join key = `dispatchId`. Evidence: V5 O-08; diff analysis C-019, A-014.
4. **It audits V1 line by line and says which findings survive.** The RT table classes 18 findings (closed, changed, wrong premise, remains, not affected), so the owner can discard exactly the stale ones and keep F-02, F-05, F-07, F-13, F-18. Evidence: V5 re-test table; diff analysis S-011, C-018.
5. **It found the dark and unpushed state.** The questions read is off on master (`commands.py:67`, re-read), the switch-on exists only as two local commits in a sibling worktree whose history does not contain `origin/master`, the remote branch is gone, and the owner's pass record is an untracked or unpushed file. Evidence: SC-14, SC-18, my own `git -C issue-409-mvp log` and `ls`; diff analysis U-011.
6. **It found stale statements on the sweep side that only the observer's existence reveals.** Design 7.5 "because #409 is unmerged" (`orchestrator-mode.md:603`, re-read), the cursor field called open at `:559` although `probes.py:1054` reads `page.hasMore`, and `report.py:199,534` ("pending questions cannot be read without consuming them") against the observer's gate research. Evidence: V5 O-02, O-05; SC-16.
7. **Its overlap table separates real duplication from apparent duplication.** Two readers of `worker-list` with different caps (observer: 100 per page, up to 5 calls, 15 s; sweep: once per Run, 50 `worker-show`, 90 s, at most once per 5 minutes) and two allow-lists that disagree on `check`, but the observer classifies nothing, so the "two classifiers that could disagree" danger V1 feared does not exist. Evidence: V5 O-01, O-04, S4; `adapter.py:29-32,57` and the forbidden-prefix list (re-read); WC-16 (SC-11).
8. **Its first steps are small, reversible and avoid the open PR.** A relationship note, a corrected #409 body, a log entry in #534. It tells the owner not to edit #539 for wording while two real review threads (one a git-config security item) are still open. Evidence: D1, D4; my GraphQL read of the threads.
9. **It protects the one fact that is safety-relevant.** It will not widen any Orca read: non-owner non-consumption of `check` is UNVERIFIED, so the sweep keeps `check` forbidden (D4) and the observer keeps one template behind a guard. Evidence: V5 O-04, O-05, D4; `adapter.py` forbidden prefixes.
10. **It reconstructs who decided what and finds the missing record.** The acceptance criterion "a human selects the direction before production integration" is not met as recorded (no AC-S record), although the owner said "Stage it" (2026-11-08) and "Proceed with building the first release" (2026-11-11). Evidence: V5 S9, D6; PR #503 "Known gaps"; sibling `owner-decisions-20261108.md` (re-listed by me).
11. **It surfaces that V3's and V4's consumers are a different shape than assumed.** The observer is single-host, local Orca CLI, no credential, no network (R4 remote is gated), so V3's status route targets a consumer that currently has no path to it, and V4's "observer and v2 share one data source" must name which source. Evidence: V5 D9, S10, RT-F04; diff analysis X-014, C-011 V5 effect.

## 4. Weaknesses identified in the other variants

1. **V1 rests on a false premise through most of its integration analysis.** "No observer code exists" (C-03), "the observer reads the sweep JSON" (F-01), "the observer would have to run `sweep run` itself" (F-03), "the observer must merge several reports" (F-04), "observer must render from JSON" (F-14), "delta needs two stored JSON files" (F-15), "reading the JSON keeps the observer free of an LLM polling loop" (C-21). Evidence: `main.py:572,574`; PRs #503 and #514 MERGED; the observer reads Orca only and already has a 15 s poller, atomic 0600 snapshot, STALE after 45 s (`collector.py` `INTERVAL = 15.0`; diff analysis SC-17). V1's `gh pr list --head docs/409-orca-dashboard-designs` returned 0 only because the merged PRs came from other branches (X-012).
2. **V1's verdict sentence contradicts its own tables.** "Both are read-only and share the same dimension names" versus C-02 "Overstated" and F-02 "Gap and contradiction in vocabulary" (X-008); the built observer shows none of the ten dimension names (V5 RT-F02). Its claim tally does not add up (X-009).
3. **V1's key recommendation (Q1, "persist the JSON; it keeps one collector") was made blind to a second collector.** Evidence: two readers of `worker-list` exist (O-01); the observer's `--once` already gives a no-write static text record (`commands.py:232`).
4. **V3's Edit 5 is unachievable as written.** The status route "runs only the sweep's read-only commands ... without any write", but `sweep run` publishes a dated report then prints JSON (`commands.py:270-277`; X-004, SC-9). It also asks for an observer-facing route although the built observer has no network or credential path (`adapter.py:29-32`).
5. **V3's Edit 8 and V4's C7/F11 omit the second governed agent** the default `--pipeline worktree` launches (`issue.md:43,88`; X-001, SC-3), so the text they would put into #477 mis-describes the flow.
6. **V3 never checked whether "the observer" or a "TUI Panel" exists**, though the owner comment it analyses names one, and it labels the `/tmp` body a "draft" although V1 had verified it live (S-009).
7. **V4's proposals (a) and (b) and its #423 edit 3 sentence "both read the same sweep facts" are false after V5**: the observer reads Orca, not the sweep (X-013). F13 treats two schema requests as one contract (A-014); F16's "second scheduler" risk is already realised (X-015).
8. **V4's F19 "boundary aligned" is circular.** The sentence it cites as proof appears only inside decision 8 of the #409 body that V4 is checking (X-002, SC-7); V4 C8 checks intent, not the body's "does not exist" text.
9. **V4's F17 overstates confinement.** "confines every path" holds for file reads (`probes.py:529`) but `_worktree_paths` accepts every path from `worktree ps` with no root check and `git status` runs with `{**os.environ, ...}` (`probes.py:1203-1214` and `:309`, re-read; X-007). Two PR threads about this are still open (my GraphQL read).
10. **V2's W-10 and V3/V4's "future observer" wording are out of date**, and V2 (and everyone else) missed that the observer cannot see watcher-launched sessions either (no Dispatch, so no `worker-list` row); the labelling work V2 defers to Phase 2 applies to both programs (A-008).
11. **All four reports pose duplicate owner questions and write overlapping edits to the same #409 sentences** (S-006, S-007, X-002). Applying V1, V4 and V5 independently would rewrite decision 8 three different ways. (V5 contributes to this, see concession 8.)
12. **None of V1 to V4 recorded that the code existed in its own checkout** (A-016: commits `9e2608b03` and `cd8736199` are in the review checkout's history; my `git ls-tree` confirms the directory). The process failure, not any single claim, is the reason "silent" in a V1 to V4 cell cannot be read as "checked and found nothing".

## 5. Concessions (what is wrong or weak in variant 5)

1. **No live Orca call.** Every Orca fact is from code, golden samples or another person's records: Q-1 FAIL, non-consumption by the owner, `worker-show` value names for live workers, what `projection.attention` means for a blocked worker, whether `ask` writes type `question`. V5 marks these UNVERIFIED and I cannot upgrade them (diff analysis SC-20).
2. **The decision history rests on files that are not on master.** `owner-decisions-20261108.md`, `-20261112.md`, the pass record and the live-test guide live in a sibling worktree (untracked, or in two unpushed commits); I re-listed them but a reader of master and GitHub cannot reproduce them (S-013, A-015). The pass record says "Written by the owner" and V5 itself marks authorship UNVERIFIED.
3. **V5's own test run was not clean and was not re-run by me or the analyst.** 693 passed and 1 failed in a scratch copy for a harness reason; the 428-worker golden sample is unreproducible here (SC-20).
4. **"Nothing needs code today" is too strong.** Three cheap, reversible items are worth doing before the end-state is argued: persist the sweep's JSON (the prerequisite of V5's own option (b), V1's F-03 and any status route), open the PR that flips the questions guard (the owner already said "Do it"; no PR exists), and fix the stale design text. V5's D5 first step does lead to a PR, so the verdict line overstates its own D-list.
5. **D9 is incomplete.** V5 says "TUI Panel" in Coder#477 "probably means `superclaude observe`". The owner's own 2026-11-08 record (sibling, `owner-decisions-20261108.md` items 4 and 5) says the observer "may become a TUIBBS module" and that the snapshot exists so "a read-only Go screen inside TUIBBS can read it later". So "TUI Panel" may equally mean a TUIBBS panel, #409's future design, or something else. V5 cites the TUIBBS decision in BC-09 but not in D9. Treat D9 as an open question with at least three candidates, not a probable answer.
6. **BC-17 and RT-F16 are weak.** V5 proposes that the #409 body say v2 reuses "the sweep's output or the observer's snapshot". The snapshot's 15 required row keys contain no command, objective, branch, changed paths or issue/PR (re-read), so for v2's catalogue the sweep (whose model holds `command`, `last_activity`, `changed_paths` but does not emit them; SC-1) remains the closer source. V4's proposal (a) is better than V5 gives it credit for, for v2.
7. **Two small errors.** X-019: "All four earlier reviews ... were wrong about one fact" overgeneralises (V2 and V3 contain no explicit "no observer exists" statement). X-020: "Drop W-3 from the #409 body" is a mislabel; W-3 is V1's proposed wording, not text in the body (SC-19).
8. **V5 adds to the edit collision it criticises.** 23 body corrections against a scratch file that is already stale (the live body now has a banner and 30,756 bytes; the sibling branch it names is gone), and BC-13, BC-16 and BC-17 rewrite the same sentences V1 W-1/W-7 and V4's #409 edit 2 rewrite (S-006, A-010, A-011). The owner needs one merged edit set, not a fifth.
9. **It does not touch the watcher, trust, or v2 questions** and says "not affected". That is honest but means V5 gives no help on #450, Coder#477's trust criteria or #423's gate; "keep both" is only a verdict about observer versus sweep.
10. **Does "keep both programs" survive V1, V3 and V4? Mostly, with one real limit.** (a) *One data source (V1, V4).* For classification it survives (the observer classifies nothing, so two classifiers cannot disagree). For raw Orca reads it does not fully survive: two parsers and two allow-lists for `worker-list` (and a split on `check`) is genuine duplication, and V5's reasons for not sharing code (sweep `models.py` stdlib-only, exact-token owner-signed allow-list, PR #539 in review) are properties of an unmerged design, not laws. The defensible reading is "keep both programs now, share facts through one written contract, and revisit a shared parser after #539 merges"; "never share" is not defensible. (b) *One trust design (V3, V4).* It survives because the observer has no trust surface at all (no network, no credential, three Orca argv shapes); the trust debate is entirely about #339 grants, #477 and the v2 relay, where V4's "grant vocabulary" is the better-supported position. (c) *A fleet view (V1, V3, V4).* For one host's Orca ledger the observer already is the fleet view (all Runs, paged to 500 rows, PARTIAL beyond); multi-host stays gated (R4) and needs #477, so V3 and V4's cross-host points stand. The deepest limit: the reviewer's four asks need sweep-side facts, so the observer will eventually read sweep data; "keep separate now, optional panel later" (D1 a then b) is right only if the owner accepts that the "later" step needs the sweep to persist its JSON.

## 6. Shared assumptions (A-001 to A-016)

Each line states the verdict on the assumption as the diff analysis words it.

- **A-001 (the sweep JSON v1 is the right integration point for the observer, v2 and any #477 status route): QUALIFY.** False for the observer's Orca ledger (it reads Orca and has its own snapshot, `snapshot.schema.json`); plausible for v2, because the sweep model holds `command`, `last_activity`, `changed_paths` that the observer snapshot lacks (15 row keys, re-read); unsuitable as it stands for a #477 status route because each `sweep run` writes a report (`commands.py:270-277`).
- **A-002 (one host, one Run, one checkout is the unit; a fleet is a merge of per-Run reports): QUALIFY.** Wrong for the Orca ledger: the observer reads all Runs, paged (`WORKER_LIST_TEMPLATE` with `--limit 100`, `MAX_CALLS_PER_CYCLE = 5`, `--run` optional); right for the sweep's disk evidence (git, contracts, transcripts are per root); one host is the unit for both programs, so multi-host is still gated (R4).
- **A-003 (the owner wants the observer built and its direction will be chosen; no code exists): REJECT.** The observer is built and merged (PRs #503, #514; `main.py:572,574`) on the owner's staged decision of 2026-11-08 and "Proceed with building the first release" of 2026-11-11; what is missing is the dated selection record (AC-S) and the later releases (R3, R4), which have no package.
- **A-004 (PR #539 merges essentially unchanged; adding JSON keys later is cheap): QUALIFY.** Two review threads are unresolved (`probes.py:1052`, `probes.py:1211`, my GraphQL read), one a git-config security item that must be fixed or recorded as accepted, so #539 will change; adding sweep keys is not free because a golden test pins the key set (diff analysis SC-1), while the observer's schema is additive-only by its own rule.
- **A-005 (Phase 2 and #534 will exist and absorb everything deferred): REJECT.** The only Phase 2 artifact is a reading list, #534 is stale ("Nothing is committed or pushed yet", V1 C-10), and the observer's own R3 and R4 have no package or issue (V5 S10); deferred items have no owner or date, so they should be treated as unowned risk until someone is named.
- **A-006 (the sweep is read-only in effect and safe to run on demand for other consumers): REJECT.** Each run publishes a dated report (`commands.py:270-277`), `_worktree_paths` accepts every path from `worktree ps` (`probes.py:1203-1214`) and `git status` runs with the inherited environment (`probes.py:309`); the Codex Security thread is open. For the observer "read-only" holds only toward Orca: it writes a snapshot and an export under the user state directory (mode 0600) and permits one `check` template.
- **A-007 (a single owner and trust domain; one OS user per sweep; one human answering consistently): QUALIFY.** It is an accurate description of what is built but should be written down as a limit, not left as a silent premise. The pass record names one owner, one terminal and one Run, and Q-1 FAIL means the questions panel works only in the terminal that holds the Run; nothing built supports a second owner, so say so in the docs rather than leave it implied.
- **A-008 (a coordinator is active and runs WC-16, so a Run exists): QUALIFY.** True for the sweep (a Run is required: `worker-list --run`, else exit 2 per V2 W-04); false for the observer's worker ledger (no bound Run needed; `--run` optional); person-started and watcher-started sessions are invisible to both programs (the observer reads Dispatch-based `worker-list`; the sweep reads transcripts only if it can join them, `classify.py:748-767`).
- **A-009 (Orca CLI output shapes and version are the same on every host): QUALIFY.** Both programs' captures saw the same `worker-list` page object (V5 O-01), which is a small mitigation; the observer records no Orca version while the sweep locks 1.4.245, and `worker-show` names for live workers are uncaptured; treat as an open risk with one named capture (V5 D3), not as an assumption to carry silently.
- **A-010 (the text under review is stable): REJECT.** The live #409 body already changed (30,756 bytes now versus 30,030 in the scratch file; updatedAt 2026-11-13T16:57:51Z; correction banner), the sibling branch `409-Phase2` is gone, and #539 may be rebased; pin every citation to a commit sha or an updatedAt stamp.
- **A-011 (edit sets can be applied independently; questions answered in any order): REJECT.** V1 W-7, V4's #409 edit 2 and V5 BC-17 rewrite the same decision-8 sentence three ways (X-002), and several questions are duplicated (S-007); one coordinated edit pass per issue is needed.
- **A-012 (the owner wants BOTH programs kept and aligned): QUALIFY.** Nobody has asked the owner: only V5 states it (Q2). The owner's records are consistent with it (the observer was chosen as a standalone Python plus Rich program with a snapshot for a later TUIBBS reader; the owner asked #477 and #409 to "align", not merge), but that is inference, so it stays a question with a recommendation, not a fact.
- **A-013 (Orca CLI read verbs, polled, are the only practical read source): QUALIFY.** `orca-ide orchestration --help` on this host lists no watch, stream, event, subscribe, follow or tail verb, so for the CLI surface polling is all there is; but the Orca server itself speaks over a local WebSocket (V3 cites `runtime.mjs` `ws://127.0.0.1:6768`) and #409 L66 lists event replay as unverified, and no report examined that API, so a push source is unexamined, not excluded.
- **A-014 (there is one JSON v1 that all consumers mean): REJECT.** Two exist and cannot parse each other: observer `schemaVersion`, camelCase, `"UNKNOWN"` strings, rows keyed `dispatchId`; sweep `schema_version`, snake_case, `null`, rows keyed `row_id` (`orca:<dispatch>`, `classify.py:891`); name them "observe snapshot v1" and "sweep report JSON v1".
- **A-015 (the owner's decisions are recorded where a reviewer can read them): REJECT.** Master holds only `owner-decisions-20261111.md`; the 2026-11-08 and 2026-11-12 records, the pass record and the live-test guide are untracked or unpushed in the sibling worktree (my `ls` and `git status` of it); the dated selection record does not exist anywhere.
- **A-016 (reading the issue text and the branch under review finds every relevant implementation): REJECT.** The observer package was in the review checkout's own tree and history; add a mandatory step to future alignment reviews: search the whole of `origin/master` for the feature name (`git grep`, codegraph) before writing "no code exists" or "silent".

## 8. Course of action for the owner

Rules I used: nothing is posted by an agent without the owner; "Reversible" means the step can be undone by editing text or reverting one small commit; "Depends on" names what must happen first. The owner decisions that gate most of the list, in the order to ask them (one at a time): O1 = does "TUI Panel" in Coder#477 mean `superclaude observe`, a TUIBBS panel, or #409's future design; O2 = keep both programs with one written contract, add an optional observer panel later, or merge (my recommendation: keep both, contract now, panel later); O3 = scope of the questions-read permission (named Run, any Run the terminal holds, or off); O4 = the watcher's claim level (`--pipeline none` or the default); O5 = is Coder#477 still wanted for the watcher (option A or B/C).

### 8.1 #409 (the TUI Dashboard issue)

1. Correct the facts that do not depend on any owner choice: L16 "No observer code exists", L22 table row and the missing observer rows, L33, L64, L95, L102, L195, L199, L201, the missing Related PRs #503 and #514, and the banner wording about the deleted `409-Phase2` branch (V5 BC-02, 04, 05, 07, 11, 14, 15, 19 to 23). Also keep V1's sweep-side fixes: six threads not five (W-6), `policy` is a JSON key (W-2), the boundary sentence labelled "proposed" (W-7). Depends on: nothing. Reversible: yes (issue edit history). One editor, one pass, against the live body (it moved since the scratch copy).
2. Hold the design-dependent edits (BC-08 on "no second collector", BC-13 diagram, BC-16 decision 1, BC-17 decision 8) until O2 is answered; then write them once. Depends on: O2. Reversible: yes.
3. The owner writes the dated direction-selection record (AC-S) or amends acceptance criterion 1 (BC-18). Depends on: the owner. Reversible: yes. Agents may cite it but not write it.
4. Decision 6: replace "Decide one design, not two" with "one grant vocabulary and one audit record" only if the owner accepts V4's position (X-005). Depends on: owner. Reversible: yes.

### 8.2 `superclaude observe` (built)

1. No code change now. Open one small PR from a fresh branch off `origin/master` that flips `QUESTION_GATE_PASSED` only after O3 is answered, using `/sc:pr-submit --monitor 3`. Depends on: O3 and the owner record being committed (it exists only in the sibling worktree). Reversible: yes (flip it back).
2. Add one routing row to `HOW-WE-WORK.md` and one line in the `sc-recommend` table (V5 D8), in its own PR. Depends on: nothing; the owner may decline. Reversible: yes.
3. Record the Orca version in the next golden capture and keep the "no watcher or sweep panel" line until the watcher's live test (#450 FU-1) is done. Depends on: FU-1 for the second part. Reversible: yes.

### 8.3 Sweep and #339 (PR #539 and follow-ups)

1. Settle the two open #539 threads before anything else touches the PR: restrict git-status targets and disable executable git config, or record the risk as accepted (X-007). Depends on: owner ruling. Reversible: yes (small commit).
2. Do not widen #539 for observer alignment. Log in #534: design 7.5 "because #409 is unmerged" (`orchestrator-mode.md:603`), the cursor text at `:559` and section 17 item 5, and `report.py:199,534` wording; apply after #539 merges. Depends on: #539 merge. Reversible: yes.
3. If the owner picks O2 option (b) or wants a Coder#477 status route, add a persisted `<stem>.json` next to each report as a Phase 1 follow-up (additive, same atomic publish as the report). Depends on: O2 (and #539 merged). Reversible: yes. If not, do not build it.
4. The chat report every 3 to 5 minutes: keep the sweep's counts-and-path reply as the WC-16 output; the owner says on #339 whether `observe --once` text is also wanted (V5 D2). Depends on: O2. Reversible: yes.
5. One shared capture of live Orca facts (live and blocked worker names, `projection.attention`, message non-consumption for a non-owner, Orca's event surface) recorded under #534 item 2 and cited by both programs (V5 D3, A-013). Depends on: owner read permission under the sweep allow-list. Reversible: it is a read-only capture.

### 8.4 #450 (watcher rollout)

1. Decide the claim level (O4) before the first live test; if `--pipeline none`, change `build_claim_prompt`, the argv pin and the property test; if the default, record the OB-H1/H2/H3 gate (V2 W-01, W-02). Depends on: O4. Reversible: yes (one pattern change).
2. Add the FU-1 capture items: session identity fields, before and after `run-list`, Orca Claude command, tab-map entry, permission mode (V2 W-03, W-04, W-08, W-09); the same capture feeds 8.3 item 5. Depends on: nothing. Reversible: yes (text).
3. Strike item 4 (already done by #485) and fix the test count (V2 W-12). Depends on: nothing. Reversible: yes.
4. Rewrite V2 W-10 to "no watcher panel in the existing observer until FU-1". Depends on: FU-1. Reversible: yes.

### 8.5 Coder#477

1. Do not post anything before O1 and O5 are answered. Depends on: the owner. Reversible: n/a.
2. The wrong paths (V3 Edit 2) are independent of the owner's answers: five of eight omit `image/`. Fix them first. Depends on: nothing. Reversible: yes.
3. After O5: apply V3 Edit 1 (status), Edits 6 and 7 (read and send separated; testable trust criteria), Edit 9 (lifecycle), Edits 10 to 12. Rewrite Edit 5 so the route serves a named payload (persisted sweep JSON or observer snapshot), not "runs the sweep" (X-004), and rewrite Edit 8 to describe the real launch (including the second agent unless O4 removes it). Depends on: O5, O4, and for Edit 5 the 8.3 item 3 decision. Reversible: yes (issue text).

## 2. Updated assessment of V2..V5 after their Round 1

**V2 (watcher): up.** Its Round 1 concessions are honest and narrow the claim correctly: W-04 "no Run or Dispatch record" is inference from help text and a host listing (R1-V2 concession 3), and W-01 is a contradiction with design D2, not proof of what the owner wants (concession 7). I keep X-001 at high confidence for the code-level fact only (default `worktree`, `launch.py:33`, `issue-watcher.md:44`). New from R1-V5 item 10: a watcher-launched session is also invisible to the observer, because `agent launch` is not an orchestration verb and the observer reads `worker-list` only. That makes V2's Run-less blind spot a two-program problem and raises its weight on A-002 and A-008. It rests on V2's UNVERIFIED "no Run record", so I treat it as likely, not shown.

**V3 (Coder#477): up on content, down on Edit 5.** R1-V3 strength 3 resolves two of V3's own unknowns in its favour: the Coder image pins Claude Code 2.1.289 and launches it with gateway auth (`ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` from `LITELLM_API_KEY`, `llm-env.tf:37`; claimed by R1-V3, not opened by me), so Claude Code cannot be the cross-workspace channel. R1-V3 strength 10 gives the sweep/observer data a real far-side consumer (TUIBBS on a separate SSH host). That is the strongest reason to schedule JSON persistence (section 3, A-001 below), and it changed my "defer indefinitely" to "schedule as Phase 2 item 1". V3's concessions 1 and 2 (Edit 5 wrong, observer missed) are accepted; I add that Edit 5 is also blocked by NFR-001 as pinned (response 5), not only by the "no write" wording.

**V4 (v2): up on F1, F7, F14; converged with V1 on X-002 and X-007.** R1-V4 concessions 2 and 4 withdraw the circular C8 proof and the "confines every path" claim; concession 8 downgrades "Collision Lens" to an unbuilt design image; concession 10 accepts that `sweep run` writes a report and runs git with the inherited env. R1-V4 weakness 9 is right that V5 BC-17 ("or the observer's snapshot") widens v2's input too far: the snapshot's 15 row keys have no `command`, `changed_paths` or branch, so for a catalogue the sweep's model is the closer source. I now side with V4 against V5 on BC-17.

**V5 (built observer): still the decisive report, but weaker than it presents.** Accepted: the observer exists; two readers, two schemas, two allow-lists; three vocabularies. R1-V5 concessions 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10 retract or narrow six of V5's own claims: "nothing needs code today" is too strong, D9 ("TUI Panel") is incomplete because TUIBBS is a third candidate, BC-17 is weak, X-019 and X-020 are slips, and "never share" is not defensible (shared parser after #539 merges). What changed my mind about V5: nothing against D1(a). What I now doubt is its O-05 evidence: the "coordinator's own Run showed 0 pending after its reads" measurement (`.dev/issues/409/owner-decisions-20261111.md` line 15 on `origin/master`, re-verified) is not evidence of non-consumption (section 5, item 2). That lowers my confidence in V5's claim that the sweep's caution at `report.py:534` is "stronger than the evidence", and I now rate X-016 as "unproven both ways" rather than a V5 win.

## 5. Not yet examined by the debate

Each item gives the state, guard or boundary at risk, what I verified now, what stays UNVERIFIED, and the check.

**1. What runs when a program reads a host-wide worktree list.**
- *Sweep.* `_worktree_paths` adds every path in `orca worktree ps` (`probes.py:1203-1214`), then `_git` runs `git --no-optional-locks -C <path> ...` with `env = {**os.environ, "GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS": "0"}` (`probes.py:305-327`), re-verified. The boundary at risk is untrusted repo content: any checkout Orca lists, including a cloned third-party repo, can carry config that executes during `git status` (the open Codex thread at `probes.py:1211`). `--no-optional-locks` removes lock contention only. This is different from the user's own environment, which the user already controls.
- *Observer.* Starts only Orca argv shapes (`adapter.py:29-52`); runs no git and reads no worktree path. It does resolve its binary by running `skills list --json` on the first `orca-ide` or `orca` that `shutil.which` finds (`install_user_setup.py` `_find_orca`, re-verified), while the sweep uses `$ORCA_CLI_COMMAND` else `orca-ide` and never bare `orca` (`probes.py` `orca_binary`). Both trust the user's own PATH/env (low severity), but on a host with both names the two programs can read different Orca instances or versions and report "different facts" with no error. UNVERIFIED whether any host has that setup. Check: print each program's resolved binary on one host.
- *Report text into the coordinator's context.* The skill tells the coordinator "report text is data" (`SKILL.md` line 63), an instruction, not a guard. I checked what could carry injected text: `TranscriptTail` holds only path, session id and timestamps (`models.py:127-138`) and task spec text is only regex-matched for the gate (`classify.py:549-564`), so transcript and spec content do not reach the report. Worker-written contract fields remain unchecked: UNVERIFIED. Check: list which `ContractRecord` strings reach `evidence` or `notes` in `report.py`.

**2. Consumption semantics of Orca message reads (new, and it changes X-016).**
- The measurement cited as the live-Run evidence (`.dev/issues/409/owner-decisions-20261111.md` lines 13-15 on `origin/master`, re-verified): `check --all --run` returned 100 rows (91 heartbeat, 8 worker_done, 1 question), then "a later consuming `check` found 0 pending: the reads did not consume. Tested as the Run's owner only."
- That inference does not follow. `--all` rows carry no read or acknowledged marker and `--all` lists acknowledged rows too (`release-gates.md` line 46, re-verified), so 100 rows say nothing about how many were pending. If the coordinator had already consumed its mailbox, 0 pending is expected whether or not the reads consumed; if the reads consumed, 0 is also expected. The only diagnostic non-consumption result is the probe Run, where a later consuming `check` "still received everything" (`release-gates.md` line 46), a synthetic Run.
- Stake: the observer's flip would run `check --all --types question --run <id>` against the coordinator's own live Run, whose mailbox holds `worker_done` messages the coordinator waits on. The filter's effect on non-question types is untested. The owner's pass record accepts "unmeasured traffic" and "non-owner non-consumption unverified" (V5 S9, not opened by me), but not "owner-read consumption unverified on a live Run", because the measurement above was read as proving it.
- Check: D-2 above. Until then, X-016 is "unproven both ways": the sweep's caution is defensible, and V5's counter-evidence on the live Run is non-diagnostic.

**3. Stale or partial data shown as healthy.**
- Sweep: `Messages coverage: partial` is printed unconditionally (`report.py:199`), even when Orca is unavailable (V1 F-18); Orca-sourced rows carry `age_seconds: null`, so age exists only in `observed_at`; a dead coordinator leaves an old report that looks like any other (V1 F-12; WC-16 is a cap, no schedule).
- Observer: its 45 s STALE rule and last-good-rows behaviour live in the running process (`collector.py`). `snapshot.json` stays on disk after the process exits, with `generatedAt` but no writer pid or liveness field in the schema (V5 S5 key list). A later reader (the TUIBBS screen the schema was designed for) sees `source.status` as last written, which may read OK, unless it applies its own age rule. Check: confirm the key list in `snapshot.schema.json` has no writer-liveness field.

**4. Two writers of one file.**
- Observer: fixed name `snapshot.json` (`export.py:26, 255-257`) rewritten each live cycle by `write_atomic` (`mkstemp` + `os.replace`, mode 0600): never torn, but last writer wins. Two live observers (two terminals, or `--run A` and the all-Runs view) overwrite each other, and the file carries only the winner's scope. `commands.py:84-85` warns about doubled Orca load but not about this.
- Sweep: unique dated names, `os.link` never overwrites (`commands.py:182-190`); safe today. A sidecar `.json` would introduce exactly this pairing problem (response 5).

**5. Merge and rebase order (all checked now against file lists).**
1. **#541** changes one file, `tests/core/test_claude_md_worktree_rule.py`; mergeStateStatus CLEAN, all six listed checks SUCCESS. It fixes a test red on master since #532.
2. **#539** does not touch that file (0 matches in its 1003-file diff), so it needs no rebase, only a CI re-run after #541; resolve the two threads first. Its diff touches `src/superclaude/cli/main.py` (adds the sweep registration), `src/superclaude/core/HOW-WE-WORK.md` and its plugin mirror, `tests/core/test_how_we_work_routing_table.py`, `WORKER-CONTRACT.md`, `.gitignore`, `output-styles/pragmatic.md`.
3. **Observer routing row (V5 D8)** edits `HOW-WE-WORK.md` and the byte-for-byte mirror under `plugins/.../sc-recommend/refs/` (project rule) and likely the routing-table test: all touched by #539, so it must wait until #539 merges.
4. **Observer guard flip** touches only `observe/commands.py` and `tests/observe` (V5: 3 files, 23 insertions, 12 deletions; SC-14), so no overlap with #539. It needs a fresh branch off master (sibling history predates the squash; `origin/master` is not an ancestor), cut after #541 merges so its CI does not inherit the red test. Its constant carries the comment `# owner record: .dev/issues/409/owner-pass-record-20261112.md` (SC-14), but that file exists only in the sibling worktree (SC-18); the flip PR must commit the record or it cites a path that does not exist on master. The "owner records are written by the owner, cited by agents" rule (`release-gates.md` line 22, per V5) means the owner, not an agent, must supply it.
5. **Relationship note** after #539 merges (it edits the sweep design in `docs/designs/orchestrator-mode.md`, which #539 also owns). **#534 text** edits can go any time.

**6. Retention and growth.** The sweep adds one dated file per wake (WC-16 cap: at most one per 5 minutes per coordinator, so up to 288 a day), gitignored (`.gitignore` hunk in #539), no retention (person deletes by hand, V1 SP-22); the observer's `e` key adds `observe-<stamp>.txt` records beside its snapshot. Low severity, but it is the one place a sidecar `.json` would double the count.

**7. Not examined here:** whether a human can use the observer's questions panel in the same terminal that already holds a Claude coordinator session (V5 D5 UNVERIFIED); the sibling worktree's owner records (not opened); live Orca; any test run.

Tested as the Run's owner only". Zero pending after the reads is expected whether or not the reads consumed, if the coordinator had already drained its mailbox. X-016 is "unproven both ways". This does not touch the watcher, which reads no Orca messages.
- *Binary resolution differs between observer and sweep (R2-V1 section 5 item 1).* **Accept and narrow it.** The watcher uses the same rule as the observer: `_ORCA_CLIS = ("orca-ide", "orca")` (`install_user_setup.py:36`, imported at `launch.py:22`), a probe of `skills list --json` for the `orca-cli` topic, and an absolute-path requirement (`launch.py:193-203`). Only the sweep differs (`$ORCA_CLI_COMMAND` else `orca-ide`, `probes.py:201-202`). So the "two programs read different Orca instances" risk is a sweep-versus-everyone-else risk, and three programs now resolve Orca in two ways.

- *Join key `dispatchId` = `row_id` minus `orca:` is unproven (D-5).* **Accept.** Not a watcher matter; the watcher record has no `dispatchId` at all.

**V5: still the best report for the observer, now with a second limit.** (1) It is silent on the watcher. A program-wide "one list of read-only Orca verbs" (D4) therefore has to be a per-caller list: observer = 3 shapes, sweep = 10 templates, watcher = `skills list --json` plus `agent launch` (`launch.py:200,103-116`). (3) The A-008 effect ("the observer needs no bound Run") is true only for Workers. Everything else in R1-V5 stands.

## 5. Not yet examined: state, guard and boundary risks of the watcher and its launch path

Each item gives the state or boundary at risk, what I verified now, what stays UNVERIFIED and the check. Items 1, 4 and 6 are new findings; 2, 3 and 5 sharpen R1 items. To my reading none of these is in the diff analysis (R12, R13, R14 are in the watcher's own runbook, but not tied to the debate's questions).

### 5.1 What the launched session may do (permission boundary)

- **What the design claims.** The launch argv has 12 elements and no permission flag, pinned by a test (T10, `issue-watcher.md:667`; `launch.py:95-116`). Runbook 14.2 (`issue-watcher-setup.md:625-630`) concludes "the session runs in its default, interactive mode, which keeps a human between the session and any write", and names the risk: the claim reads the issue title, body and comments, text "anyone with comment access can write" (R13, R18). NG-4 adds no session constraint beyond the deployment gate (`issue-watcher.md:70`).
- **Why that conclusion is not established (new).** The argv decides nothing about the mode; the Orca Claude command does. The command that makes a session identifiable and governed is the ccsession wrapper (`claim.md:125`: "Orca's Claude command must be the ccsession-orca wrapper"; V2 W-09). `ccsession-orca` ends in `exec "$CCSESSION" ... --claude-args` (`ccsession-orca:186,189`), and `ccsession` defaults to `--dangerously-skip-permissions --allowedTools=Grep` unless `CCSESSION_PERMISSIONS=default` is set (`ccsession:135-141`, re-read). So the two things the debate wants for the watcher session, an identity the sweep can join (W-03, W-09) and a human gate before writes (14.2), pull in opposite directions unless the host sets `CCSESSION_PERMISSIONS=default`. If it is unset, a session that reads untrusted issue text runs with no permission prompts (`--dangerously-skip-permissions`).
- **What bounds it either way.** The prompt is only `/sc:issue --claim <int>` (D3, `launch.py:84-92`); the approval gate (T2-T4: allow-listed human approver, body and non-own comments older than the approval, `issue-watcher.md:659-661`) bounds who can put text in front of the session, but text arriving after the re-read still reaches it (R13).
- **UNVERIFIED.** The mode the first session actually runs in on a Coder workspace and the host's `CCSESSION_PERMISSIONS`. FU-6 asks the first half; the second half is not in the runbook.
- **Check.** D-C above. **Action:** make FU-1 record `CCSESSION_PERMISSIONS` and the session's real mode, and decide explicitly (owner) whether the watcher host sets `default`; put the decision beside Q1.

### 5.2 Who owns the launched session

- The watcher is a plain process, never an Orca Worker (`issue-watcher.md:68`), so no coordinator, Run or WC-16 loop owns the first session; WC-13 does not apply to it. It starts in one configured worktree via `--worktree path:<wt>` with `cwd` set there (`launch.py:95-116,358-370`), up to the day cap of 6 per 24 h (`issue-watcher.md:501`), and nothing closes or reaps those sessions. Orca's own rule for worktrees ("for a new checkout with its agent, use `orca worktree create --agent`", `agent launch --help`, re-read) gives the worktree's creator ownership of its agent; the runbook does not say who that person is (V2 W-07).
- At the default claim level a second agent is started by the claim script, in a worktree that script created or reused (`claim.md:99-120`). That agent is governed by the launch-level hook; the first session is not (`sc-issue-launch-level-hook.md:23`: only claim-script-launched sessions are governed; V2 W-08).
- **UNVERIFIED:** whether any later Phase 2 coordinator would adopt watcher sessions. **Action:** the owner names the person who owns the watcher worktree's agent (runbook step 1.3a) and states that watcher sessions are never coordinator-owned (this is V2 Q2).

### 5.3 What record survives

| Place | What stays | What is missing |
|---|---|---|
| Watcher state dir | `dispatch.json` v1 (state, `operation_id`, `launch_cwd`, `launch_login`, times; mode 0600; `issue-watcher.md:404-440,510`) and `audit.jsonl` (host and login on every line, T7) | any session id, pane key, terminal handle or permission mode (`status_report` returns typed scalars only, `cycle.py:1326-1335`) |
| GitHub | assignment, accuracy-check comment, claim comment, each ending in a marker (`claim.md:87-91`); the watcher's claim detection reads the marker (T-10/T-11/T-12, `issue-watcher.md:488-490`) | nothing ties the comment to the Orca operation |
| Watcher worktree | `.dev/issues/<N>/` files (`issue.md:77`), untracked by rule (`claim.md:93`: "Do not stage or commit `.dev/issues/`") | lost if the worktree is removed; the sweep shows the worktree as uncommitted |
| Issue worktree (default level only) | copy of `.dev/issues/<N>/`, `worktree.json` launch entry, `~/.claude/state/launch-level/` records, an L2 pre-push hook (`claim.md:99-120`) | n/a |
| Claude side | transcript; a ccsession tab-map entry only if the Orca command is `ccsession-orca` (V2 W-09) | nothing joins it to the operation id |

**Action:** FU-1 records the join fields (V2 edit 3); until then no consumer should assume a watcher session can be traced back to its dispatch entry.

### 5.4 Restart and double-launch

- **At-most-once is per issue, per intact single store.** `pending` is saved durably before the spawn (`issue-watcher.md:524-534`). A crash after the spawn leaves `pending`, which the next cycle's sweep marks `abandoned(pending_orphan)` and never relaunches (T-8, C3/C4): a session may be running while the record says abandoned. If the claim comment was posted, the marker later promotes the entry to `claimed` (T-10).
- **A lost reply is `unknown`, not a retry.** Timeout at 120 s, non-zero exit, non-JSON or no id all give `unknown` (`launch.py:326-349`); the entry leaves only through a marker or `reset N --forfeit-guarantee` (T-13, `issue-watcher.md:471-472,491`). Orca's help is explicit: "re-issue the same command from the same directory on the same host with --retry-launch <operationId>. Never retry as a fresh launch" (re-read). `status --json` prints that retry argv only for entries that have an `operation_id` (`cycle.py:1308`).
- **New failure mode from the assumed shape.** `OPERATION_ID_PATHS` (`launch.py:39-45`) is a guess pinned by four `_assumed_shape` fixtures. If the real success reply carries the id elsewhere, every correct launch classifies `unknown` / `no_operation_id`, has no id for a retry command, counts toward the day cap and stays `unknown` until a marker appears. That is a real outcome of running FU-1 on an unverified shape, not a theoretical one, and `reset --forfeit-guarantee` would then permit a second launch of an issue whose first session is alive.
- **Documented residual risks:** R11 restored backup, R12 two stores on one login ("one enabled workspace per login"), R14 a hand claim during a launch (`issue-watcher-setup.md:641-644`). None has a mechanical guard.
- **The claim itself is mostly safe against a second agent (R1 concern narrowed, verified).** `cmd_launch` returns `already_launched` when the issue worktree's `worktree.json` record or its mirror holds a `launch` entry (`orca_worktree.py:1689-1707`; `claim.md:120`), and re-claiming is idempotent (`claim.md:43`, comments edited via markers `:87-92`). Residual: if `issue-<N>` or the untracked `.dev/issues/` data is removed, a re-claim can start a new agent. A claim whose Wave 8 is refused (`W-ENFORCEMENT-ABSENT`, `claim.md:124-126`) still posts its comments and still leaves the marker, so the watcher records `claimed` while no second agent exists (V2 W-15; aligned but opaque).
- **Action:** FU-1 must show the real operation-id path before the cron entry is enabled (already the gate); the runbook should say that `reset --forfeit-guarantee` on an `unknown` entry requires first checking Orca for a live session in the configured worktree.

### 5.5 The two open decisions, as risks

| Decision | If chosen | State or guard at risk | Needs |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 claim level | `--pipeline none` | Claim ends after the comments (`issue.md:88`): no worktree, no second agent, ship gate not triggered. The first session sits idle at a prompt in the shared worktree until a person opens `next-steps.md` (`issue.md:80`). Code change: `PROMPT_PATTERN` (`launch.py:33`), `build_claim_prompt` (`:84-92`), the 12-element pin, `retry_argv`, NFR-001 test, runbook strings | owner answer; small PR |
| Q1 claim level | default `worktree` | Up to 6 new worktrees and governed agents a day; claim ship gate (`claim.md:131`, hook doc `planning_status`) forbids a governed claim on a real issue until OB-H1 item 1, OB-H2, OB-H3 pass | owner answer and the #394 gate |
| Q2 / Coder#477 | keep option A (no listener) | The one guarantee stays local (per login, per store). #477 rescopes to read-only registration or closes; nothing in IronClaude changes | owner answer; V3 Edit 1 |
| Q2 / Coder#477 | revisit B or C | Creates a write path from outside the workspace into a session start; needs the trust vocabulary (V4 F9) and a credential rule that bans the Orca pairing offer (V3 F11) before any code; the watcher's argv/D3 pin then becomes the listener's contract | owner answer; #339 grant decision |

The decisions interact: with `--pipeline none` and option A the watcher is a notifier that opens a session for a person, which is exactly the "ordinary top-level session" #409 decision 9 and the design describe; with the default level and option B/C it becomes a remote agent-spawner and every V3/V4 trust finding applies at once.

### 5.6 What the live FU-1 test writes

- **The capture sheet (`issue-watcher-setup.md:595-611`) is read-only toward GitHub:** `gh issue list`, `gh api --method GET`, `gh api user --include` and GraphQL queries from constants with no mutation (NG-2, `issue-watcher.md:70`).
- **The single owner-run test launch is not read-only** (`setup:612,621`: launched by hand with Orca while `enabled` is false; do not run a real `once`). It starts `/sc:issue --claim <test issue>`, which writes: (1) `gh issue edit <N> --add-assignee @me` (`claim.md:48`); (2) the ClickUp script `clickup_claim.py` when `CLICKUP_API_TOKEN` and `CLICKUP_WORKSPACE_ID` are set (`issue.md:88`; the exact ClickUp write is in the script, not read by me: UNVERIFIED); (3) the accuracy-check comment and the claim comment, each posted or edited by marker (`claim.md:87-91`); (4) `.dev/issues/<N>/` files in the watcher worktree. The claim documents that "the assignment and the comments cannot be undone" by the claim itself (`claim.md:101`); a person can delete comments and reassign by hand; ClickUp reversal is unknown.
- **At the default level it also** creates or reuses worktree `issue-<N>`, copies `.dev/issues/<N>/` into it, installs the L2 pre-push hook, writes launch-level records under `~/.claude/state/launch-level/`, and starts a governed agent (`claim.md:99-120`), which the ship gate forbids on a real issue; the test issue is a "test issue" but the gate text does not exempt it (V2 W-02).
- **Side effect on the watcher later (check the ordering).** The hand launch leaves no `dispatch.json` entry. If the test issue later carries the trigger and approval labels in Backlog, T-11 treats a marker "after the current approval" as `claimed` with no launch, but a marker older than the approval does not (`issue-watcher.md:489-490`). So the order "claim first, then label" can make the enabled watcher claim the test issue again. Close or unlabel the test issue after the test.
- **Action:** the FU-1 record lists these writes and the test issue, and the person running it confirms ClickUp env is unset or the ClickUp effect is acceptable.

### 5.7 Items I could not verify

The host's `CCSESSION_PERMISSIONS`; the actual mode of a launched session; Orca's real launch reply; whether `agent launch` makes a Run; the exact ClickUp write; the real Orca behaviour of `--retry-launch`; whether the hook `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh` fires on the claim script's own `orca worktree create` (the claim script runs it inside `uv run`, not as a direct Bash command; I did not read the hook).

### Variant 3 Advocate rebuttal

(Source file: round2-rebuttal-V3.md; full text below)

# Round 2 rebuttal for variant 3 (Coder#477 listener review)

## 1. Response to each criticism raised against V3

R2-V2 D-D narrows the effect: a re-claim is mostly idempotent (`already_launched`), so the extra agent appears once per issue, not per cycle. That lowers the severity, not the omission. | New, and it matters for Edit 8's live-test line: the shipped Coder image launches every Claude session with permission checks off. `README.md:60-66`: "Permission checks are off for every call the wrapper routes (a deliberate choice, #455): `ccsession` adds `--dangerously-skip-permissions --allowedTools=Grep` to every launch". `ccsession:135-141` sets those flags unless `CCSESSION_PERMISSIONS=default`; a search of `templates/VSCode-AIDev02` finds no `CCSESSION_PERMISSIONS` setting and `CC_CLAUDE_FLAGS` only in that README sentence. ## 2. Updated assessment of V1, V2, V4, V5 and of their Round 2 claims

**V1 (and R2-V1): up on method, still not on the Coder edge.** Accepted from R2-V1: NFR-001 blocks a sidecar JSON (verified, row 1); #541 needs no rebase of #539 (V1 and V2 now agree); the live-Run "0 pending" measurement does not show non-consumption, so X-016 is "unproven both ways" (R2-V1 section 5 item 2, which R2-V2 also accepts; I did not re-derive it, it follows from `--all` listing acknowledged rows). Where I differ: R2-V1 D-1 frames persistence as the only way to give a second consumer the sweep's facts. A third route exists and is already built: the observer's snapshot is atomically written at mode 0600 (SC-17) and carries no message subject, body or handle (V5 S5), so a #477-style status record can start from the snapshot (Orca facts) and treat the sweep's per-Run facts as local-only until Phase 2. R2-V1's source-sharing point (section 5, item 1: two programs resolve the Orca binary differently) is correct but not a #477 matter.

**V2 (and R2-V2): up.** Section 5.1 (the launched session's permission mode) and 5.5 (the decision matrix: `--pipeline none` plus option A is "a notifier that opens a session for a person"; default level plus B or C is "a remote agent-spawner") are the clearest statement of the stake in #477, and I adopt both. 5.4's warning that `reset --forfeit-guarantee` on an `unknown` entry can permit a second launch of an issue whose first session is alive is a state risk any listener inherits. I settle part of 5.1 (row 2 above). I do not agree that W-04 ("no Run record") matters for #477; it matters for the observer.

**V4 (R1 only; its Round 2 is not written): up on F1, F7, F8, F9, F14, F17 and on conceding C8, F17 and F13.** My remaining difference: its #423 edit 3 conditions "all workspaces" on #477, but #477's own design status (unnecessary under option A, `issue-watcher.md:47-49`) means the condition should read "if #477 is kept". That is one clause.

**V5 (R1 only; its Round 2 is not written): still decisive on the observer, but its D9 is a guess and its D1 omits the third party.** R1-V5 concessions 5 and 9 accept both. My finding on D9 is in section 5.2 and it differs from R1-V5's "probably `observe`" only in weight: textual evidence favours the #409 TUI dashboard over TUIBBS, and the requirement set is the same for both. Its concession 4 ("nothing needs code today" is too strong) does not apply to #477: nothing should be built on #477 today.
