## 5. Not yet examined by the debate: Coder#477 and workspace-to-orchestrator routing

Nothing below is posted. Each item gives the state or boundary at risk, what I verified now, what stays UNVERIFIED, and the check.

### 5.1 Is the listener still needed under watcher option A?

- **For the watcher: no.** `issue-watcher.md:28` (out of scope: "Any listener, port, webhook, GitHub Action or service"), `:43` (D1 option A: "No listener, inbound port or address registry"), `:47-49`; the owner's decision is in `seed-brief.md:46`; both files are tracked. The 2026-11-13 owner comment does not reverse it (#477 still has one comment).
- **For the observer on one host: no.** `superclaude observe` reads the local Orca CLI only (SC-15) and is single-host. Remote fleet is its release R4, gated on cross-host ownership joins "Not established" (V5 S10).
- **Possibly, for a cross-workspace read.** That need is real only when R4 or a TUIBBS screen gets a package. Neither has one (V5 S10: no R3 package; TUIBBS is "Background, not deciding", sibling `owner-decisions-20261108.md` item 4).
- **A per-workspace listener cannot solve the one revisit condition most likely to trigger a rethink.** The first of the nine revisit conditions is "stopped workspaces cause excessive waiting" (`issue-watcher.md:60`); the runbook says "a stopped workspace polls nothing (risk R9)" (`issue-watcher-setup.md:559`). A listener runs inside the workspace, so it is equally silent when the workspace is stopped; only the Coder control plane can start a workspace. So options B and C, which add a listener, do not cure it; a poller outside the workspace with Coder API access would (inference from the design text; not tested).
- **Another revisit condition is not met today.** "Account binding becomes available" (same line): #477 AC3 asks that workspace information be "securely associated with the appropriate GitHub account", which is the thing the design says does not yet exist. A registration cannot carry a verified GitHub binding until it does; at most it carries the workspace's own claim of its `gh` login.
- **Verdict.** Do not build a listener for the watcher. Keep #477 only as a narrowed read-only status feature if the owner confirms a cross-workspace reader (section 5.6). Otherwise close it.

### 5.2 What "TUI Panel" means in the owner's 2026-11-13 comment

- **The words.** The comment (2026-11-13T16:20:07Z, ironbelly) reads "Ensure this aligns with Orchestrator and TUI Panel". Titles: #339 is "[Feature]: Orchestrator - To Run and manage ALL sessions within a worktree"; #409 is "[FEATURE] TUI Dashboard to Manage Orchestration at Scale" (both re-read). The comment's two nouns mirror those two titles. The phrase "TUI Panel" appears in no owner-written file in either repo: a search of `/home/coder/workspaces` (excluding JSON) finds it only in this debate's own review files. So the reading "panel" = "dashboard" of #409 is a textual inference, about 75%.
- **TUIBBS is a real but weaker candidate (about 15%).** The owner's 2026-11-08 record item 4: "The owner is also exploring TUIBBS as an internal communication tool and platform for all human staff who manage agent teams; the observer may become a TUIBBS module" and the same file marks it "Background, not deciding for release 1". The sibling TUIBBS review: "TUIBBS runs on a separate SSH host ... Orca and the planned observer run in this Coder development environment", so Orca data must reach that host "by some other channel" (`tuibbs-review/notes-3.md:78-82`); the snapshot schema exists so a read-only Go screen can read it later (V5 S9). If TUIBBS is meant, a cross-workspace read route is not optional; if the #409 dashboard is meant, it is optional until R4.
- **The answer changes the transport, not the requirements.** Both candidates are read-only readers that need identity, freshness and no send path. TUIBBS additionally needs a Coder-to-another-host channel (push or pull). The one fact that settles it is the owner's reply (D-2).
- **A boundary risk either way.** The #409 draft says "Decide whether the observer reads through that registration" (item 5). The built observer has no network or credential path; it should not gain one until the route exists and the owner has decided (V5 D9 and R1-V5 concession 5 agree).

### 5.3 What a registered workspace may expose read-only

Rule: expose the smallest record that lets a reader tell workspaces apart and judge freshness; never expose content.

| Allowed (read-only, typed, with an age) | Why safe | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Workspace id, owner name, workspace name, build status, agent last-connected | Coder already holds these | Coder API fields (docs fetched now) |
| Profile slugs; "has Orca / has IronClaude" flag | Needed to show a Minimal workspace as "no orchestrator" | `image/catalogue/profiles.yaml:14-21` |
| Image hash, Claude Code and Orca versions | Version skew (A-009) | `main.tf:111`; `Dockerfile.coder:110` |
| The observer snapshot v1 (rows keyed by `dispatchId`, counts, `source.status`, `generatedAt`) | Already minimised: questions carry no subject, body or handle (V5 S5); atomic, mode 0600 | `snapshot.schema.json`; SC-17 |
| The watcher's `status --json` counts | Typed scalars only | `cycle.py:1326-1335` (V2 W-10) |

| Never exposed | Why |
|---|---|
| Orca pairing offer, device token, `CODER_AGENT_TOKEN`, `LITELLM_API_KEY`, any standing credential | `#195` PRD: the offer "is a bearer credential with no expiry" and whoever holds it "can control the workspace's Orca, including its terminals"; env keys are in the workspace environment (`runtime.sh:28`) |
| Addresses and ports | They change on restart; resolve at call time through the Coder app URL (5.4) |
| `retry[].argv` from the watcher status | A runnable launch command (V2 W-10) |
| Transcripts, prompts, issue bodies, message bodies, worker-written contract fields | Untrusted content; whether contract strings reach the sweep report is UNVERIFIED (R2-V1 5.1) |
| The sweep's Markdown or JSON, until the git-config thread on #539 is closed and a no-write form exists | `probes.py:309,1203-1214`; NFR-001 pins |

Every read must be non-consuming (D-4) and re-sanitised by the reader (two sanitizers exist, V5 O-12). A record older than a stated limit is shown stale, never current (the observer's own rule is 45 s for its feed; a registry needs its own).

### 5.4 How a monitor would learn workspace identity

- **Do not self-register for identity.** The existing heartbeat is anonymous and best-effort: `collector/app.py:5` ("anonymous (no auth)"), `emit-heartbeat:7-9` (workspace id and owner are positional arguments the workspace chooses), `collector/Dockerfile:11` binds `0.0.0.0:8080`. Anyone who can reach it can claim any id. #477 AC4 forbids that.
- **Take identity and lifecycle from Coder.** The Coder API lists owner, id, name, build status and agent connectivity (docs fetched now: `GET /api/v2/workspaces`, `Coder-Session-Token`). The workspace already authenticates to Coder with its agent token (`startup-git-askpass.sh:96-121`, endpoint `/api/v2/workspaceagents/me/external-auth`), so no new workspace secret is needed for Coder to know who is who. The agent token itself must never be given to a monitor.
- **Reach by name, not by address.** The Orca app has its own origin `orca--<workspace>--<owner>.<access host>` (`modules.tf:75-77`) and is `share = "owner"`, `subdomain = true` (`orca/module.tf:36-38`). A registry that stores "address and port" (AC3) would store values that change and could go stale; the app URL is derived from ids Coder already holds. Whether an owner API token can drive that app non-interactively is UNVERIFIED (D-7).
- **Use the workspace's own `gh` login as a claim, not a proof.** The watcher's identity is the workspace's own `gh` login (`issue-watcher.md:15`). Until Coder-owner-to-GitHub binding exists (revisit condition, `issue-watcher.md:60`), a monitor can record it as "reported by workspace, unverified".
- **Key by workspace id, never by name.** Image names embed owner and workspace name (`main.tf:111`); names can be reused after deletion. A registration keyed by name can route to a different workspace than the one registered.

### 5.5 The listener as a second command channel to bypass-permission sessions

- **Stock state (verified).** Every Claude launch in a stock workspace has permission checks off: `README.md:60-66` (deliberate, #455), `ccsession:135-141`, no override in the template, and the Orca launch goes through the same wrapper (`README.md:51`). Every orchestrator and every watcher-started session is therefore in the bypass class.
- **What a listener can and cannot do to such a session.** (a) Peer messages: the docs say a bypassing receiver holds each message unless the sender also identifies as bypassing, drops it after `dialogExpiry` (default five minutes), and never runs a slash command in it. A non-Claude listener process is not a bypassing sender, so peer messaging is not a usable route (V3 F8, re-read now). Whether a same-user process can assert a class is UNVERIFIED (D-8). (b) `orca agent launch --prompt`: starts a new top-level session in bypass mode with the given prompt; this is the only route that works, and it is exactly what the watcher does (`launch.py:95-116`).
- **Consequence.** A listener that accepts a prompt from outside is a remote-trigger for a permission-free session that holds the workspace's secrets (`LITELLM_API_KEY`, GitHub credentials in the environment) and can write and push. Today the guards sit in the watcher, not in any listener: a fixed prompt pattern, integer issue number (`launch.py:30-37`), allow-listed approver, label and unchanged-since-approval checks (`issue-watcher.md:659-661`, per R2-V2), day cap of 6 (`issue-watcher.md:501`, per R2-V2). A new listener must re-create them or it is weaker than the thing it replaces. The claim also reads untrusted issue text (R13, R18 in the runbook, per R2-V2), so even the watcher-started session needs the approver gate; a listener with a free-text route removes it.
- **An existing channel of the same strength already exists.** The Orca pairing offer is a no-expiry bearer credential that controls the workspace's Orca, terminals included (#195 PRD). The Orca server is loopback-only (`runtime.mjs:285`) and reached through owner-shared Coder apps (`orca/module.tf:36,54`). A new listener is justified only if it adds no new reachable surface and no weaker authentication than those. Hence the acceptance criteria below ban any free-text route and any standing credential in the registry.
- **UNVERIFIED.** What a paired Orca device can do beyond terminals (V3 relied on the PRD text); the host-level `CCSESSION_PERMISSIONS` value on each live workspace (D-5).

### 5.6 Exact proposed rewrite of Coder#477

Recommendation: **narrow** (about 65%), and only after the owner answers D-1 and D-2. If the owner says the observer stays single-host and TUIBBS is not near, **close**. Keep-as-written is the least defensible: its premise (a watcher that sends prompts to a listener) contradicts the built watcher.

Nothing here edits the issue. Paths are relative to the Coder repo root.

**A. NARROW (recommended).**

Title: `Read-only workspace status and identity for the Orchestrator observer (no prompt routing)`

Feature Summary:
> Let a read-only observer outside a workspace (IronClaude's `superclaude observe`, IronbellyOrg/IronClaude#409, and a possible later TUIBBS screen) see which of one owner's Coder workspaces exist, whether each is running, whether it has Orca and IronClaude, and its supervision status record. Nothing can be sent to a workspace through this feature.

Problem Statement:
> The IronClaude issue watcher (IronbellyOrg/IronClaude#331, rollout #450) runs inside each workspace and starts a claim with a local `orca agent launch`. By its design (IronClaude `docs/designs/issue-watcher.md` section 2.1, decision D1 option A, 2026-11-07) it needs no listener, inbound port or address registry, so this issue no longer carries watcher routing. What is still missing: the observer reads one host's Orca only (remote fleet is its release R4, gated on cross-host ownership joins) and cannot see another workspace.

Proposed Solution:
> Workspace identity and running state come from the Coder control plane (workspace id, owner, name, build status, agent last-connected), not from a value the workspace reports about itself. Each workspace also exposes one read-only record: profile slugs, Orca and Claude Code versions, image hash, a last-written time, the observer snapshot (`observe snapshot v1`) and the watcher's status counts. It is reached through an owner-authenticated route (for example the existing owner-shared Coder app mechanism), never an unauthenticated port. No address or port is stored; the route is resolved from the workspace id at read time.

Acceptance Criteria:
> - [ ] Identity (workspace id, owner, name) and running or stopped state are read from Coder. A workspace that reports another workspace's id is rejected and the event is logged.
> - [ ] No address, port, Orca pairing offer, device token, agent token, API key or other standing credential is stored in the registry or sent through this feature.
> - [ ] The read route returns only the fields listed under Proposed Solution, as a named, versioned record with an age. It runs no `superclaude sweep run`, writes nothing, and consumes no Orca message.
> - [ ] This feature has no prompt, command or session-start route. A test shows the route rejects every non-read request and every request type other than "status".
> - [ ] Only the workspace owner, or a read credential the owner issued, can read. Unauthenticated and other users' requests are rejected and logged; no unauthenticated port is exposed; transport is protected.
> - [ ] A stopped, deleted or never-registered workspace is reported as stopped, deleted or none, with a time. A record older than [N minutes: owner decides] is reported stale, never current.
> - [ ] A Minimal-profile workspace is reported as "no orchestrator", not as missing.
> - [ ] Each read writes an audit line (time, reader, workspace id, result) that the workspace owner can read.

Out of Scope:
> - Routing a prompt or command to any workspace, session or orchestrator.
> - The issue watcher (IronbellyOrg/IronClaude#331, #450); it needs no listener.
> - Choosing a grant format (IronbellyOrg/IronClaude#339) and Claude Code cross-session messaging.
> - Reusing the central heartbeat collector (`templates/VSCode-AIDev02/collector/`); its endpoint is anonymous.
> - Upstream component monitors (#419, `.github/workflows/orca-upstream-monitor.yml`).
> - Changing the sweep or the observer.

Components Affected (corrected paths): `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/main.tf`; `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/startup.tf`; `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/modules.tf`; `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/image/catalogue/orca/module.tf`; `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/image/catalogue/profiles.yaml`; `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/image/orca/runtime.mjs`.

Related: IronbellyOrg/IronClaude#339 and PR #539; #409 (and PRs #503, #514); #450; Coder#195; Coder#419. Depends on: the owner's answers to D-1 and D-2; observer release R4.

**B. KEEP (only if the owner revisits option B or C). Replace the Problem Statement with the status paragraph above plus "the owner has chosen to revisit option A", and replace the acceptance criteria with:**
> - [ ] The listener exists only in workspaces whose profile includes the `orca` and `ironclaude` slugs (`image/catalogue/profiles.yaml`). Minimal workspaces are reported as "no orchestrator".
> - [ ] A request is one of a fixed list of types. For the issue watcher the only type is `claim` plus one integer issue number (IronClaude decision D3); no free text ever reaches a session prompt, and the requester cannot choose a flag, a working directory or a permission mode.
> - [ ] An accepted `claim` starts a new top-level session in the workspace checkout whose `origin` is the issue's repository, with the same fixed prompt and launch the watcher uses (the claim level is decided in IronbellyOrg/IronClaude#450). It is not sent as a message to a running session.
> - [ ] The owner has accepted in writing the permission mode of every session the request starts. The stock image launches every Claude session with permission checks off (`README.md`, "Permission checks are off", #455); a live test records the actual mode.
> - [ ] Every request carries sender identity, a grant naming the workspace, the request type and an expiry, and a one-use nonce. Unsigned, expired, wrong-workspace and replayed requests are rejected and logged. The grant format is the one chosen in IronbellyOrg/IronClaude#339; the route stays disabled until it exists. The grant does not replace the watcher's approver, label and integer-only checks.
> - [ ] A request for a stopped, stale or deleted workspace is refused with a reason; it is never queued. A per-type rate cap applies (the watcher's is 6 a day).
> - [ ] Reading status and sending a request use separate credentials; a read credential is rejected by the request route; a test shows it. No address, port, offer or standing credential is stored.
> - [ ] Each decision is audited with time, sender, workspace id, request type, issue number and reason, and can be joined to the watcher's `audit.jsonl` by issue number and workspace id.

**C. CLOSE (comment text).**
> Closing as superseded. The IronClaude issue watcher was built as a poller inside each workspace (IronbellyOrg/IronClaude#331, rollout #450; design `docs/designs/issue-watcher.md` section 2.1, decision D1 option A, 2026-11-07), so no listener, inbound port or address registry is needed. Reopen, or open a new issue from the narrowed text in [link to the comment holding option A], when (1) one of the nine revisit conditions in that design section 2.3 occurs, or (2) the IronClaude observer's release R4 (remote fleet) or a TUIBBS screen has a task package that needs to read another workspace.

### 5.7 State, guard and boundary risks specific to #477 (checklist for any version)

1. **Stale registration.** A registry entry for a stopped or deleted workspace must not look live; key by workspace id; show age; remove on delete. Coder, not the workspace, reports deletion.
2. **Name reuse.** Names are reusable; ids are not (`main.tf:111` embeds names). Never route by name.
3. **Spoofed identity.** The existing collector accepts any caller; a registry that copies it fails AC4.
4. **Credential leakage into Coder's own stores.** The #195 PRD forbids putting a standing credential in agent metadata, `coder_metadata` or build logs because Coder stores them. A registration must not do so either.
5. **Read credential escalation.** A reader's credential must be rejected by any route that can start a session; the observer must never hold a credential that can.
6. **Consumed state.** A status read must not consume Orca messages (D-4).
7. **Version skew.** Carry Orca, Claude Code and image versions in the record (A-009).
8. **Untrusted content in records.** Records come from workspaces that read untrusted issue and repo text; readers re-sanitise and treat every string as data.
9. **Bypass by default.** Any route that can start a session starts it with permission checks off on the stock image (5.5). That is the strongest reason the narrowed version has no send route.

### Variant 4 Advocate rebuttal

(Source file: round2-rebuttal-V4.md; full text below)

# Round 2 rebuttal for variant 4 (#423 v2 and the #339 boundary)

Advocate: read-only (GitHub and all checkouts); this file is the only thing written. Re-checked on 2026-11-14 against the review checkout (`873599d59`), `origin/master` (`d2d8a2d16`), `gh` reads, and `/home/coder/workspaces/Coder` read-only. "R1-Vn" = round1-advocate-Vn.md, "R2-Vn" = round2-rebuttal-Vn.md, "digest" = round1-contested-digest.md. Convergence after Round 1: 63.9% (23 of 36 points agreed).

## 1. Response to each criticism raised against V4

Eleven criticisms: I concede eight in full or in part (rows 1-5, 7, 8, 11), counter one in part (row 6), and agree with two while adding a newly checked fact (rows 9, 10).

So v2 cannot assume "persist the JSON" is cheap. The coordinator already runs the sweep every pass and receives the JSON on stdout (`commands.py:275-278`), so a v2 pass can consume stdout without a new file. That is a design option for v2's brainstorm, not a decision. | `test_readonly.py:396-402`; `commands.py:275-278` |

**One self-correction nobody raised.** R1-V4 F7 and section 3 item 3 said "a worker cannot message peers (WC-13)". WC-13 (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:62`) forbids a worker from starting workers or sessions (`worker-start`, `dispatch`, `task-create`, `agent launch`); it does not name peer messages. What the contract shows is that it defines no worker-to-worker channel (lifecycle messages go to the coordinator, WC-7 and WC-12). The conclusion of F7 holds; the citation was loose.

## 2. Updated assessment of V1, V2, V3, V5 and of their Round 2 claims

**V1 (up on its sweep-side facts, down on its premise).** R2-V1 is a model concession: it gives up the false premise, withdraws the rebase claim, and agrees with V4 on X-002, X-007 and BC-17. Claims I now rely on, all re-checked: the sweep prints JSON after publishing a report (`commands.py:275-278`); persistence is blocked by the one-file pin (`test_readonly.py:396-402`); `_worktree_paths` and `_git` (`probes.py:1203-1214`, `:305-327`) execute git in every Orca-listed checkout with inherited environment. One claim I do not accept as stated: R2-V1 A-007 says "the live risk is not other users but untrusted repo content". Both exist; v2 adds a third (agent-written text, section 5.4). Its D-4 (BC-17) I accept.

**V2 (up, with one unverified live fact).** R2-V2's reframing of W-10 ("do not add a watcher panel to `observe` before FU-1") and its third contract (watcher `status --json`, `dispatch.json`) are right and change my store inventory (section 5.2). Its new live observation (27 agent rows, no slash-command `prompt`) I cannot re-run; it remains UNVERIFIED by me and it only bears on D-B (labelling a watcher session by its agent row). It supports, not weakens, V4's per-class AC amendment.

**V3 (up on trust and scope; one gap in its Round 2).** R2-V3 concedes Edit 5 and Edit 8 and adds a fact I verified: new Claude sessions on the stock image run with permission checks off unless `CCSESSION_PERMISSIONS=default` (`ccsession:135-141`; `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/README.md:60`, "Permission checks are off for every call the wrapper routes"). That raises the stakes of any relay (section 5.4). Its clause on edit 3 ("if #477 is kept") I accept. Its D-7 proposal (use the Coder control plane for workspace identity) rests on docs and no live call; it is a candidate for v2's later cross-workspace step, not evidence.

**V5 (the decisive correction, with three limits that Round 1 left in place).** Its core is verified and unchallenged. Limits: BC-17 is now withdrawn by V1, V2 and V5 itself (R1-V5 concession 6); "keep two readers" is right for the observer and neutral for v2; R2-V1 argues the questions-read consumption evidence is not diagnostic on a live coordinator Run (R2-V1 section 5 item 2); I did not open `owner-decisions-20261111.md` and leave that UNVERIFIED. For v2 the practical consequence is that no v2 acceptance criterion may depend on message reads.

**Net movement of V4:** F1, F7, F8, F9, F14, F17 (with the corrected reason) are now agreed or unchallenged by all four other advocates; F13 and F16 are reframed (not dropped); F18 shrinks; (a)/(b) are replaced.

| X-008 | V1's verdict line contradicts its own C-02 and F-02 and is false for the observer (it shows none of the ten dimension names); V1's tables stand. | V5 on the fact; V1's tables | 92% | Drop the verdict sentence; publish one word-mapping table (observer / sweep / Orca) in the relationship note. |
| X-009 | Tally miscount; no decision depends on it. | none | 95% | None. |
| X-019 | V5 overgeneralised. V1 stated "no observer exists"; V2 W-10 and V4 F13/(a)(b) assumed it; V3 wrote generically and did not check. | none (V5 slip) | 88% | Cite as "V1 stated it; V2 W-10 and V4 F13 assumed it; V3 did not check". |
| X-020 | W-3 is V1's proposed wording, not body text (no match in the live body, SC-19). | none (V5 slip) | 95% | Read as "do not add V1's W-3"; no body edit. |

## 4. Remaining disagreements and the single fact that would settle each

|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-1 | When and how the sweep's JSON becomes readable without re-running it (needed by v2, a status route) | R2-V1 (Phase 2 item 1, after #539, with a pairing design), R1-V5 concession 4 (now), V4 (stdout capture in the WC-16 pass is a third option) | Whether the owner relaxes the one-report-file pin (`test_readonly.py:396-402`, `test_cli_contract.py:1192,1361`) or accepts stdout capture by the caller | Owner; PR #539 author |
| D-2 | What gates #423: Phase 1 merged only, or also `/sc:close`, resume, trust layer, orchestrator of orchestrators | V4 (Phase 1 plus #534 capture), the #339 issue summary (v1 = sweep + `/sc:close` + research) | The owner's one-line answer to V4 Q1 | Owner only |
| D-3 | Should v2 read the observer snapshot at all | V5 BC-17 (yes), V1, V2, V4 (not until fields are named) | v2's field list with a source per field (the snapshot has none of command, objective, branch, changed paths, issue, PR) | The v2 brainstorm |
| D-4 | One grant vocabulary: conditional on a listener? | V3 item 4 (conditional on #477), V4 (consumers exist without it: child grants and sub-orchestrators, #339 comment 2 line 41) | The owner's #477 answer (keep, narrow, close) and whether the #339 trust layer (research Phase 4) is scheduled before v2 relays to agents | Owner |
| D-5 | Can the sweep label a watcher-launched session | V2 W-05(b) vs V4 AC and V1 | One `worktree ps --json` agent row after a real `agent launch`: does `prompt` hold `/sc:issue --claim N` and does `paneKey` equal a tab-map name | The FU-1 runner |
| D-6 | Do `check --all --types question` reads consume on a live coordinator Run | V5 O-05 vs R2-V1 D-2 | A seeded test: known `question`, `worker_done` and heartbeat messages, two reads with the observer template, then one consuming `check` returns all of them | Owner or the 409 session with live Orca |
| D-7 | Is `dispatchId` = `row_id` minus `orca:` a real join key | V5 D1 (assumed), R2-V1 D-5 | Diff the observer's dispatch ids against one sweep's `row_id` list for the same Run | Owner or the 409 session |
| D-8 | Must v2 ever deliver a course correction to a non-worker session | V4 (no; report to the person, WC-16), #423 AC ("relay them to affected agents") | The owner's answer to V4 Q4 | Owner |
| D-9 | May v2 keep a store that is shared between orchestrators given WC-16's "report is never posted" | V4 F4 (reconcile explicitly), #423 (share knowledge) | The owner's statement of the store's reader set and location | Owner |

## 5. Not yet examined by the debate: risks specific to v2 (#423) and to the v1/v2 boundary

Nothing here is posted. Every fact marked "re-read" or "re-checked" was verified on 2026-11-14; "per Vn" means taken from that report and not re-run by me.

### 5.1 Three meanings of "v1" and "v2" (not two)

| Source | What it calls v1 | What it calls v2 |
|---|---|---|
| #339 issue summary (`.dev/issues/339/issue-summary.md:6`, re-read) | `/sc:sweep` with the static report, `/sc:close`, the comment-3 research and debate | signed grants plus the orchestrator of orchestrators |
| #339 comments of 2026-11-05 (re-read) | n/a | "Version 2 should include an orchestrator of orchestrators" (comment 1); "Build Steps 2 and 3 as part of version 2, before any orchestrator-of-orchestrators work" (comment 2) |
| Phase 1 research report (`RESEARCH-REPORT-orchestrator-mode.md`, "Later phases", re-read) | Phase 1 sweep only (the owner's first-PR scope); it records the source disagreement as a `[CONTRADICTION]` and numbers Phase 2 `/sc:close`, 3 resume, 4 trust layer, 5 orchestrator of orchestrators, 6 management-server relay | Phase 5 is labelled "Version-2 work" |
| #423 (re-read) | "the v1 orchestrator (#339)", gate "after ... is complete" | the catalogue and cross-agent reviews |

Consequence the earlier reports did not draw: under the issue summary's grouping, "#339 is complete" means `/sc:close` is also done, which is Phase 2 and not on PR #539. Under the research grouping it means Phase 1 only. #423's gate therefore has at least two readings and cannot be applied as written.

### 5.2 Is a catalogue a further data store? Yes, at least an eighth.

Stores that already hold agent facts, all checked except the last:

| Store | Where | Protection and sharing | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sweep report | `<root>/.dev/sweeps/<stamp>-sweep.md` | git-ignored (`.gitignore:256`); WC-16: "a dated local file; it is never posted"; the sweeps folder holds exactly one name (`test_readonly.py:396-402`) | JSON goes to stdout only |
| Observer snapshot and export | `$XDG_STATE_HOME/superclaude/observe/snapshot.json` | mode 0600, overwritten every live cycle, per process (V5; SC-17 confirmed) | camelCase schema, `UNKNOWN` strings |
| Watcher store | `state_dir` (0700): `dispatch.json` (0600), `audit.jsonl` (0600) | per workspace and per login (`issue-watcher.md:117,398,400`; `issue-watcher-setup.md:642`) | `dispatch.json` `schema_version` 1 |
| PM memory | `<project>/.superclaude/memory/` | "one store per worktree", self-ignored with `*` (`sc-pm-protocol/SKILL.md:23`) | checkpoints every 30 minutes |
| Lessons learned | `docs/memory/solutions_learned.jsonl`, `patterns_learned.jsonl`, `reflect/` | tracked in git (`git ls-files docs/memory`), shared only through branches and PRs | the only reviewed, cross-worktree store |
| Task ledgers | `.dev/tasks/<pkg>/` | tracked in git (project rule) | per package |
| Coder heartbeat collector | central endpoint | anonymous, no auth (per V3 F14, `collector/README.md:10`; not re-run) | not an IronClaude store |

The catalogue would be another. Only two of these (lessons, task ledgers) cross worktrees, and only through git. The existing live channel to another agent is the WC-12 follow-up, which reaches Orca workers only (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:61-62,288`). So "share knowledge between orchestrators" has no existing runtime carrier; v2 must either extend a named store or justify a new one with owner, reader set, retention, file mode and a reconciliation with WC-16's "never posted". Default if undecided: local, per host, mode 0600, not shared, never posted, so it creates no new exposure beyond the sweep report. This is why my #423 AC 5 below is mandatory, not optional.

### 5.3 Who may read whose sessions

- **Today.** The sweep reads one OS user's `~/.claude/projects/<key>` folders for every path `worktree ps` lists (`probes.py:1344-1350`, `:1203-1214`), but it never holds transcript text: `TranscriptTail` carries only path, session id, readable flag, timestamps, mtime and project key (`models.py:127-138`, re-read). The report therefore cannot leak transcript content; its remaining risks are untrusted checkouts (git config runs with inherited environment, `probes.py:305-327`) and worker-written contract strings (whether they reach `evidence` or `notes` is UNVERIFIED per R2-V1).
- **v2.** #423 proposes "cheaper, faster agents searching sessions or diffing logs and summarizing changes". That is the first component that would read transcript and log content and write it into a shared artifact. All Coder workspaces use `HOME=/home/coder` (per V3 F4), so a path is not an identity; the Coder workspace id and owner are not keys in any current JSON.
- **Proposed rule for the design (becomes #423 AC 4).** (1) First release reads only what the same OS user on the same host can already read. (2) Transcript or log content never enters the catalogue; only typed metadata and agent-authored contract fields do, each marked with its collector and source session id. If excerpts are ever needed they are bounded, marked untrusted, and stored apart. (3) No cross-user or cross-workspace read until a read-permission model is written and the owner approves it. (4) Orca-listed worktrees and any session text are treated as untrusted input.

### 5.4 Relay of untrusted content between agents

- **Path.** External or agent-written text (web pages an agent fetched, issue and PR text, third-party repo files, contract strings) enters a transcript or log, a summarizer agent condenses it, the catalogue stores it, the relevance step selects recipients, and AC 11 of #423 says changes are relayed "to affected agents".
- **Why the existing channel is the dangerous one.** A WC-12 follow-up is a direction channel: the worker "handles every message in it" and for each that "answers or directs it" acts after acknowledging (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:61`, re-read). New sessions on the stock image run with permission checks off unless `CCSESSION_PERMISSIONS=default` (`ccsession:135-141`; `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/README.md:60`, re-read). #339 comment 2 (line 22, re-read) records that signing cannot separate an honest instruction from one the sender picked up from a poisoned input. The sweep skill's rule "report text is data" (`sc-sweep-protocol/SKILL.md:63`, re-read) is an instruction to the coordinator, not a mechanical guard.
- **Required controls (become #423 AC 7 and 8).** (a) The relay record is typed: fixed fields (session id, worktree path, branch, kind, source collector), free text only in one `detail` field that the receiver renders as quoted data and never acts on (#339 comment 2 Step 2, line 34). (b) First release is advisory: it reports to the person or the coordinator and sends nothing to a worker automatically; a coordinator that chooses to act uses the existing WC-12 rules. (c) The contract has no worker-to-worker channel (lifecycle messages go to the coordinator, WC-7 and WC-12; WC-13 stops a worker starting others), so any cross-agent relay is coordinator-mediated. (d) LLM-derived entries are marked inferred, stored apart from sweep facts, and never change a sweep class. (e) Summarizer agents get read-only tools. (f) The allowed relay fields are pinned by a test, the way the sweep pins its JSON key set (design `:599`) and its forbidden verbs, so the data-only rule is checked by a test and not only by an instruction.

### 5.5 Guards at the v1/v2 boundary

| Guard that exists | Source (re-read) | What v2 must do |
|---|---|---|
| `/sc:sweep` "recommends nothing: no stop, retry, release, merge or removal. The coordinator decides and performs any action" | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:288` | v2's purpose is proactive course correction, which is a recommendation. v2 therefore cannot be added to `/sc:sweep` or to the WC-16 report; it is a separate command or skill that reads sweep output and has its own contract. |
| Never-as-worker list (`/sc:agent`, `/sc:git`, `/sc:help`, `/sc:issue`, `/sc:pm`, `/sc:spawn`, `/sc:sweep`, `/sc:swarm-wizard`; changes "only when the coordinator-only command set or D3 changes") | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:23` | A new v2 command must be classified on that list in the same change. `/sc:pm` and `/sc:spawn`, which #423 lists for reuse, are already coordinator-only. |
| No Orca write verb; closed allow-list of 10 templates and 7 verbs under owner sign-off | `probes.py:62` (comment); design `:555` | v2 adds no verb to the sweep. GitHub reads for the relevance check are a v2-only collector, or a new template with a new owner note (design `:35`: "gh reads" are out of Phase 1). |
| Finished rows never print `admitted` | design `:515` | v2 keeps sweep classes unchanged; overlap is reported as possible overlap, never as conflict. |
| The sweep, WC-16 and `/sc:sweep` exist only on PR #539, not on master | `origin/master` has no `cli/sweep`; PR #539 head `873599d59` | #423 must say "on PR #539" wherever it cites them. |

### 5.6 Remaining state and guard risks for v2 (checklist for the design)

1. **Staleness.** The sweep's "stale" is relative to a 6-hour window, the observer's STALE is 45 s without a good read; a catalogue entry needs its own age rule and must show the source's age, not its own.
2. **Truth direction.** A catalogue entry is derived data; it must never be read back as a fact by a collector (the sweep never reads `.dev/sweeps/`, design `:146`).
3. **Run-less sessions.** Neither collector sees watcher-launched or person-started sessions with no Run; the catalogue must show "not observable", never "absent".
4. **Coordinator death.** WC-16 is a duty of a person-opened coordinator; if it dies no refresh happens. v2 must show catalogue age.
5. **Double cadence.** Five clocks would exist (section 1, row 4). v2 reuses the WC-16 pass or records an owner decision.
6. **Identity.** Paths are not identities across workspaces; the catalogue needs workspace id, owner login, host and Orca build per fact (A-009).
7. **Reversibility.** First release must be removable: a derived, local, deletable artifact and a command that can be unregistered, with no change to sweep JSON v1.

### 5.7 Proposed rewrite of #423 and the minimal edits to #339

Both are drafts for the owner to post or discard. They carry four corrections to R1-V4's text (data source names both collectors; per-class criterion; observer components and PRs; #331 caveat) and the "if #477 is kept" clause.

**#423 title.** `[FEATURE] Orchestrator v2 (knowledge layer): catalogue of agent work and advisory cross-agent reviews` (keeps "v2" because #339, #409 and #423's own links use it by number).

**#423 body (replaces Feature Summary, Proposed Solution, Acceptance Criteria, Out of Scope, Components Affected, Related; Problem Statement and User Story stay as they are).**

````markdown
## Feature Summary

Orchestrator v2 (knowledge layer) gives an orchestrator, here a coordinator session running the WC-16 loop, a catalogue of what agents are doing and regular cross-agent reviews that share relevant knowledge and flag duplicate or overlapping work. It is advisory: it reports to the person or the coordinator and sends no instruction to an agent on its own. In this issue "v2" means this knowledge layer. It is not the "version 2" in the comments on #339 of 2026-11-05 (trust layer and orchestrator of orchestrators); those are later #339 phases.

## Proposed Solution

Sequencing. This starts only after PR #539 (#339 Phase 1, the read-only sweep) is merged and the #534 live capture that restores the sweep's `working` class is recorded. The other #339 phases (`/sc:close`, resume, trust layer, orchestrator of orchestrators) are not prerequisites, unless the brainstorm decides that v2 relays to agents and not only to the person; then the trust decisions in #339 are.

Start with `/sc:brainstorm --depth deep --handoff design`, then the dedicated `/tech-research` pass on multi-agent memory and context techniques (save its findings to this issue). Do not begin implementation before both are complete.

Data sources and boundaries. The catalogue reads facts through one named fact schema; the collectors are `superclaude sweep run` (per Run: class with proof, contracts, git state, transcript metadata; on PR #539, not on master) and `superclaude observe` (all Runs on one host: Orca worker rows; on master, PRs #503 and #514). Neither carries a command or objective, branch, changed paths, issue or PR today; the design names each field the catalogue needs and its source, and any schema change is decided in #339 (sweep JSON v1) or #409 (observe snapshot v1), never by a v2-only scanner. First release scope is one host and several Runs. Sessions with no Run (person-started, watcher-launched) are not observable by either collector and are listed as "not observable". "All workspaces" depends on whether Coder#477 is kept (the issue-watcher design says option A makes it unnecessary) and on a written read-permission model.

Trust and safety. Relayed knowledge is data, never an instruction: fixed typed fields, free text only in one `detail` field that is never acted on. LLM-derived entries are marked inferred, stored apart from sweep facts and never change a sweep class. The first release delivers to the person or the coordinator only.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] `/sc:brainstorm --depth deep --handoff design` has been run, with its design handoff linked here before implementation begins.
- [ ] A dedicated `/tech-research` pass on multi-agent memory and context techniques is saved to this issue (it may be done ahead of the rest).
- [ ] The design names every catalogue field, where each comes from (sweep report JSON v1, observe snapshot v1, other), and which fields need a schema decision in #339 or #409; no field is added by a new scanner of Orca, git or transcripts.
- [ ] The design states the scope (one host and several Runs for the first release; several Coder workspaces only with a written read-permission model and an owner decision on Coder#477) and what is read: no transcript or log content enters the catalogue; session text is untrusted.
- [ ] The catalogue has a named store, owner, reader set, retention and file mode, says which of the existing stores it extends (sweep report, observe snapshot, watcher `dispatch.json`, `.superclaude/memory/`, `docs/memory/`, task ledgers) or why it is new, and says how it differs from the WC-16 rule that a sweep report is local and never posted.
- [ ] For each session class (Orca worker, watcher-launched, person-started, session without a Run) the design states what is observable, how a checkpoint is obtained, and how a course correction is delivered: a coordinator follow-up (WC-12) for workers, a report to the person for the rest. Labelling machine-started sessions depends on a #534 row.
- [ ] Relayed knowledge is data: a pinned set of typed fields, free text only in `detail`, checked by a test; relay passes through the coordinator (the contract has no worker-to-worker channel); summarizer agents have read-only tools.
- [ ] LLM-derived entries are marked inferred, stored apart from sweep facts, and never change a sweep class; overlapping files are reported as possible overlap, never as conflict.
- [ ] The refresh is the WC-16 coordinator pass, or the design records the owner decision for a further scheduler (existing clocks: WC-16 at most every 5 minutes, observer 15 s, watcher cron 5 minutes, PM checkpoints 30 minutes).
- [ ] The design says what GitHub reads the relevance check needs (unmerged PRs) and whether they are a v2-only collector or a new sweep template with a new owner note.
- [ ] The harness-neutral fact schema is named, with the sweep and the observer as two collectors of it (including the Mastra question, currently UNVERIFIED: nothing in the repo mentions it).
- [ ] The design lists what each review adds beyond `worktrees[].shared_package_ids`, the watcher's one-claim-per-issue rule and the observer's "changes since last look"; the "Collision Lens" design is a synthetic image, not built.
- [ ] The design and resulting components are checked against `docs/developer-guide/SuperClaude-Developer-Guide-Commands-Skills-Agents.md`, with the review recorded; any new command is placed on, or kept off, the never-as-worker list in the same change.
- [ ] v2 is delivered as a separate later release after the sequencing gate above.

## Out of Scope

- v1 orchestrator work (#339 Phase 1 and later phases: `/sc:close`, resume, trust layer, orchestrator of orchestrators, management-server relay).
- A second scanner of Orca, git or transcripts; changing sweep classes or the sweep JSON v1 keys (decided in #339).
- Sending prompts or commands to person-started sessions (WC-16: they are never messaged) or to workers outside WC-12.
- A listener or workspace registry (Coder#477).
- Implementation before the brainstorm and research are complete.

## Components Affected

Existing surfaces to review, not a file list: `docs/developer-guide/SuperClaude-Developer-Guide-Commands-Skills-Agents.md`; `src/superclaude/core/WORKER-CONTRACT.md` (WC-12, WC-13, WC-16 and the never-as-worker list); `src/superclaude/skills/sc-pm-protocol/SKILL.md` (memory is one store per worktree, self-ignored; not a cross-workspace store) and `src/superclaude/commands/spawn.md`; `src/superclaude/cli/sweep/`, `src/superclaude/commands/sweep.md`, `src/superclaude/skills/sc-sweep-protocol/SKILL.md`, `docs/designs/orchestrator-mode.md` (all on PR #539, not on master); `src/superclaude/cli/observe/` (on master); `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/` (starts an ordinary session; notifies no orchestrator); `src/superclaude/pm_agent/reflexion.py`, `docs/memory/solutions_learned.jsonl`, `docs/memory/reflect/` (existing lessons-learned stores); `~/.claude/skills/orchestration/SKILL.md` (installed Orca discovery stub, external).

## Related Issues/PRs

- #339: orchestrator; Phase 1 is PR #539. Its comments use "version 2" for the trust layer and orchestrator of orchestrators; this issue is the knowledge layer.
- #409 and PRs #503, #514: the read-only observer (`superclaude observe`) is built; it shows the human what happened, this layer shares knowledge between agents.
- #331: issue watcher, built (PRs #447, #448; rollout #450). It starts an ordinary `/sc:issue --claim <N>` session through `orca agent launch` and notifies no orchestrator; the claim at its default level also launches a second governed agent (#450).
- #450: watcher rollout.
- #444: sub-orchestrator evaluation (open); relevant to who holds a catalogue when a coordinator sits under another.
- #534: Phase 1 follow-ups; the `working` class and agent-wait rule gate what a catalogue can say.
- PR #539: Phase 1 of #339, the sweep.
- IronbellyOrg/Coder#477: workspace listener; the only planned cross-workspace channel if it is kept (the watcher design says option A makes it unnecessary).
- #326: Git/GitHub communication improvements, not the catalogue itself.
````

**#339: minimal edits.** One comment, no body edit, no scope change (the issue body stays as the owner wrote it):

### Variant 5 Advocate rebuttal

(Source file: round2-rebuttal-V5.md; full text below)

# Round 2 rebuttal: variant 5 (the already built observer versus the sweep)

Advocate: adv-r1-v5 (read-only; wrote only this file). Date: 2026-11-14. Inputs read: round1-contested-digest.md; sections 4 and 5 of round1-advocate-V1..V4; round2-rebuttal-V1..V4 (sections 1 to 5 where they touch V5). New facts below were verified now against `origin/master`, the PR #539 checkout, GitHub (read calls) and the sibling worktree `issue-409-mvp` (read only); each is marked "(verified now)".

## 1. Response to each criticism raised against V5

Sources: R1-V1 section 4 items 6 to 8, 12 and R2-V1 sections 2 and 5; R1-V2 item 10; R1-V3 item 6; R1-V4 items 7 to 9; R2-V2, R2-V3 and R2-V4 where they cite V5. Repeats are grouped. Result: I concede nine in full or in part, counter two in part, and I withdraw three of my own Round 1 recommendations.

| # | Criticism | Response | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | X-019: "all four earlier reviews wrong about one fact" overgeneralises (V1, V2, V3, V4) | **Concede.** Correct citation: "V1 stated it; V2 W-10 and V4 F13/(a)(b) assumed it; V3 wrote generically and did not check." | Already in R1 concession 7. |
| 2 | X-020: "drop W-3 from the #409 body" is a mislabel (all) | **Concede.** Read it as "do not add V1's W-3". | SC-19. |
| 3 | F-04 "CLOSED" is true only for the Orca ledger (V1 #6iii) | **Concede as a wording point, counter on substance.** V5's own RT-F04 cell says "REMAINS for the sweep" and its owns-table gives git, contract and transcript facts to the sweep, so V5 did not overclaim; but the headline word CLOSED invites the misreading, so cite it as "closed for the Orca ledger on one host; open for per-Run depth and for multi-host". | V5 RT-F04; `adapter.py` `--limit 100`, `MAX_CALLS_PER_CYCLE = 5` (verified in R1). |
| 4 | O-05: "the sweep's reason is stronger than the evidence" is not shown; the observer's non-consumption evidence is owner-only and non-diagnostic on a live Run (V1 #6iv, V4 #8c, R2-V1 section 5.2, R2-V2 D-F, R2-V3 D-4, R2-V4 D-6) | **Concede in full and withdraw O-05's verdict and its proposed rewording.** I re-read the two sources (verified now). `owner-decisions-20261111.md` on master: "`check --all --run <run>` returned 100 rows ... A later consuming `check` found 0 pending: the reads did not consume. Tested as the Run's owner only." `release-gates.md` on master: "`--all` rows carry no read or acknowledged marker", and the only diagnostic result ("a later consuming `check` by the owner still received everything") is from a synthetic probe Run. On the coordinator's own Run, "0 pending afterwards" is expected whether or not the reads consumed, so V1 is right that it proves nothing. Position now: X-016 is unproven both ways; the sweep's caution is defensible; its wording should say what is true (see section 5.8). | `git show origin/master:.dev/issues/409/owner-decisions-20261111.md` lines 13 to 15; `.../release-gates.md` lines 36 to 50 (both verified now). |
| 5 | The owner records behind V5's decision history are not on master and cannot be reproduced (V1 #7, V2, V3, V4) | **Concede.** New, verified now: master holds only `owner-decisions-20261111.md`; the sibling has the 2026-11-08 and 2026-11-12 records, the pass record, `live-test-guide.md` and `tuibbs-review/` as untracked files or in the two unpushed commits (HEAD `f104ca51a`). The pass record says "Written by the owner" and names the Run `run_93bfa00cae2b`, accepts Q-1 FAIL, unmeasured traffic and non-owner non-consumption as unverified; in the lines I read it does not say "owner-read consumption on a live Run is unverified", because the measurement in row 4 was taken as proof. Authorship stays UNVERIFIED. | sibling `ls`, `git status`, `sed -n 1,30p owner-pass-record-20261112.md`. |

Counter: V3's own R2 says the first status record can be the observer snapshot; V4's R2 offers stdout capture inside the WC-16 pass; neither needs a new sweep file. | R2-V3 D-3; R2-V4 D-1. |

## 2. Updated assessment of V1 to V4 and of their Round 2 claims

**V1: up on the sweep side, and its Round 2 corrects V5 on three points.** V1 withdrew every claim that rested on "no observer exists" (C-02, C-03, C-21, C-26, F-01 "observer reads the JSON", F-04 and F-12/F-14/F-15 as observer gaps, Q1, W-3) and kept the sweep-side findings (C-07, C-25, C-34, F-02, F-05, F-07, F-08 to F-11, F-13, F-17, F-18). That is the right net. Its Round 2 then adds three things I verified and accept: (1) the sidecar-JSON idea collides with NFR-001 and its pins (`test_readonly.py:300`, `test_cli_contract.py:1474`, design line 128); (2) the observer's "0 pending after reads" measurement on the coordinator's own Run is non-diagnostic (`owner-decisions-20261111.md` lines 13 to 15; `release-gates.md` "`--all` rows carry no read or acknowledged marker"); (3) merge order: #541 changes one file (`tests/core/test_claude_md_worktree_rule.py`, OPEN, mergeStateStatus CLEAN), and #539 changes `src/superclaude/core/HOW-WE-WORK.md`, its plugin mirror, `tests/core/test_how_we_work_routing_table.py` and `cli/main.py` but not the #541 file (all verified now through the pulls API file list), so the observer routing-row PR must wait for #539 and #541 needs no rebase of #539. V1's new "untrusted repo content" framing for A-007 is a better reason than "other users" and I adopt it (any checkout Orca lists, including a third-party clone, reaches `git status` with the inherited environment: `probes.py:1203-1214`, `:309`). One V1 Round 2 claim I only half accept: "sweep reads of host-wide worktrees and the observer's binary resolution could make the two programs read different Orca instances". The mechanism is real (V5 O-03), but nobody has shown a host with both names, so it stays a note, not a risk row.

**V2: up.** Its Round 2 narrows W-04 correctly (help text and a host listing, not an Orca source read), re-frames W-10 the way V5 needs ("no watcher panel in `observe` before FU-1"), and adds a third contract (the watcher's `status --json` and `dispatch.json`). Its 5.1 point (the ccsession wrapper that gives a session an identity defaults to `--dangerously-skip-permissions` unless `CCSESSION_PERMISSIONS=default`) was cited from `ccsession:135-141`; I did not re-read it here and treat it as V2's verified claim. It matters to this debate only through D-A/D-B: the observer and the sweep both stay blind to a watcher session until FU-1 shows what `agent launch` leaves in `worker-list` and `worktree ps`.

**V3: up on the Coder side and on a decision it made for itself.** V3's Round 2 resolves its own unknowns about gateway auth and Claude Code messaging (cited from the Coder repo; not re-opened by me), narrows its proposal to "read-only status, no prompt routing", and says the first status record can be the observer snapshot. I agree with that last point, which is the cleanest answer to V1's and V4's "the sweep output cannot be read without re-running it" worry for #477. Its "never exposed" list includes the sweep's Markdown and JSON until the #539 git-config thread is closed and a no-write form exists; I agree (open thread at `probes.py:1211`, GraphQL verified in Round 1). Its textual estimate for "TUI Panel" (the #409 dashboard about 75 percent, TUIBBS about 15 percent) is the best-argued position in the folder; I agree and give my answer in 5.7. Its D-8 (can a same-user process assert a permission class on a session's inbox socket) is outside what I can check.

**V4: up on F1, F7, F14 and on its self-corrections.** V4 withdrew edit 3 ("both read the same sweep facts") and the circular C8, conceded F17 and F16, and added that there are at least four contracts, not two (sweep report JSON v1, observe snapshot v1, the watcher's `status --json` and `dispatch.json`, and any v2 record), plus a fifth cadence for a v2 timer. I accept the count: my contract registers all of them but governs only the first two. V4's D-3 (should v2 read the observer snapshot at all: not until it names its fields) is the answer I now give (row 9 above). V4's statement that v2 cannot live inside `/sc:sweep` because "recommends nothing" is a WC-16 rule is a good boundary and consistent with V5: the sweep stays a record, not an actor.

**Net effect on V5's own standing.** After Round 2 the V5 core is unchanged (the observer exists, reads Orca only, never mentions the sweep and is never mentioned by it; two schemas; two readers). What shrank: O-05, BC-17, D5(ii), "persist now", and D9 as a guess. What grew: the contract in section 5 is now needed by three other variants' proposals (V3's status record, V4's field list, V1's mapping table), which makes it the main product of the debate.

## 5. Not yet examined: the integration contract between the sweep and the observer

Everything in this section is a proposal for the owner; nothing is posted or written outside this file. "Verified now" marks facts I re-read for this round.

### 5.1 What the contract is, and what it is not

A short, tracked, written note that names the two programs, the two JSON schemas, the one join key, the words both print, the Orca verbs each may call and the files each writes. It is documentation plus a pinned vocabulary. It adds no runtime link between the programs in its first version: the observer does not read the sweep, the sweep does not read the observer, and neither gains a new file or key. That is deliberate, because the sweep's one-report-file rule is pinned (NFR-001: design line 128; `tests/sweep/test_readonly.py:300`; `tests/sweep/test_cli_contract.py:1474`; verified now) and its JSON goes to stdout only after the report is published (`commands.py:270-277`).

### 5.2 Owner and location

- **File.** `docs/designs/observe-sweep-contract.md` (tracked and shipped with the repository; not under `.dev/`, which is task and issue scratch space). New file, so it does not touch PR #539.
- **Owner: the Orchestrator mode design (#339).** Reason: the sweep's JSON is the stricter contract (golden-tested key set; `ROW_KEYS` pins), it is the one other consumers ask to change (V1 F-01, V3 Edit 5, V4 F2), and the observer's snapshot is, by its own schema, "PROPOSED, not an Orca contract" and additive-only. The observer's spec (`.dev/issues/409/bmad/spec-orca-observer/release-gates.md` and `adapter-contract.md`) and `docs/designs/orchestrator-mode.md` section 1 link to the note; neither restates its tables.
- **Amendment rule.** A change to a shared word, the join key, either schema's version, or an Orca verb either program may call must edit the note in the same PR. Review of both programs asks "did this touch the note".
- **Registry rows, not governance.** The note lists four more contracts so nobody calls them "the JSON v1" again: the watcher's `status --json` and `dispatch.json` (R2-V2, R2-V4) and any future v2 record. It governs only the first two rows below.

### 5.3 Units and facts each program owns

| Subject | Observer (`superclaude observe`) | Sweep (`superclaude sweep run`) |
|---|---|---|
| Unit | all Runs on one host; up to 5 calls x 100 rows per 15 s cycle; PARTIAL beyond | rows for the bound Run; worktrees and transcript scans are host-global |
| Orca ledger facts (state, outcome, liveness, next action, evidence age) | owns; verbatim Orca words | reads per Run, `worker-show` for at most 50, 90 s budget; classifies with proof |
| Git, contracts, transcripts, `safe_to_close`, frozen plans, Task specs | none | owns |
| Coordinator questions (Orca message rows) | owns, one named Run, behind a guard that is False on master | none; `check` is forbidden |
| Sessions with no Run (watcher-launched, person-started) | not observable | transcript rows only, `could not determine` today |
| Orca binary resolution | `orca-ide` then `orca`, accepted only if `skills list --json` names `orca-cli` | `$ORCA_CLI_COMMAND` else `orca-ide`, never bare `orca` |
| Files written | `snapshot.json` and `observe-<stamp>.txt` under `$XDG_STATE_HOME/superclaude/observe/`, mode 0600 | one dated report under `<root>/.dev/sweeps/` |

### 5.4 What each program publishes for the other: nothing new, and the rules for what already exists

- **Nothing is published for the other program in version 1 of the note.** Reason: any sweep-side addition (sidecar JSON, new keys) collides with NFR-001 and the golden pins in a PR that is still contested; any observer-side addition is unneeded because the sweep has no reader.
- **What exists today and how a future reader must treat it.**
  - *Observer snapshot* (`snapshot.json`): last writer wins (`SNAPSHOT_NAME` fixed, `export.py:26`; two live observers overwrite each other); written by atomic replace, mode 0600; required top-level keys `schemaVersion, producer{name,version}, cycle, generatedAt, source{namespace,status,observedAt}, page, scope, counts, rows`; no writer pid and no liveness flag, so the file keeps its last `source.status` after the process exits (all verified now). Rule: a reader applies its own age rule to `generatedAt` and shows an old file as stale, never as current. `scope` is only `object` or `"UNKNOWN"`: the snapshot does not name the workspace, host or owner, only per-row `hostId` and `hostKind`; an envelope (workspace id, owner, login) belongs to Coder#477 (V3 Edit 4), not to either program.
  - *Sweep report and stdout JSON*: one dated Markdown report; the JSON is printed once and not stored. Rule: a caller that wants the JSON captures stdout itself (the WC-16 pass keeps only `report_path` and `summary` today, `SKILL.md` step 3); nobody re-runs the sweep to get it, because each run writes a report and runs `git status` in every worktree Orca lists.
  - *All strings from either program are untrusted input to a reader.* Two sanitizers exist (observer `sanitize.py`, sweep `report.py:563-598`, which also Markdown-escapes JSON strings); a reader re-sanitises and unescapes.
- **First possible exchange, gated.** If the owner answers D-6 "yes" the first exchange is (a) caller-captured sweep stdout in the WC-16 pass, or (b) a Coder-owned copy of the observer snapshot (see 5.7). Both are Phase 2 and need D-2 or the transport decision.

### 5.5 Versioning rule for the two JSON schemas

- **Names.** Always "observe snapshot v1" and "sweep report JSON v1". A bare "JSON v1" is wrong in any issue, doc or test name.
- **Observe snapshot v1.** `schemaVersion` const 1, camelCase, `"UNKNOWN"` strings, rows keyed `dispatchId`, `additionalProperties: false`. Adding an optional key: same version, with a schema and test change in the observer's PR. Renaming, removing or changing the meaning of a key: v2. Consumers must ignore unknown keys and must not validate with a frozen copy of the producer's schema, because the producer's own schema rejects extras and would reject a newer file.
- **Sweep report JSON v1.** `schema_version`, snake_case, `null`, rows keyed `row_id`; key set pinned by a golden test. Adding an optional key: stays v1 with a deliberate golden-test change in the owner's PR (not inside #539); renaming or removing: v2.
- **Join key.** `dispatchId` (observer) equals the part of `row_id` after `orca:` (sweep). Structural basis verified now: the sweep reads `dispatchId` from the `worker-list` row (`probes.py:435`) and builds `row_id=f"orca:{o.dispatch}"` (`classify.py:891`); the observer rows are keyed `dispatchId`. It is called a join key in the note only after one real-id comparison (D-3). `transcript:<session>` rows have no observer counterpart. Runs join on observer `runId` and sweep `scope.runs`.

### 5.6 Mapping table of shared words

Each cell states what the program means; the last column is the rule for any screen or text that shows both.

| Word | Observer | Sweep | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| stale | its own feed: no good read for 45 s (`STALE_SECONDS = 45.0`, `view.py`); also a snapshot file judged by `generatedAt` | a transcript-only row older than the 6 h window (V1 F-02, `classify.py` freshness) | say "feed stale" or "session stale"; never show one as the other |
| unknown | the string `UNKNOWN` in JSON and on screen | `null` in JSON, `unknown` in text | `unknown` never becomes `not admitted`, `not frozen` or `no issue observed` |
| unavailable | `unavailable (no source)`: the program does not read the fact (report status, acceptance, tests, approval, SETTLED, POLICY FROZEN, cost) | `unavailable`: a source it tried and could not read | "no source" means not collected here, not false |
| could not determine | no counterpart (it classifies nothing) | a class and a `safe_to_close` value when proof is missing | if a panel ever shows sweep rows it prints the sweep's words verbatim and labels the report age |
| historical | Orca `terminalState` in (`released`, `retained`), hidden behind `h` (`HISTORICAL_STATES`, verified now) | a row older than the window when the list is complete (design FR-010) | label which program's meaning |
| frozen | `POLICY FROZEN` printed `unavailable (no source)` | `policy_of` returns `frozen` only from a blocked or failed worker contract with stop codes (`classify.py:377-392`, verified now) | the observer never prints `not frozen` |
| settled | `SETTLED` printed `unavailable (no source)`; Orca `outcome` shown verbatim | `_settled(orca)`: Orca outcome in `_SETTLED_OUTCOMES` (`classify.py:113-114`, verified now) | the observer shows the Orca word, not a derived verdict |
| coordinator question versus waiting on a person | Orca message rows of type `question` for one Run, labelled "coordinator questions", never "human" (owner decision 8, 2026-11-11) | "Waiting on a person" from blocked return contracts (and an inert agent-wait rule), plus frozen plans | neither implies the other is empty; both print "seen, not resolved"; fix #409 acceptance criterion 2, which still says "human attention items" |

### 5.7 My answer to: is the TUI panel the observer, a TUIBBS Go screen reading the snapshot, or both?

**Both, in sequence, with different jobs; and the observer is the panel that exists.** (1) Read "TUI Panel" in Coder#477 as `superclaude observe` (the #409 dashboard) until the owner says otherwise; V3's textual estimate (about 75 percent) matches mine, and I withdraw my earlier "probably, unverified" wording as too casual. (2) A TUIBBS Go screen is a possible second reader of the observer's snapshot, not a second program with its own collector. The owner's 2026-11-08 record (sibling, item 4 and 5) says the observer "may become a TUIBBS module" and marks it "Background, not deciding"; the snapshot was shipped so such a screen could read it later. (3) Before a TUIBBS screen can read anything, four things are missing, all verified now: a transport (TUIBBS runs on a separate SSH host; "No document in TUIBBS mentions Coder or Orca", sibling `notes-3.md:78-84`), an identity envelope (`scope` is untyped; only per-row `hostId` and `hostKind`), an age rule (no liveness field), and re-sanitising. So Coder#477 should be designed for a read-only, identity-tagged, aged copy of the observer snapshot, and should not be designed around either screen. Confidence about 70 percent; only the owner can settle it (D-4), and the contract holds under all three readings.

### 5.8 Orca verbs, `check`, and `projection.attention`

- **One list of read-only Orca verbs**, kept in the note, per caller: sweep 10 exact templates over 7 verbs (owner-signed, NFR-014), `check` forbidden; observer 3 argv shapes (`worker-list --json --limit 100 [--run][--cursor]`, `skills list --json`, and one `check --all --types question --run <id> --json` template behind `QUESTION_GATE_PASSED`, False on master: `commands.py:67`, verified now).
- **The sweep's two sentences** (`report.py:199,534`): keep the caution, say only what is established: "This command reads no messages. A plain `check` consumes them. Whether `check --all --types question` consumes on a live Run is not established." Edit after #539 merges; log in #534 now. (This replaces my withdrawn O-05 rewording.)
- **`projection.attention{categories, requiresAction}`.** Verified now: no `.py` file of either package reads it (it appears only in the observer's golden fixtures, kept by name). Proposal: after the shared capture (D-8), the observer shows it verbatim like every other Orca field, because the observer classifies nothing; the sweep does not read it in Phase 1 (it would add a key to a pinned set, and an Orca claim is not proof for a class); if the capture shows `requiresAction` true for blocked workers, the sweep may later cite it as evidence through its own proof rule. Neither program classifies from it until its values for live and blocked workers are recorded.

- Round 1: 23 of 36 X/A points with a stable winner (>= 2/3 of advocates), 63.9%, below the 80% threshold (digest: round1-contested-digest.md)
- Round 2: all 13 contested points reached one final verdict and winner across the five advocates; points resolved 36 of 36 on X/A (100%); A-009 is agreed to be unresolved by evidence (a real open question, not a disagreement)
- Taxonomy coverage gate: L1 surface (X-009 tally, X-019/X-020 wording), L2 structural (contract owner and venue, two programs, merge order, Coder#477 scope, #423 scope), L3 state mechanics (git config execution in `git status`, Orca message read consumption, snapshot last-writer-wins, guard-flip constant) all debated
- Invariant probe gate: BLOCKED_BY_INVARIANTS. 1 HIGH and UNADDRESSED invariant: INV-008. The gate asks that it be addressed in a further round or accepted as a risk; accepting a security risk in an open PR is the owner's decision, so it is carried into the return contract as unaddressed and into the plan as the first action
